# Block-FP8 linear tuning winners per capacity (offline key reconstruction)

Generated by `claude_tuning_key_recon.py` from the retained tuning receipt
`receipts/moe-control-tuning.json` (a copy of the B12X selection cache written
by the 2026-09-25 MoE-control boot; same identity as the combined boots: same
model, TP4, DSpark, GB10 48 SMs). Keys are rebuilt exactly as
`b12x.preparation.session._choice_key` builds them at pin a7d7d29b, so a match
proves both the shape and the winner. 114 of the receipt's records matched.

Why it matters: the DS4.1 block-32 FP8 linears (`B12xFP8LinearMethod`,
`dsv41_block32_linear`) pick a plan by `bisect_left` over
`_execution_capacities()` = graph sizes up to 32, the CED window multiples
128/256/384/512, and the 8192 chunk. Prompts of 257 to 384 rows therefore run
every such linear on the 384-row plan, 385 to 512 on the 512-row plan, and
129 to 256 on the 256-row plan. The measured repeatability band is exactly
257 to 384.

The tuner ranks candidates by timing only (`preparation/_measurement.py` has
no reference comparison), so a fast but numerically unsafe tactic can win.

Distinctive property of the 384-row winners below: every k=5120 shape and the
Engram projection use `swap_ab=True` with `tile_n=64`. Under swap the public
tiling is unchanged (rows by tile_m, output columns by tile_n) but the weights
become the MMA A operand, so the MMA M extent over the weight rows is tile_n=64,
and the activations become the MMA B operand with extent tile_m. No other plan
in this workload combines swap, MMA-M=64 and more than one activation row
tile: the 8-row decode plan for 5120->1792 is (64,64, swap) with a single row
tile and is exact in every decode run; the stable swapped plans (256-row Engram,
512-row 5120->512) are (64,128, swap), MMA-M=128.

## Kernel inspection status (read-only, pinned B12X a7d7d29b, DSL helpers 4.5.2)

Code that is specific to the variable class (swap_ab with tile_n=64, so
`sfb_tiles_per_block == 2`, persistent multi-tile loop) versus the stable
swapped class (tile_n=128, `sfb_tiles_per_block == 1`), in `b12x/_lib/dense_gemm.py`:

- consumer sub-tile views of the weight scale block used in the MMA-A role:
  lines 2352 to 2375 (`sfb_tile_offset = tile_coord[1] % 2`, `local_tile(sSFB,
  (tile_n, tile_k))`, `_partition_fragment_SFA(sSFB_tile)`);
- producer block coordinate: line 3553 (`tile_coord[1] // 2`);
- MMA operand roles and copy atoms under swap: lines 2149 to 2180, 2283 to 2345;
- swapped direct-store epilogue without smem staging: lines 2862 to 2900;
- MMA tile, atom shape and warp count are keyed on the swapped MMA tile
  (lines 877 to 885, 958 to 987); scale k-reuse is forced off under swap (8224).

Every one of these has a role-symmetric twin on the unswapped path
(`sfa_tiles_per_block == 2` for tile_m=64, exercised by the stable 256-row
5120->1792 plan), and the CUTLASS DSL helpers `thrfrg_SFA/SFB`,
`partition_fragment_SFA/SFB` and `get_layoutSFA_TV/SFB_TV` (4.5.2 copy in the
local uv cache) differ only in which thread coordinate they use. No wrong
index or unsynchronised access was found by inspection. The image pins DSL
4.6.x, which was not readable locally. Attribution therefore rests on the
isolated operator matrix (same plan and tuning cache, fixed inputs, bitwise
repeats, config overrides), not on this inspection.

## Status after the isolated tactic matrix and the combined-image cache (2026-09-25)

Retired: the "384-row plan tactic" hypothesis.

- `receipts/block32-tactic-full.log` and `block32-tactic-poison.log`
  (`probe_block32_tactic.py`): three shapes, six row counts, four configs
  including the real 384-row winners, capacity 512 and the tile_n=128
  alternative, 64 repeats each, scratch poisoned with 0/127/255 between
  repeats. Every case bitwise exact, no changed rows.
- `receipts/combined-tuning.json` (sha256 5faeef64…) is the cache of the boot
  in which the 257 to 384 band was measured. Decoded with
  `claude_tuning_key_recon.py` it has the same identity as the MoE-control
  cache but 91 of 114 matched records differ. In the combined cache the
  384-row winners are: 1280->4096 (128,64) unswapped, identical to its 256-row
  plan; 1280->8192 (128,64); 5120->512 (64,64, swap), identical to its
  256-row plan; 5120->1152 (128,64); 5120->1792 (128,64), identical to its
  512-row plan; 6144->25600 (64,128, swap). No tactic property separates the
  384-row plans from both stable neighbours. The winner table earlier in this
  note describes the MoE-control boot only.
- Consequence for reasoning about cross-boot numerics: the tuner picks by
  measured timing with no numeric check, and the winners change between boots
  of the same image and cache identity. Bitwise agreement is therefore only
  meaningful within one boot (one selection cache). Retain each boot's cache
  with its receipts.

What the 384-row bucket still changes in serving: the block32 scratch sizes
(activation MXFP8 rows and scale blocks) carved from the single shared
`WorkspaceManager` buffer, and therefore the offsets that every later
`get_simultaneous` caller in the same forward receives. This cannot be
reproduced by an isolated single-op probe.

## Open lead: Engram disk lookups (host writes read by a side-stream kernel)

Facts from the pinned sources (vLLM 1794dcf, B12X a7d7d29b):

- The launcher sets `--engram-config {"table_memory":"disk", ...}` and
  `VLLM_DS41_ENGRAM_OVERLAP` defaults on. `prepare_disk_engram` (nvidia/model.py
  522) stages one lookup binding per Engram layer and submits
  `engram_native.run_lookups` to a worker thread that runs on a private CUDA
  stream (`_start_engram_job`, 505). The forward waits per Engram layer with
  the `_wait_engram_rows` spin on an epoch flag (engram.py 900).
- `run_lookups` (b12x/sequence/engram/_impl.py 112) stages ids for every
  table, then reads rows for tables 2..n in a three-thread pool while the job
  thread reads table 1, then launches `lookup_op` per table on the side stream.
- The row store is `cudaHostAlloc(Mapped | WriteCombined)` host memory
  (disk_table.py 80). The io_uring completion handler `ple_scatter`
  (b12x/loader/_ple_reader.c 42) copies each fragment into that store with a
  plain `memcpy`. Neither the C reader nor the Python transaction issues a
  store fence or a device-visibility barrier before the GPU kernel is launched
  from another thread; the only ordering is the thread join and the CUDA
  launch. On aarch64 (GB10) write-combined mapped memory is non-cacheable and
  ordering of CPU writes against a device reader needs a full-system barrier;
  x86 gets this implicitly from the launch path.
- The workspace and epoch bookkeeping are stream ordered and look sound; the
  hazard is confined to host-write visibility of the last fragments written
  before each `lookup_op`.

Why it fits the symptoms: prefill only; a small fraction of rows stale gives
a moderate logit error; exposure depends on timing (io_uring wave count is
proportional to tokens times 24, page-cache warmth changes it, which matches
256 settling after two requests); isolated GEMM and NCCL probes never touch
this path. It does not by itself explain the exact 257 and 385 edges; the
384-row bucket changes workspace offsets and timing in the same forward, so
the edges may be an interaction rather than a gate.

Prediction for the attention-boundary trace: the first divergent record is
the attention input of the first Engram layer (or of the layer right after
it), with all earlier layers exact. If instead divergence starts at a
non-Engram layer, this lead is wrong.

Decisive isolated reproducer (no model): bind one Engram disk table as serving
does, take 300 tokens of real hash ids, run the threaded `run_lookups` pattern
64 times comparing `staged_rows[:300]` bitwise, then the synchronous
`run_lookup`, then with the page cache dropped between repeats. A next-boot
arm that removes the host-write path entirely is `B12X_DISK_BACKEND=gds`;
`VLLM_DS41_ENGRAM_OVERLAP=0` only narrows the window. Neither is in the
launch contract whitelist today.

## Trace result and the layer-2 FFN segment (2026-09-25, later)

Trace (`receipts/attention-trace-requests/*-256-analysis.json`): first
divergence at the attention input of layer 3, all attention inputs and
outputs through layer 2 exact on all four ranks. Layer 3 is not an Engram
layer (Engram layers are 1 and 14), so the Engram host-write lead above does
not explain this divergence; it stays open only for lengths not yet traced.
The segment is: mHC post_pre(2) -> FFN(2) -> mHC pre(3).

Source facts for that segment (vLLM 1794dcf):

- Every layer's FFN is a MoE (`num_moe_layers = num_hidden_layers`). Routed
  experts run the B12X dynamic compact M16 split-materialized path with the
  deterministic phase 2 and the ordered top-k sum declared by the MoE repair.
  That path has never been bitwise-tested in isolation with real routing; only
  its declaration was tested.
- Shared experts (when `n_shared_experts` is set) run on the process-wide aux
  stream only when rows <= `VLLM_SHARED_EXPERTS_STREAM_TOKEN_THRESHOLD` (256,
  runner/shared_experts.py 121-141); above that they run sequentially before
  the routed experts. Workspace for routed and shared is leased disjointly in
  one `get_simultaneous` call (modular_kernel.py 1198-1207); events order the
  aux stream both ways and the main stream waits before the combine
  (`result = shared_output + fused_output`, moe_runner.py 826, then one
  all-reduce). No aliasing or ordering defect found by inspection. The gate at
  256 coincides with this boot's 256 first-token variation but not with the
  earlier 257 to 384 band, which ran the sequential path.
- mHC pre and post_pre lease scratch from the shared workspace at offset 0 on
  the main stream and contain no atomics.

Open kernel-level question in the routed path: `dynamic.py` uses atomic task
claiming and arrival counters (lines 2334, 3570 to 3621, 4008). The
deterministic branch at 3621 was not fully read; whether any partial sums are
merged in arrival order under `deterministic_output=1` is unverified.

## Shared-expert down projection, K=576 (omitted from the first key sweep)

`claude_tuning_key_recon.py` KS did not include 576 (2304 / TP4), so the
shared-expert down projection 576->5120 was missing from the tables above.
With 576 added, `receipts/trace-tuning-before-ops.json` matches 133 records
and shows for 576->5120: m256 (64,64,k128) unswapped, m384 and m512
(128,64,k128) unswapped, m128 (128,128,k64), m8192 (64,128,k128) swapped, all
split 1, unroll on except m8192.

K=576 is the only DS4.1 block-32 shape whose K is not a multiple of the 128
K tile; it is padded to 640 (five K tiles, twenty 32-wide scale groups).
Inspection of the padding path at pin a7d7d29b:

- Weights: `pack_weight` zero-fills columns 576..639 and pads the scale
  tiles with the neutral exponent (127, or 1.0 for the e8m0 dtype), so padded
  weight columns are exactly zero with a defined scale
  (`gemm/_shared/block_fp8.py` 427-455).
- Activations: the MXFP8 quantizer iterates all twenty physical groups per
  row, zero-fills elements at or beyond the source K, forces the amax to zero
  for whole padded groups and writes both the values and a defined scale
  byte for them (`_lib/quant/mxfp8_rows.py` 118-161, 177-246, 250-271). The
  row-M padding is left unspecified by design (scratch bind comment), which
  only affects rows never stored.
- Kernel: K tiles come from the physical extent (five tiles); the per-K-tile
  loop uses an unroll pragma with no manual remainder path
  (`_lib/dense_gemm.py` 2533-2538); scale blocks divide exactly (kb = 5).

No padding defect and no nondeterministic construct found for this shape. It
was not part of the isolated tactic matrix; adding 576->5120 at rows 256
(64,64) and 300/384 (128,64) with the scratch-poison variant would close it
empirically. The next trace's routed/shared/mHC split covers it in situ.

## Prepared, not run: `claude_probe_routed_moe.py` (contingent routed-MoE reproducer)

Isolated bitwise repeatability of the B12X routed MoE at DS4.1 geometry
(E=384, H=5120, I=576 per rank, top-6, w4a8_mx, W31 layout, silu, bf16 io).
It mirrors the vLLM B12X MoE method at pin 1794dcf: `plan_weights` with
`PackedSource(fp4_e8m0_k32, w31)` and `ActivationSpec(A8, silu, bf16)`,
`prepare_weights` on synthetic uint8 MXFP4 nibbles with uint8 e8m0 block
scales (the serving parameter dtypes) and unit global/activation scales,
`plan_execution` with the serving `ExecutionCapacity` (max 8192, top-6,
warmup counts 1..8, 12..32 step 4, 8192, route_num_experts=0),
`RoutingSpec(apply_router_weight_on_input=False)` and the serving invocation
key, a `PreparationSession(autotune=False)` whose namespace is taken from the
retained receipt identity, then `bind`/`run` on the composite plan.

Fail-closed gates: serving switches present in the environment; live
selection-cache identity equal to the receipt identity; every variant's
selection source "cached" (frozen choices, no measurement, no default); the
capacity variant used at 256 and 300 rows resolves to the dynamic backend;
finite, non-degenerate outputs; 64 bitwise-equal repeats. Options: scratch
poison between repeats, and an unrelated bf16 matmul on a second stream during
each run to mimic the shared-expert overlap window.

It must run inside the combined or trace image with the launcher environment
and the serving JIT cache at /cache, so the session finds the same preparation
cache and compiled programs the server used. It does not compare against a
reference implementation; a bitwise-repeatable but wrong kernel would pass.

## Engram layer 1: single-rank divergence before the collective (ops trace, 129 and 160 rows)

Evidence (`receipts/ops-small-trace-requests/*analysis.json`): on one rank
(rusty at 160 rows, dusty at 129) the first changed tensor is the residual
entering mHC pre(1), i.e. the output of Engram(1); the preceding mHC post(1)
output and MoE(0) outputs are exact on every rank; the other ranks first
differ at attention(1) OUTPUT, after the TP all-reduce. So the Engram(1)
output differs on exactly one rank before any collective propagates it.

What that excludes and what it leaves (pinned sources):

- The all-reduce input (`staged_rows[:M]`) differing on one rank is excluded
  for these cases: the reduced sum would then differ on all ranks equally.
  This also disfavors the Engram lookup and host-write path for the traced
  cases (it stays possible for other lengths).
- A differing all-reduce input is not needed for a single-rank difference if
  the collective itself delivers different bytes to one rank. At 129 and 160
  rows the Engram all-reduce is 1.59 MB and 1.97 MB of bf16, under the 2 MiB
  RoCE cap (`roce_oneshot.should_allreduce`: contiguous, 16-byte multiple,
  nbytes <= max_size), so it runs the B12X one-shot, which reduces locally on
  every rank from per-peer slots after per-(peer, HCA) acquire spins. 192 rows
  (2.36 MB) is NCCL; 192 also varied but was not traced at this granularity.
- Rank-local kernels after the collective: the Engram projection `wkv`
  (block-32 6144->25600, 256-row plan, trace-boot tactic (64,128) swapped,
  unroll on) and `hyperconnection_engram_mix` (custom op, no atomics in the
  package). The isolated GEMM matrix covered rows 64, 65 and 257 to 384 at
  capacities 384 and 512 for this shape, not rows 129 or 160 at capacity 256.
- The wait kernel's `failed` flag (epochs[2]) is written on timeout and never
  read anywhere; it must be captured by any Engram trace.
- The DS4.1 NVIDIA model class carries no `support_torch_compile` decorator,
  so `Engram.forward` and everything it calls run as eager Python in prefill.
  Python-level wrappers there take effect without touching custom ops.

Minimal in-process Engram trace (three wrappers, about 20 MB at 160 rows):
`Engram.forward` (clone hidden_states, hash_ids, `staged_rows[:M]`, and
epochs ready/expected/failed before and after; clone the returned out),
`CudaCommunicator.all_reduce` filtered to inputs of shape (M, 6144) (clone
input and output), and `block_fp8_linear.run` filtered to in_features 6144
(clone source and output). Decision table per rank and run: staged_rows
differ -> lookup/wait path; all-reduce output differs on one rank with equal
inputs -> collective; kv differs with equal rows -> wkv GEMM; out differs with
equal kv -> engram mix. Cross-rank: the all-reduce output must be bitwise
identical on all four ranks in every run.

Complementary arms: (a) isolated GEMM probe extension for 6144->25600 at
capacity 256, tactic (64,128) swapped, rows 129/160/192, with poison; (b)
next-boot `VLLM_ENABLE_ROCE_ALLREDUCE=0` (or a max size below 1.5 MB) so the
Engram all-reduce at these sizes takes NCCL; exact 129/160 results would
implicate the one-shot path. Neither is in the launch contract whitelist.

## Engram projection attributed at 192 rows (toby); `failed` flag caveat

Trace with the extended helper (2ada7f43): at 129, 160 and 192 rows the
Engram all-reduce output is bitwise identical on all four ranks in every run
(`summarize_engram.py`). On toby at 192 rows the first repeat's projection
output (`projected_kv`, block-32 6144->25600, 256-row plan, this boot's tactic
(64,128) swapped, unroll on) differs from the other three ranks and from
toby's next two repeats in 496 bf16 elements (max 0.0387); the projection
input and the mix's other inputs are exact; the mix input `arg1` carries the
identical 496 differences. The later two repeats match the other ranks. So
the projection GEMM on one rank is the localized site for 192. The differing
repeat was the first captured request, not the first execution of that
shape in the boot: six cold 192-row requests preceded the trace in the same
boot (`receipts/combined-prefetch-off-20260925T043946Z/repeatability/`)
and all varied. This boot runs the prefetch-off arm
(`VLLM_DS41_L2_PREFETCH=0`), so the L2 prefetcher is not a factor here. The 160-row early window is exact while
final logprobs still vary, so at least one later site exists.

Caveat: the lookup wait's `failed` word (epochs[2]) reads 1 on all ranks in
every captured request. It is set on any timeout and never reset or read, so
it is sticky from some earlier boot phase (warmup or a slow first read) and
cannot exonerate or implicate the lookup wait for any specific request. The
per-request evidence is only that `local_rows` were exact run to run and the
reduced rows identical across ranks in the traced runs.

## Production-only fault examination (2026-09-25, after the quantized traces)

Read-only, pinned sources (B12X a7d7d29b, vLLM 1794dcf1). No hosts touched.

### New evidence incorporated (user, quantized-engram-trace-requests)

- At 129 rows two ranks fault (toby 962 elements, maxabs 0.504; rusty 512,
  maxabs 0.0781). Not a single-GPU problem.
- Within each rank the source, quantized values, compact scales and the whole
  physical MMA scale plane are bitwise identical across repeats, at identical
  addresses; logical scales match compact on all ranks. The GEMM executes with
  identical operands at identical addresses and returns different bytes. That
  is an execution-time fault, not a stale-input or lifetime fault.
- Geometry, both runs: activation rows 96..127 (192-row run: 96..111), i.e.
  always the second half of activation row tile 1 (tile_m 64 -> rows 64..127,
  local rows 32..63 = MMA N-warp 1 under swap). Columns are single M-warp
  fragments in different CTAs (129: N-tile 0 M-warp 0, N-tile 2 M-warp 3; 192:
  N-tile 5 M-warp 1). Rows 96..127 are also the last quarter of the shared
  128-row scale atom (`sfb_tiles_per_block` = 2, row tiles 0 and 1 share one
  atom) and the last half of the A operand stage. The fault therefore sits at
  the tail of a stage, read by one warp at a time.

### Candidates examined and cleared

- Stream placement: only the disk lookup runs on the side stream
  (`nvidia/model.py` `_start_engram_job`). The projection `self.wkv(rows)` runs
  on the main stream inside `Engram.forward` after `_wait_engram_rows` and the
  all-reduce (`common/engram.py` 900..903).
- Arena aliasing: `_block32_linear` carves x_q scratch at arena offset 0
  (`b12x_layers.py` 106..118). The side-stream lookup writes only the
  persistent `staged_rows` buffer and mapped host memory (`b12x/sequence/engram`
  `_impl.py` run_lookup, `_disk.py`); nothing on the side stream uses the
  workspace arena. The all-reduce output is `torch.empty_like(inp)`
  (`roce_oneshot.py` 559), not arena memory. Quantized traces confirm x_q is
  unchanged anyway.
- Scale handoff for this tactic: `_preparation.py` `_run` passes
  `x_q.scale_mma` and `weight.scale_mma` into the TMA stage (tx_count includes
  SFA and SFB bytes, `dense_gemm.py` 1845..1849). The per-thread global scale
  reads (`_accumulate_block_fp8_stage`) belong to the 128-block `block_fp8`
  flag, which is off here; `direct_sfa_prefix`/`direct_sfb_representative`
  apply only to (16|32,64,128) tiles. No mid-kernel global reads of mutable
  memory on this path.
- Pipeline ordering: each MMA warp waits on the stage's full barrier itself;
  the empty barrier needs all eight warps; the hoisted-tail release
  (2762..2764) precedes the final MMA but all fragments and the whole-stage SF
  copy were already in registers. Swap epilogue is direct scalar stores
  (2862..2890), so no smem staging or TMA store is involved. No PDL in this
  kernel. TMA descriptors are rebuilt per call and passed as launch params.
- Compile environment: `_compile_environment_key` (compiler.py 1605..1641)
  keys the disk cache on CUTE_DSL_ARCH, CUDA_HOME and every B12X_/CUTE_/CUTLASS_
  variable not in the operational list. The probe container
  (`run_block32_engram.py`) lacks nine of the serving launcher's keyed vars
  (CUTE_DSL_ARCH, CUDA_HOME, B12X_MOE_FORCE_A8, B12X_MLA_SM120_UNIFIED,
  B12X_W4A16_TC_DECODE, B12X_ROCE_TRAFFIC_CLASS, B12X_*_COMPILE_WORKERS), so it
  compiled its own cache entry rather than loading serving's program. The DSL
  auto-detects sm_121a when CUTE_DSL_ARCH is unset (cutlass env_manager
  `detect_gpu_arch`), so the target is the same, but binary identity between
  probe and serving is unverified. The probe log carries no compile-start or
  cache-hit lines, so which happened is unknown.

### Remaining hypotheses (ranked)

1. Stage-fill completion versus consumer read of the stage tail under memory
   contention. The only per-warp, tail-localised, timing-dependent mechanism in
   this kernel is a consumer reading operand or scale bytes of a stage before
   they landed. That requires the full barrier's transaction count to be short
   of the bytes actually issued for this configuration. Static check not
   completed: the SF smem stage comes from `sm120_make_smem_layout_sfb`
   (tile-based) while the global SF tile is the 128-row atom
   (`sfb_tile_shape_nk` = (128, tile_k)); `tma_copy_bytes` uses the smem
   layout's byte size. Whether the TMA box for the swapped (64,128,128) tile
   covers the same bytes it is counted for needs a one-line dump of
   `tma_copy_bytes` and the four TMA box shapes in the probe container.
2. Concurrency exposure only: the isolated probe ran idle. In serving the side
   stream may still be running lookup kernels and mapped-host reads during the
   projection whenever `_wait_engram_rows` timed out (`failed` is sticky, so
   per-request coincidence is unknown), and the ordinary per-step overlap with
   attention/MoE of layer 0 differs from the probe in any case.
3. Program identity (see above).

### Review of the prepared `_replay_engram_dense` delta (helper sha e1ce59ff…)

- Mirrors `_BlockFP8ExecutionState._run` exactly: same views, same
  `scale_mma` operands, same `split_k_workspace`, guarded to split_k 1. Runs on
  the main stream immediately after the serving launch, so it sees the same
  x_q scratch before the mix touches anything. Serving output untouched.
- Byte bound: weight prefix 1024*6144 plus scale plane 25600*6144/32 =
  25600*192 plus replay M*25600*2. Correct.
- `k` is taken from `binding.source.shape`; equal to `_physical_mxfp8_k` only
  because 6144 needs no padding. Fine for this shape.
- The replay is one more sample a few microseconds later, not a control. It
  discriminates transient (replay agrees with the other ranks while the first
  launch differs) from deterministic (replay reproduces the wrong bytes), which
  is the right next question given identical operands. Add a second replay
  after `torch.cuda.synchronize()` so one sample runs with no co-resident
  work; if the immediate replay ever faults and the synchronized one never
  does, the fault is co-scheduling exposed.
- Also record `binding.expected_m`, the kernel `m`, and a program identity
  (cache key or cubin digest) per row; and zero `failed` (epochs[2]) at the
  start of each traced forward so the timeout coincidence becomes visible.

### Suggested order

1. Deploy `dense_replay=true` with the synchronized second replay and the
   `failed` reset (in-process, no image change).
2. Next boot: `VLLM_DS41_ENGRAM_OVERLAP=0` A/B on the same image and cache.
3. Probe container re-run with the full serving environment from
   `launch_contract.render` and a concurrent side-stream load (mapped-host
   reads plus a large copy), plus the tx_count/box dump.

Fix candidates only after attribution: if 1 or 3 implicate stage completion,
the fix is in the kernel's transaction accounting or an explicit wait before
the tail read; if concurrency alone is implicated, serialise the lookup as the
mitigation and treat the kernel hazard as open; if the program differs, the
probe must be rerun before any kernel claim.

### Follow-up (2026-09-25, later): transaction count closed, chunk geometry, fitter

- Transaction count versus TMA box: closed by construction, not by number.
  `_make_tma_atoms_and_tensors` builds every TMA atom from the same staged
  smem layout (stage slice) and tile shape that `tma_copy_bytes` measures
  (`cute.size_in_bytes` of the same slices). The scale atoms are 128 rows x
  4 groups = 512 B per operand per stage (`sfa_tile_shape_mk` =
  `(max(128, tile_m), tile_k)`), and the sm120 SF smem layout cosize is 512 B
  (basic chunk (32,4):(16,4), four K groups, one 128-row block). Under swap the
  activation scale atom is sSFA and the 64-row half is selected by
  `tile_coord_mnl[0] % sfa_tiles_per_block` (2377..2381), which is the row
  tile parity. No undercount path exists for this tactic.
- Retraction: rows 96..127 are not a contiguous tail of the stage. In the
  (32,4):(16,4) chunk, row r sits at byte (r % 32) * 16 + (r // 32) * 4 + group,
  so rows 96..127 are the last 4 bytes of every 16-byte unit of the activation
  scale stage, and for the value operand they are the second half of the
  64-row A stage (rows 32..63 of row tile 1). The observable is "row block 3
  of the scale chunk, N-warp 1 of row tile 1", not a stage tail.
- Stage depth for (64,128,128): about 3 (A 8 KiB + B 16 KiB + SF 1 KiB per
  stage, one 16 KiB epilogue tile, sm_120 capacity), assuming occupancy 1.
  Stale-stage hypotheses therefore have shift 1..3 k tiles.
- Lifetime: agreed, post-call snapshots cannot exclude a transient overwrite
  during execution. The source audit found no writer, and the same-address,
  same-bytes evidence makes it unlikely, but it is not excluded.
- Helper additions (sha 0b769e53…) reviewed: `program_keys(state.dense.gemm)`
  is valid at the pin (`_get_compiled_dense_gemm` returns an
  `attach_programs` wrapped callable); the `failed` reset precedes the wait
  kernel on the main stream and retains the prior value; the synchronized
  replay drains the side stream too, which is intended; bound arithmetic for
  two replays is correct. Nothing unsafe found. One caveat: `torch.cuda.synchronize`
  inside the forward changes the co-scheduling of every later operator in the
  traced request, so compare only Engram-projection rows across control arms.
- Concrete testable step added: `claude_fit_error_structure.py` (sha
  1fa28f7c…) fits the captured faulty block against exact per-32-group partial
  products in float64: one group scaled by 2^j - 1 (a wrong e8m0 scale or a
  doubled/missing group), a k tile whose activation or weight values (with or
  without scales) came from a stale stage 1..3 tiles back, scales-only stale,
  and row substitution. Smoke-tested with injected faults (residual 0.006 and
  0.008 against a rounding floor of 0.03..0.05; wrong hypotheses stay above
  0.7). Runs offline on the quantized-trace captures: x_q values and compact
  scale rows, the weight prefix and weight scales, the faulty and a good
  output. The earlier missing-K-block fit tested only one of these shapes.

### Fitter v2 for trace dictionaries, and the consumer-release review (2026-09-25, later)

`claude_fit_error_structure.py` (sha bc71e123…), CPU only, one Torch thread:

- `trace` mode reads a helper trace (`{'operators': [...]}`), takes the single
  `engram_projection` row, decodes the physical MXFP8 scale planes with the
  quantizer's mapping (row32 = r % 32, row4 = (r // 32) % 4, tile_m = r // 128,
  k4 = c % 4, tile_k = c // 4; logical view = physical permuted (3,4,1,5,2,0)),
  cross-checks the activation compact scales against the physical plane, and
  refuses to fit if they differ. Observed and reference keys are chosen on the
  command line (`projected_kv`, `dense_replay`, `dense_replay_synchronized`).
  With `--reference-trace`, every operand (values, compact and physical
  activation scales, weight prefix, weight scale plane) must be bitwise equal
  or the comparison is refused.
- One CTA block only: `--list-blocks` prints faulty (row_tile, n_tile) pairs
  under the swapped (64,128) tactic parsed from `dense_config`; `--block r,n`
  fits that block alone. Blocks outside the 1024-row weight prefix are refused.
- Exponent labelling fixed: j in -12..12 without 0 gives factor 2^j - 1
  (j = 1 doubled, j = -1 halved); "missing group" is its own factor -1 entry.
- Tests (synthetic trace in the helper's format, M 129, K 6144, three blocks):
  stale activation values+scales tile 5 from tile 3 ranks first at 0.014
  (floor 0.07); stale activation scales only tile 7 from 6 ranks first at
  0.014; group 20 halved ranks first at 0.081 (floor 0.67 on that small
  region). Cross-trace identity accepted for identical operands and refused
  for a one-byte difference.
- Printed row and column indices inside a block are block-local.

Consumer-release ordering in the swapped TMA path (pinned kernel, DSL pipeline
helpers read from the local cutlass checkout at 4.4 dev; b12x pins
nvidia-cutlass-dsl 4.6.2, so the election code should be confirmed inside the
probe container with `inspect.getsource(PipelineTmaAsync.consumer_release)`,
which needs no GPU):

- `PipelineTmaAsync.consumer_release` arrives only from the signalling lane
  (`tidx % 32 < cluster_size`, i.e. lane 0 with cluster (1,1,1)), so eight
  arrivals per stage match `CooperativeGroup(Agent.Thread, num_mma_warps)`
  (1881..1883). The cpasync path uses `PipelineAsync`, whose release arrives
  from every thread, and there the kernel sizes the group by threads
  (1886..1893). Both are consistent; no miscount.
- In the main loop the release at the last k block (2573) comes after the
  fragments of that block were loaded in the previous k block and after the
  whole-stage scale copy at acquisition; the next stage is then waited before
  any read of it. In the hoisted tail (2762..2764) nothing reads shared memory
  after the release. `consumer_try_wait` tokens carry acquire semantics, so the
  peek-then-skip path is sound.
- The producer's DMA warp waits the empty barrier, re-arms the transaction
  count from an elected lane, and issues TMA into the released stage. That is
  the CUTLASS TMA pipeline contract; no proxy fence is required for this
  generic-read then async-write reuse.
- Remaining dependence, not a defect: the per-lane 8-bit scale loads
  (`CopyUniversalOp`) and the lane-0 release rely on warp convergence and the
  warp-level fence behaviour of `mbarrier.arrive`, which CUTLASS relies on
  everywhere. No source-level release ordering hazard was found in the swapped
  dense path.

### Replay receipts and `probe_engram_dense_replay.py` review (2026-09-25, later)

Evidence (user's replay-engram-trace-requests receipts, per-rank CPU
comparisons): the fault reproduces inside `state.dense.run` after a full device
synchronize, in the serving process, with bitwise identical operands, on all
four ranks (dusty 4/4 synchronized replays at 129 rows differ from the original
output; every rank has at least one differing immediate or synchronized
replay). All traced `failed` epochs were 0 after the reset. Operands, weight
prefix and weight scales are stable across requests. This removes
concurrency, the quantizer, the lookup, and the wait timeout as necessary
conditions. What remains is the launch itself: the compiled program in that
process, its launch parameters, and process-level device state. The earlier
isolated probe (fresh compile, different cache key, 64/64 exact at 129, 160 and
192 rows) is the only observation on the other side of that line.

Correction to the earlier release-ordering paragraph: `mbarrier.arrive`
carries `.release` at `.cta` scope by default (PTX 8.0+), and release
semantics order only the executing thread's own prior memory operations. No
PTX rule makes one lane's arrive a warp-wide fence. The elected lane-0 arrive
therefore does not formally order the other 31 lanes' earlier per-lane scale
byte loads or their ldmatrix participation. A `bar.warp.sync` between the last
shared-memory read of a stage and the elected arrive would make the pattern
formally correct. This is a hardening candidate, not an attributed cause: the
practical window (a lane's in-flight shared load overtaken by a TMA refill that
needs the producer to observe the arrive, wait, issue, and complete a global
fetch) is very narrow, and the fault reproduces on an idle device.

`probe_engram_dense_replay.py` (sha 7ab87de9…), against the pin:

- API surface matches: `Caps(device, max_tokens, in_features, out_features,
  block_size)` with bf16 defaults, `plan(caps, override=config)`,
  `Plan.scratch_specs()` before preparation (memory_requirements path),
  `pack_weight(weight, scale, block_size=(32, 32))` keeps E4M3 values verbatim
  for UE8M0 scales so the prefix comparison is valid, `bind(plan, scratch=...,
  source=..., packed_weight=..., output=...)`, `state.dense.run` called with
  the same views as `_BlockFP8ExecutionState._run` and as the serving replay.
  `PreparationSession(autotune=False)` with a pinned override resolves the
  selection from the override, so no tuning record is needed.
- Environment handling copies only the manifest's compiler inputs and unsets
  B12X_ variables absent from it. Unrelated operational variables such as
  `CUTE_DSL_CACHE_DIR` would trip the allow-list; harmless, fails loudly.
- Operand reproduction is complete: the captured physical scale plane is the
  full logical view, so padding rows are copied too; values are sized m as in
  serving; fresh output per repeat; synchronize before every launch; operands
  re-checked after the loop.
- Gap 1: the identity check proves identical compile inputs (the program key
  is derived from inputs), not an identical binary. If the probe container
  does not mount the serving compile cache, the program is recompiled under
  the same key. Set `B12X_PRINT_COMPILE_PROGRESS=1` (operational, not keyed)
  and require a `disk-cache-hit` line for the gemm program, or hash the cache
  entry for that key in both places and record the digests in the report.
- Gap 2: no contrast arm. Run a second process with a fresh compile cache
  directory and the earlier probe environment (which was exact). If the
  serving-program arm reproduces and the fresh arm does not, the binaries
  differ and a `cuobjdump -sass` diff of the two cache entries is the next
  step (offline). If both reproduce, the earlier exact result was the
  anomaly and the fault is in the kernel regardless of program. If neither
  reproduces, the remaining difference is process state, not the program.
- Gap 3: `same_as_serving` compares against the original serving output of
  the traced request, which may itself be a faulty sample. Add a consensus
  reference: the other ranks' `projected_kv` for the same request where they
  agree bitwise, or the request's synchronized replay when it matched the
  other ranks. Record equality against both.
- Minor: `raise SystemExit(1)` when any repeat differs is right; also report
  the (row_tile, n_tile) block set of each mismatch so the geometry can be
  compared with serving (rows 96..127 of row tile 1 under N-warp 1).

Process-level state that differs between serving and a fresh probe process
and is worth recording in the report, since it survives a device synchronize:
the loaded module and its cubin digest; `cudaLimitStackSize` (b12x
`program_cache.py` sets it); the CUDA graph capture history (the projection is
not captured, but pool state differs); allocator addresses of operands and
scratch (TMA descriptors encode them). None of these is a source claim.

### Exact-program isolated replay RED; ordering discriminator review (2026-09-25, later)

Evidence (user): `engram-dense-isolated-dusty.json`, idle dusty, exact serving
program key 68f673… and object sha 96f193… matched before and after, captured
q and scales, checkpoint weights, no server, lookup, collective or quantizer in
the loop: 1/64 wrong (repeat 1), the rest bitwise equal to the unanimous
four-rank reference sha 97399c9d…. The fault is therefore intrinsic to this
program's execution on these operands; serving raises its rate, isolation
lowers it. The first CPU fit on CTA (1,14) with verified checkpoint weights
found no warp-uniform explanation (best 0.528 against a floor of 0.128).

Mainloop ordering re-read for the swapped TMA path (2540..2762): at the last
k block the stage is released, the state advanced, the shared-memory views
re-sliced to the new index, the new stage waited, the last MMA emitted with the
current stage's scale registers, then the k-block-0 fragment copies and the
whole-stage scale copy for the new stage. All per-thread reads are ordered by
the wait; the only cross-lane point without a formal PTX ordering is the
elected lane-0 release.

Proposed discriminator (user): `sync_warp` immediately before both
`consumer_release` sites (2573, 2768), arithmetic and tactic unchanged.
Review: sound and cheap. Every lane of the MMA warps reaches both sites (the
guards are compile-time uniform), so no divergence deadlock; `bar.warp.sync`
orders the participating lanes' prior shared-memory reads before lane 0's
arrive, closing the formal gap. If the fault rate is unchanged over enough
repeats to resolve 1/64, that gap is excluded. A pass would implicate it but
would not yet name the mechanism.

Variants worth running on the fast reproducer, one change each, arithmetic
unchanged:

1. Every-lane release instead of the elected one: arrive from all 256 MMA
   threads with the empty barrier sized `num_mma_warps * 32` (the cpasync path
   already does this). Each lane then releases after its own loads with
   `.release` semantics, which is the formally sufficient form without adding
   a warp barrier. If variant 1 passes and the sync_warp variant also passes,
   the lane ordering is the mechanism; if only one passes, the difference
   narrows it further.
2. Always block on `consumer_wait` without the `consumer_try_wait` token
   (prologue and the stage transition), which removes the peek-then-skip path.
3. `large_m_unroll=False` for the same tile: changes instruction scheduling
   only. The 384-row winner already runs that way.
4. Pipeline depth forced to 2 (minimum or cap): if a stale-stage read is the
   mechanism, the error signature changes with the reuse distance, and the
   fitter's `--stages` should be set to match.
5. Repeat-1 focus: the wrong sample was the second launch after a cold first.
   Insert an idle gap before each launch and, separately, launch back to back
   without synchronize, to see whether the rate follows launch spacing.

Fitter extension (sha ce008e9c…): H6 prints a per-row table with each row's
best single stale 32-wide k block (shift 1..stages) and best scaled group.
Rows of one warp fragment that agree on the same block and shift indicate a
warp-uniform stale read; rows that disagree indicate lane-level (per-thread)
staleness, which the whole-warp models cannot express. Tested with a mixed
injection (rows 96..103 stale block 20, rows 104..111 stale block 21): the
per-row table recovers both while every whole-block hypothesis stays above
0.93. Re-run the CTA (1,14) capture through this table before choosing
between variants 1 and 2.

A fresh same-source compile as a contrast arm is still useful, but only with
both object hashes recorded; a pass or fail alone does not prove different
binaries.

### First-work-tile geometry, controls, late-release patch (2026-09-25, later)

Isolated arms on idle dusty (receipts `engram-dense-isolated-dusty-*.json`,
all object sha 96f193…): fresh same-source compile 24/256, serving cache
26/256 (50 ms gaps both), sync_warp before both releases 13/512, no-peek
1/256, unroll2 16/256. Every faulty launch differs from the unanimous
reference 97399c9d…. No ordering variant passes, and the same object fails
from both caches, so a stale cache object is excluded.

Scheduler mapping verified from the pinned host code and the DSL scheduler:
`PersistentTileSchedulerParams(num_ctas_mnl, (1,1,1), swizzle_size=1)` with
the default `raster_along_m=True` gives a column-major linear order, m
fastest: tile i -> (m = i % 3, n = i // 3). The persistent grid is
`min(600, max_active_clusters)` = 48 CTAs at occupancy 1, so CTA c's first
tile is (c % 3, c // 3) with n in 0..15, and tile m = 2 holds only row 128 so
its faults are masked. The observed set (m 0..1, n 0..15, all 50 baseline
faults) is exactly the first work tile of each CTA; by chance alone the
probability of 50 faults landing in 48 of 600 tiles is negligible. The unroll2
arm has one exception, tile (1,70) = linear 211 = CTA 19's fifth tile, so the
first-tile confinement is a property of the baseline programs, not a law.

First-tile-only code in the kernel (everything else is per tile): mbarrier
init and the block sync (1895..1912, 2228), `setmaxregister_increase(232)`
for the eight MMA warps (2278) and `setmaxregister_decrease(40)` for the DMA
warp (3522), descriptor prefetch by warp 0 (1784..1804), the initial
scheduler work tile, and the first use of each pipeline stage and phase. The
NVIDIA `blackwell_geforce/dense_gemm.py` example uses the same 232/40
reallocation at the same places, so the pattern is NVIDIA's own; that does
not make it correct for this program's register allocation.

Hypothesis that fits the geometry and the failed operand fits: leftover
accumulator registers. If, for the first tile only, some accumulator
registers of one warp are not zeroed (zeroing scheduled before
`setmaxnreg.inc`, whose newly granted registers have undefined contents, or
otherwise ineffective), the output is bf16(Y + G) with G whatever those
registers held: the previous CTA's final accumulators on that SM in
isolation, arbitrary other-kernel values in serving (higher rate). This is
per warp fragment, register-specific (same rows across faults), first tile
only, timing and codegen dependent, and independent of k structure. It is
unproven.

One-change controls, arithmetic unchanged, in order of discrimination:

1. Second `accumulators.fill(0.0)` immediately after the first
   `consumer_wait` of each tile (2447..2449). It cannot be scheduled above the
   wait. If the first-tile faults vanish, the accumulator initialization is
   the mechanism; if not, leftover registers are excluded.
2. Remove both `setmaxregister_*` calls (one flag). Tests the reallocation
   interplay as a whole, including the DMA warp's 40-register budget.
3. Column-slice runs: N = 2048 (n tiles 0..15 only, one tile per CTA) and a
   second slice of weight rows 2048..4095 as its own N = 2048 problem. Same
   rate on both means temporal (first tile); rate following rows 0..2047
   means data dependent.
4. Offline SASS on object 96f193…: locate `USETMAXREG` and check for writes
   to registers above the launch count before it, and where the accumulator
   zeroing sits. Also read the launch register count from the cubin header.

`dense-late-release.patch` review: it removes the hoisted-tail release at
2767..2769 and re-issues it after the whole final k tile's MMAs and the
block-FP8 accumulation. Every MMA thread still executes it once per tile, the
consumer state still advances once, and the producer's next acquire simply
waits longer; arithmetic unchanged, no deadlock. It only changes the LAST
stage's release of each tile, and the faults are in the first tile, where
that release precedes the second tile's prologue; a pass would say something
about tile-to-tile stage reuse, a fail excludes it. Lower priority than
controls 1 and 2.

All-lane release versus sync_warp: with `bar.warp.sync` before the elected
arrive, lane 0's release is ordered after every lane's prior reads through
the barrier, so the two are formally equivalent for the lane-ordering gap.
All-lane release adds no coverage beyond that, and both variants fail, so
the gap is excluded as the cause. Its only extra property is independence
from warp convergence assumptions.

Fitter (sha 634bb0fb…) H7: tests output = bf16(Y + G) with G the same
block-local fragment of every other work tile taken from the reference
output, plus doubled and lost accumulators. Injected leftover fault from
tile (0,5) fits at 0.003 against a floor of 0.011 while every other
hypothesis stays above 0.76. Run it on every first-mismatch capture; a hit
names the previous occupant tile, which in isolation should be a last tile
of the previous launch (c + 528 or c + 576 for CTA c). The probe should save
every mismatch, not only the first (about 6.6 MB each).

### Prologue re-examination, corrections, stronger discriminators (2026-09-25, later)

Status (user): late-release arm also fails; all DS4.1 servers stopped for
isolated diagnosis on dusty; GLM untouched. Post-wait accumulator
reinitialization and the register-reallocation control are being tested.

Corrections to my earlier claims:

- m tile 2 is not automatically masked. Row 128 is a valid row of that tile
  (local row 0). Its absence from the fault set is explained only if every
  fault sits at local rows 32..63 (N-warp 1), which the 129- and 192-row
  serving traces showed but the isolated receipts do not record (they carry
  tiles, not rows). Until the probe records rows and columns per fault, the
  m = 2 absence is an observation, not a masked case.
- The sync_warp result excludes the elected-release lane-ordering gap only
  conditionally: the `bar.warp.sync` must be present in the SASS before both
  arrives (the DSL lowers `sync_warp` to `nvvm.bar_warp_sync` with the full
  mask; ptxas will not delete it, but this should be confirmed on the object),
  and the exclusion covers that gap alone. The rate change (24/256 to 13/512)
  is a timing perturbation, consistent with a race elsewhere.

Prologue read again (1770..1810, 1881..1912, 1912..1946, 2220..2236, DSL
helpers): descriptor prefetch by warp 0 on kernel-parameter descriptors;
mbarrier init by warp 0 (every lane re-initializes the same barriers with the
same count, harmless), `mbarrier_init_fence`, block sync inside `create`, a
second block sync before the role split; register reallocation after that
sync, mirroring NVIDIA's geforce example line for line. No concrete defect
found by reading. The leftover-accumulator hypothesis is weakened by the
rate: a static leftover from the previous occupant would fault nearly every
first tile, whereas the observed rate is about 0.2 percent per CTA, which
looks like a race, not static contents. It stays on the list only because
nothing else explains the k-independent structure; H7 on real mismatches
decides it.

A duplicate `accumulators.fill(0.0)` can be eliminated as a redundant
constant store. A non-eliminable form: keep the first fill at 0.0 and make
the post-wait fill `alpha_value * 0.0` (alpha is loaded at runtime; strict FP
does not fold x * 0.0), so the second store is a distinct SSA value.

Stronger discriminators that do not modify the kernel:

1. Register and shared-memory preconditioning kernels launched immediately
   before each GEMM launch, two arms: poison (write a recognisable pattern,
   e.g. 0x7F800001 or 2^20, into as many registers per thread as a 288-thread
   CTA can hold, and into the full dynamic shared memory) and zero. Under an
   uninitialized-register or uninitialized-shared-memory mechanism the poison
   arm changes the fault values to the pattern and likely the rate; the zero
   arm lowers the rate. Under a race neither arm changes anything. A CUDA C++
   kernel with `__launch_bounds__(288, 1)` and `asm volatile` register writes
   is the reliable way; Triton cannot pin register usage.
2. Non-persistent launch: `single_work_tile_per_cta` (grid of 600, one tile
   per CTA), same tactic and arithmetic. Every tile becomes a first tile. A
   uniform spread over all 600 tiles at a similar per-CTA rate confirms a
   CTA-start mechanism; a vanishing fault implicates the persistent loop
   (stage and phase continuity between tiles); a fault still confined to the
   first 48 linear tiles implicates the data of those tiles (weight rows
   0..2047), not the timing.
3. NVIDIA's own `blackwell_geforce/dense_gemm.py` example at this shape and
   tile, many repeats, same operands. The same flake means toolchain, DSL or
   hardware level and a vendor report; a clean run means a b12x-specific
   modification, and the diff between the two kernels becomes the search
   space.
4. Per-fault forensics: record rows, columns and values of every mismatch;
   run the fitter's H7 on each. Matching another tile's fragment proves
   leftover registers; unrelated values exclude it.
5. SASS of object 96f193…: `USETMAXREG` placement, launch register count,
   any register above the launch count written before the increase, and the
   `WARPSYNC` presence for the sync_warp arm.

Uncertainty preserved: no mechanism is established. The evidence supports a
race or an initialization dependence confined to the CTA's first tile in the
baseline programs, with codegen-dependent rates, and one non-first-tile fault
in the unroll2 arm.

### SASS inspection of object 96f193 (2026-09-25, later)

Arms (user): reinit 56/512, no-register-reallocation 4/512, late-release
84/512; compute-sanitizer racecheck on the exact baseline, 8 calls, 0
hazards. Cubin extracted from the object (`receipts/dense-original.cubin`,
`dense-original-sass.txt`, sm_121a).

Facts from the object:

- Resource usage: 96 registers, 1024 B static shared, no local memory. No
  `USETMAXREG` anywhere: the source's `setmaxregister_*` calls are not
  present in the lowered program, so the register reallocation and the
  leftover-register hypothesis are moot for this object. Retracted.
- 3 stages: six barrier words initialized (three full at the barrier base,
  three empty at +0x18, counts 1 and 8), matching S = 3.
- Mainloop is the source structure: per stage, the wait (peek TRYWAIT, then a
  NANOSLEEP retry loop), LDSM operand fragments and 24 per-lane `LDS.U8`
  scale bytes spread across the stage's k blocks, QMMA.SF block-scaled MMAs,
  and the lane-0 predicated `SYNCS.ARRIVE.TRANS64.A1T0` on the empty barrier
  after the stage's last shared-memory load. No shared-memory load sits
  between an arrive and the next wait. Consistent with the source and with
  the failure of every ordering arm.
- Prologue (0x0200..0x03e0), warp 0 only: five descriptor prefetches, then
  `FENCE.VIEW.ASYNC.S`, then the three `SYNCS.EXCH.64` initializations of the
  full barriers, then `FENCE.VIEW.ASYNC.S`, then the three initializations of
  the empty barriers, then two `BAR.SYNC`. Both fences precede the
  initialization stores; no fence follows the last initialization before
  the block syncs. The DSL source order is init full, init empty,
  `fence.mbarrier_init.release.cluster`, block sync. Whether the fence was
  hoisted or lowered differently, the ordering the PTX ISA requires between
  `mbarrier.init` and the barrier's use by other warps' phase checks and by
  the TMA unit's transaction completions is not visibly established in this
  object. (Identification of `FENCE.VIEW.ASYNC.S` as the init fence is from
  the instruction stream; the PTX should be dumped to confirm the mapping.)

Candidate mechanism (unproven, testable): the barrier initialization is
performed by the SYNCS unit and is not ordered against another warp's
`SYNCS.PHASECHK` by `BAR.SYNC` alone; that is what the init fence provides.
If a consumer warp's very first TRYWAIT on the full barrier of stage 0
observes the word before the initialization lands, it sees the previous
occupant's final barrier state. If that state reads as "phase with parity 0
complete", the warp passes without waiting for the TMA, consumes stage 0's
leftover contents (the previous CTA's last k tile in that stage), and then
proceeds normally on properly initialized barriers. This predicts: first
tile only (only the first use of the barriers), one warp at a time,
rate set by a narrow window (about 0.2 percent per CTA here), higher in
serving where the leftover words come from other kernels, k-tile-0 error
whose operands are the previous occupant's data (so no same-tile stale fit),
sanitizer-blind, and insensitive to release ordering, peek removal, unroll,
late release and accumulator reinit except through timing.

Tests, in order of decisiveness:

1. Shared-memory poison kernel before every launch: write a fixed pattern
   over the full dynamic shared range (including the barrier words). Under
   the mechanism the previous-occupant state becomes the pattern: either the
   fault becomes deterministic (pattern parity reads as complete) or
   disappears (parity reads as incomplete), and faulty values derive from
   the pattern (0xFF bytes give NaN operands). Under any other mechanism the
   poison changes nothing. No kernel change.
2. Explicit init fence after the initializations: add
   `cute.arch.mbarrier_init_fence()` and `cute.arch.fence_proxy("async.shared",
   space="cta")` after `PipelineTmaAsync.create` and before `sync_threads`,
   then confirm in the new SASS that a FENCE follows the last `SYNCS.EXCH.64`.
   If the compiler hoists it again, use `defer_sync=True` and emit init,
   fence, sync explicitly, or wrap init and fence in one inline-asm block.
   Arithmetic unchanged.
3. Dump the PTX for the object and confirm the order of `mbarrier.init` and
   `fence.mbarrier_init.release.cluster`, and whether the fence exists at
   all for the non-cluster sm_121a launch.
4. Fitter H8 (sha 1975e6b8…): predicts the faulty fragment's error as
   P(previous tile m', n', k tile 45 + s) minus P(current k tile s), with rows
   beyond M zero. Verified on an injected fault (0.015 against a floor of
   0.066; wrong candidates above 0.70). Needs the weight rows of the
   candidate n' tiles: pass the full 25600-row weight and scale plane as
   `weight_prefix` and `weight_scale_mma` in a probe-generated trace.

Uncertainty: the mechanism rests on one reading of the object's fence
placement and on the PTX ISA requirement; the poison arm and the fence arm
decide it without further inference.

### Reinit arms, fence claim retracted, CS2R zeroing lead (2026-09-25, later)

Arms (user): runtime-derived zero (`alpha_value * 0.0`) after the initial
wait passes 512 + 2048; before the initial wait passes 512; baseline still
fails. Green object `receipts/dense-reinit-runtime.*`. Negative-zero arm
running.

Fence claim retracted. `fence.mbarrier_init` admits only `.release.cluster`,
and the ISA's CTA-scope sequence is init, `bar.sync`, use; the two block
syncs in the object satisfy that for this non-cluster launch. The two
`FENCE.VIEW.ASYNC.S` before the inits are most likely the DSL's proxy fences,
not a misplaced init fence. Not a defect. (Verified against the user's
citation of the ISA text; not fetched here.)

Green object review (sha of green cubin recorded by user): same 96
registers, same structure. Accumulator initialization is `FMUL R5 = RZ * R79`
(runtime zero) then 32 `MOV Rn, R5` ALU moves interleaved with the scale-byte
loads, at 31..75 instructions before each pair's first QMMA. Baseline zeroes
the same pairs with 16 `CS2R Rn, SRZ` at 31..64 instructions before first
use. Distances are comparable; the difference is the instruction class.

Control-bit decode (Volta-style fields from the second encoding word):
`S2R` and `S2UR` carry a write scoreboard (variable latency); `CS2R Rn, SRZ`
carries none, so ptxas treats it as fixed latency; the first QMMA reading a
zeroed pair as C waits only on the LDSM scoreboard. If on sm_121 a CS2R from
SRZ can complete late (it goes through the special-register read path, which
is busiest at kernel start when every warp issues S2R), the first QMMAs read
the pair's previous contents. This predicts the whole warp's fragments at
once (the 16 CS2R are a burst), first tile only (later tiles zero when that
path is idle), per-warp, rare, codegen-dependent rate through scheduling,
sanitizer-blind, and it is exactly what the MOV-based arms remove. It is a
hypothesis, not a proof; ptxas treats CS2R as fixed latency in every kernel,
so a real latency defect would be a toolchain or silicon issue for QMMA's C
read on this part.

Discriminators, faithful arithmetic:

1. `WarpField.ACCUMULATE = False` on the first k block of each tile and no
   fill at all (the DSL's block-scaled trait admits the field; D = A * B
   exactly). No CS2R exists; expected to pass. This is also the clean
   production form.
2. `ACCUMULATE = False` with the constant fill retained: CS2R is emitted
   again and now races the QMMA's write of D instead of its read of C. Under
   the CS2R-late mechanism the fault persists with a new signature, the first
   k block's contribution lost or zeroed; the fitter's new H9 tests "k tile 0
   or k block 0 missing or doubled". If this arm passes too, lateness of the
   zeroing is not the mechanism and the C read itself is.
3. Register poison before launch: under leftover contents the faulty values
   follow the pattern. And H7 on the existing mismatch captures needs only
   the reference output and can be run now; in isolation the previous
   occupant of a warp's accumulator registers is the previous launch's last
   tile on that SM.

Fitter sha 62d4c11b… adds H9; injected missing k block 0 fits at the
rounding floor.

### Grid results, passing-arm critique, misdirected-store hypothesis (2026-09-25, later)

Results (user): grid-all-tiles (600 CTAs, one tile each, byte-identical SASS
text) passes 512; grid 300 (two tiles per CTA) fails and the faults stay in
n 0..15, the first tiles of CTAs 0..47 only; N = 2048 slice (48 tiles, one
per CTA) passes 512; runtime-zero before wait 0/512, after wait 0/2560;
negative-zero constant fill fails 71/512; original 61/512. The ACCUMULATE
field is not admissible on the installed MXF8 trait: my earlier discriminator
1 and 2 are withdrawn. H8 on dusty 129-0 found no match.

Critique of the passing arms: in the green object the runtime zero is
`FMUL R5 = RZ * R79` at 0x06a0 with wait mask bit 5, the scoreboard of the
alpha `LDG` issued at 0x00a0. That wait sits before the consumers' first
TRYWAIT and executes once, so every runtime-zero arm delays the consumer
warps' start of the first tile by one cold global-load latency. The
baseline consumes alpha only in the epilogue and starts immediately; the
negative-zero arm has no such dependency and fails. The passes are therefore
consistent with a timing shift at the start of the first tile and do not
establish accumulator initialization as the mechanism.

Static check across the tile boundary (both objects, control-bit decode):
every epilogue STG's late-read registers are guarded by read scoreboards 0..3,
and each is waited before the next tile's prologue rewrites those registers
(baseline: SB1 at 0x0730, SB0 at 0x0780). No violation found. Stores without
a read scoreboard only have address registers rewritten later, which is
ptxas's normal model. The epilogue-to-prologue register reuse is not the
visible defect.

What the grid results constrain: the per-tile instruction stream is
identical and correct when each CTA has one tile. The fault needs a second
tile in the same CTA and appears only in the first tile of the first CTA on
each SM. The second tile of CTA c is (m, n + 16) under grid 48 and
(m, n + 100) under grid 300, same m, different n.

Blind spot in the probe: `out = torch.empty_like(...)` per repeat returns the
same block, so a region the kernel never writes still holds the previous
repeat's correct values and is invisible. A misdirected epilogue store of the
second tile into the first tile's columns would then look exactly like a
first-tile fault: one warp's fragment (the column offset lives in a per-warp
uniform register), wrong values of the right magnitude, no operand-level fit,
requires a second tile, timing dependent, and the second tile's own region
silently keeps stale data. Untested, but decidable offline now.

Tests:

1. `claude_fit_error_structure.py replace --capture first-mismatch.pt` on
   every existing mismatch capture (outputs only). Prediction under the
   misdirected-store hypothesis: the faulty block equals the same-local
   fragment of tile (m, n + 16) for grid-48 runs, near zero residual. The
   mode also reports the additive match and the previous H7 form.
2. Probe change, no kernel change: fill `out` with NaN before every launch.
   Any unwritten region becomes visible; a misdirected store shows NaN in the
   second tile's region on faulty repeats.
3. Grid 599 versus 600: only CTA 0 owns two tiles. A fault confined to tile
   (0,0) with 599 localizes the effect to the CTA that has a second tile.
4. The all-releases-after-MMAs arm: per the SASS every release already
   follows the stage's last shared-memory load, so this arm only delays the
   producer; low information compared with 1..3.

Fitter sha 20e13884…: adds H10 (replacement by another tile's fragment) to
trace mode and the outputs-only `replace` mode; synthetic misdirected store
recovered at residual 0.000.

### Release does not wait for the stage's outstanding shared loads (2026-09-25, later)

Results (user): NaN-initialized output, baseline still fails 1/512 with zero
NaNs in every output, so every store lands; the misdirected or lost store
hypothesis (H10) is excluded as mechanism. Group-before-release (named
256-thread barrier before each consumer release) passes 1024, extending to
4096. All-release-after-MMA fails 1/2048. Epilogue barrier fails 84/512.
Correction: the warp-sync arm lowered to NOP, so lane ordering was never
actually tested by it. grid 300 also shows n 16 faults. The fault cause
remains open; the group-before-release arm remains green so far.

Static finding on object 96f193 (control-bit decode, consumer path): every
consumer `SYNCS.ARRIVE.TRANS64.A1T0` issues with an empty wait mask while
shared loads of the stage it releases are still outstanding (their write
scoreboards not yet waited): 9, 18, 18, 8 and 1 loads at the five arrives,
including an LDSM one instruction before the arrive at 0x1030 and `LDS.U8`
scale bytes six instructions before the one at 0x17e0. The DSL lowers
`consumer_release` through `nvvm.mbarrier_txn(kind=ARRIVE, space=CTA)` to a
plain `mbarrier.arrive.shared::cta.b64`, whose PTX default is `.release.cta`.
Release semantics require the warp's prior loads to be performed before the
arrive is observed. Nothing in the SASS enforces that: no wait mask, no
MEMBAR, no DEPBAR. Either the hardware arrive waits for the warp's in-flight
shared loads by itself, or a load still queued in the memory pipeline when
the eighth arrival lands can be overtaken by the producer's TMA refill of the
same stage, and the warp computes that k tile from the next fill's data.

The all-release-after-MMA arm (fails 1/2048) still has 2..3 outstanding
loads at each arrive: ptxas hoisted the arrive above the loads' consumers.
The group-before-release arm (passes) places `BAR.SYNC 0x1, 0x100` before
each arrive, also with an empty wait mask; a block barrier orders prior
memory accesses in hardware, which is the classic `__syncthreads` contract,
so it would close this gap whether or not the arrive does.

Consistency with the geometry: an overtaken load gives k tile t computed from
k tile t + 3 (same tile, both operands and scales) for t <= 44, and from the
NEXT work tile's k tile t - 45 for t in 45..47, which exists only when the
CTA has a second tile. The last three stages of the first tile of a
lock-stepped CTA are the most pressured moment for the shared-memory
pipeline. This is consistent, not established.

Highest-yield discriminators:

1. `cute.arch.fence_acq_rel_cta()` immediately before each of the two
   `consumer_release` calls, no barrier. Per-warp ordering only, arithmetic
   unchanged. Confirm in SASS that a MEMBAR or scoreboard waits precede the
   arrive with zero outstanding shared loads. Pass: the arrive does not order
   the warp's outstanding shared loads on this part (ptxas or hardware), and
   the fence is the faithful minimal fix. Fail while the group barrier
   passes: the coupling is cross-warp.
2. Control: the same fence immediately after the arrive. Must not fix.
3. Fitter H11 (sha f6ff53b0…): one k tile (or one k block) computed from
   k tile t + 3, both operands and scales; injected case fits at 0.011
   against a floor of 0.053. Run on the existing captures. A hit at some
   t <= 44 confirms the overtaken-load signature directly; faults with
   t >= 45 need the next tile's weight rows (full weight as prefix).
4. Vendor-facing: NVIDIA's `blackwell_geforce/dense_gemm.py` has the same
   elected release after ldmatrix; a standalone reproduction with that
   example would make this a toolchain or ISA report.

Retractions and corrections in this entry: the elected-release lane-ordering
gap was never tested (NOP arm); the barrier arms are not "cleared" but the
group barrier is the only green ordering arm; H10 is excluded by the NaN
result; grid 300 includes n 16.

### All-lanes-release variant review; claim boundaries (2026-09-25, later)

Status (user): group-before-release 4096/4096 clean, 5120 total over two
boots, SASS confirms `BAR.SYNC 0x1, 0x100` before each arrive. Outputs-only
replacement fit on the NaN-run failure: best 0.858, no match.
group-main-release-only running; tail-only and fence arms prepared.

Proposed all-lanes release: `CooperativeGroup(Agent.Thread, 256)` as the
TMA consumer group and, at both release sites,
`mainloop_pipeline.sync_object_empty.arrive(state.index,
mainloop_pipeline.consumer_mask)` from every MMA thread instead of
`consumer_release`. Against the local DSL (4.4 dev; the installed 4.6.2 must
be checked for the same fields): the empty barriers are initialized with
`consumer_group.size` arrivals, so 256 is the right count; `consumer_mask`
is the `dst_rank`, which `create` sets to None when the CTA layout has size
1, so `arrive(index, None)` takes the local `mbarrier.arrive` path, the same
instruction the elected release uses; `is_signalling_thread` is `tidx % 32
< 1`, i.e. lane 0 of each warp, and is simply bypassed. Pitfalls: replace
both sites (2573 and 2768) and never also call `consumer_release` (double
count would complete phases early and corrupt silently); leave the cpasync
branch alone; `producer_acquire` and `producer_tail` need no change; the
mbarrier count limit (2^20 - 1) is far above 256. Valid as constructed.

What it can and cannot separate: every lane then arrives after its own
loads in program order, but the SASS shows the arrive carries no wait for
the warp's outstanding shared loads, and there is no reason ptxas would add
one for 32 arrives that it omits for one. If the mechanism is "the arrive
does not order in-flight shared loads", the all-lanes arm still fails and
tells nothing new. If it passes, the election itself mattered, which would
be surprising given that lane 0's own loads are equally unordered, and
would point at a per-thread hardware release that the elected form does not
get. The fence arms are the more informative pair; run all-lanes after them
if capacity allows.

Claim boundaries (agreed with the user's critique):
- Zero NaNs excludes unwritten regions, i.e. lost or misdirected stores that
  leave holes. It does not exclude an in-place overwrite of the right
  address with wrong data. The 0.858 replacement residual further excludes
  "another tile's values" as the wrong data, not partial sums.
- A fence-before-release pass closes a window; it does not name the racing
  agent. Attribution needs, together: the fence-after-arrive control failing,
  the passing object's SASS showing the arrive issued with zero outstanding
  shared loads, and a signature match on a failing capture (H11 for k tiles
  up to 44, the next-tile variant with full weights for the last three
  stages). A standalone reproduction with NVIDIA's geforce example would then
  place the defect at the DSL, ptxas or ISA level rather than in b12x.
- The group barrier's 5120 clean launches are a strong mitigation result,
  not a mechanism; it also changes timing across warps.

### Fitter H12: next-tile forward refill of the final stages (2026-09-25, later)

Results (user): final-stage-only group barrier passes 1024, main-loop-only
group barrier fails 24/1024; fence-before-release passes 1024 with
`MEMBAR.ALL.CTA` before each arrive in its SASS; fence-after-release
reproduces faults (run active). This points at the final three stages of a
tile, whose refill is the next work tile's first three k tiles.

Fitter (sha 6d9ed102…) adds H12: for k tiles t in ktiles - stages ..
ktiles - 1, the stage is refilled with the next work tile's k tile
t - (ktiles - stages); the next tile is linear index + `--grid` (default 48,
m fastest, m' = next mod 3, n' = next div 3). The four TMA transactions land
separately, so every mixture of {A values, A scales, B values, B scales}
refilled versus current is scored (15 per k tile), plus per-k-block variants
for the operand fragments (loaded per k block; scales are loaded once per
stage). Under swap, A is the activation tile of m' and B the weight tile of
n'; rows beyond M are zero-filled as TMA does. Requires the next tile's
weight rows in `weight_prefix` (the checkpoint adapter can supply all rows).
H12 is skipped, with a message, when the CTA has no next tile under the
given grid or the weight rows are missing.

Synthetic self-checks (M 129, next tile (1,16) for block (1,0)): all four
components refilled at k tile 46 fits 0.013 (floor 0.063); A values only at
k tile 45 fits 0.012 (floor 0.057) with the nearest wrong mixture at 0.73;
B scales only at k tile 47 fits 0.019 (floor 0.086); A values of one k
block (46.2) fits at the floor with the per-block variant. This is the
falsifiable signature for the fence and final-stage results: a failing
capture should fit one of these mixtures near its rounding floor if the
mechanism is an overtaken shared load of the final stages; a residual near
1.0 falsifies it.

### Attribution: consumer release issued before the stage's last shared load completed (2026-09-25)

Evidence, three independent legs:

1. Controls (user): fence-before-release (`fence.acq_rel.cta` before both
   `consumer_release` sites) 1024/1024 clean on dusty, about 2000 of a 4096
   run clean on kirby; fence-after-release 39/1024 failures; final-stage-only
   group barrier 1024 clean; main-loop-only group barrier 24/1024 failures.
2. Object (`dense-original-sass.txt`): the hoisted-tail release
   `SYNCS.ARRIVE.TRANS64.A1T0` at 0x2f60 issues one instruction after
   `LDSM.16.M88.4 R60, [R32+0x1000]` at 0x2f50 with an empty wait mask and no
   memory barrier; that load's scoreboard is still outstanding at the arrive.
   The `+0x1000` offset is rows 32..63 of the 64-row activation stage
   (128 B per row), the last k block of the last k tile. In the
   fence-before object a `MEMBAR.ALL.CTA` precedes each arrive.
3. Arithmetic (`replay-engram-129-0-dusty-cpu-fit-checkpoint-h12.log`, full
   checkpoint weights): faulty block (1,14), rows 32..47 block-local, 432
   elements, maxabs 0.043. Best fit H12: k block 47.3 refilled from the next
   work tile (1,30), k tile 2, block 3, A values only, residual 0.033 against
   a rounding floor of 0.128; next best 0.528. Mapping check: block (1,14) is
   linear 43; plus grid 48 is 91 = (1,30). k tile 47 sits in stage 2, which
   the producer refills with the next tile's k tile 2; block 3 is K
   6112..6143 replaced by K 352..383. Rows 32..47 are the 16 rows of one
   `LDSM.x4` fragment, matching the `+0x1000` load. A only and scales
   current match the SASS: the B fragments for that k block were loaded
   earlier and the scale bytes at stage start.

Statement: in this program the consumer's stage release, lowered from
`mbarrier.arrive.shared::cta.b64` (PTX default `.release.cta`), does not
order the warp's in-flight shared-memory loads. When the eighth warp's
arrival lands while its last `ldmatrix` of the stage is still queued, the
producer's TMA refill of that stage (the next work tile's data, in the final
three stages of a tile) can overtake the load, and the warp's last k block is
computed from the next tile's activations. Whether the missing ordering is a
ptxas lowering gap or a hardware property of sm_121a is NVIDIA's to say; the
kernel-level fix is an explicit fence before the release. First-tile
confinement and the cold-start correlation are timing of when that queue
is deep enough; they are not part of the mechanism.

Caveats kept: one capture fitted so far; the fence pass is still
accumulating; main-loop exposure (same-tile refill, H11 signature) is not
observed but is the same instruction pattern.

Patch scope, minimal: `cute.arch.fence_acq_rel_cta()` immediately before
`mainloop_pipeline.consumer_release(...)` at both sites (2573 main loop, 2768
hoisted tail). Every stage, not final only: the SASS shows 8..18 outstanding
shared loads at each main-loop arrive, the same pattern; the final stage is
where the refilled data differs enough to be seen and the last load is one
instruction before the arrive. Final-only would trade an unproven margin
for one fewer fence per k tile. Cost: one CTA-scope fence per k tile per
warp; it makes the next k block's fragment loads complete before the arrive,
which the next MMAs need anyway. The same two lines serve the cpasync path.
Both sites gate on nothing else; arithmetic and tactic set unchanged.

Audit, follow-up builds: other pinned kernels calling `consumer_release`
(attention paged forward and extend, contiguous forward, MoE dynamic with
nine sites, mHC, bf16 gemv prefill, mxfp6 quantization, MTP feedback prefill)
should be checked with the same control-bit decode for arrives with
outstanding shared loads. NVIDIA's geforce dense GEMM example shares the
pattern; a vendor report with the reproducer, the SASS, and the H12 fit is
warranted.

Regression matrix for the build:

1. Isolated reproducer, patched object: at least 4096 launches at 129, 160
   and 192 rows on two nodes; baseline rate about 1 in 10 launches; expect 0.
   Keep fence-after as the negative control record.
2. SASS gate on the built cubin: a fence before every consumer arrive and
   zero outstanding shared loads at each arrive, by the control-bit decode
   used here; fail the build otherwise.
3. Fitter H11 and H12 on any residual mismatch.
4. Kernel repeatability for the other dense tactics that share the kernel:
   split-K decode tactics (m 1..8), the 384 and 512 row winners, K 576
   shared down projection, and any cpasync-path shape; 64 launches each,
   bitwise.
5. Serving exactness: first-token logprobs identical across repeats and
   ranks at 128, 129, 160, 192, 255..265, 272, 288, 320, 384, 400, 448, 512,
   513, 1024, 16384, 131K and 524K rows; the DSA tie-break patch stays.
6. Existing qualification gates: semantic, vision, strict tools, needles.
7. Performance: steps/s and prefill at the standard cells against the
   current image; report the fence cost explicitly.

Findings to act on before the build:

1. Tuning cache drift (scope gap). The tuning record key carries `pin` =
   the pinned override only and `dependencies` = [], never a source digest,
   and the selection cache lives under the cute compile cache
   (`preparation/`), which the Dockerfile moves to a fresh namespace
   `ds41-release-<lock>`. The first serving boot will re-autotune every
   shape, ranked by timing, on a kernel whose timing the fence changed. The
   qualified tactic set can drift silently. Decide explicitly: seed the
   release namespace's `preparation` cache from the qualified combined
   image's records on every node before the first boot (then every
   selection source is "cached" and the winners table is identical), or
   accept re-tuning and gate qualification on a winners comparison with
   `claude_tuning_key_recon.py` against `receipts/combined-tuning.json`.
2. Production tag. The build script's last step moves
   `localhost/voipmonitor/vllm:karmic-main-ds41-deterministic-spark-sm121`
   to the candidate before model qualification, and
   `distribute_determinism.py` distributes by that tag. Tag the candidate
   under a distinct name and move the production tag only after
   qualification; the label already says candidate-not-qualified.
3. Diagnostic overlays. Install checks `trace_attention.py` only; add
   absence checks for `trace_attention_ops.py`, `diagnostic_overlay.py` and
   `freeze_ops_overlay.py` under the vLLM tree, or a digest list of the
   whole `deepseek_v4_1` package from the base image.

triton and cute-dsl caches; first boot recompiles everything and the
serving launcher's compile-environment key applies as before. The
cross-boot 1M-context and needle checks must be re-run on this image, not
