### Shared dense-kernel regression harness (2026-09-25, tightened after review)

`claude_dense_regression.py` (sha 5607a8cf…) with `claude_test_dense_regression.py`
(13 CPU tests, pass) and a `--self-test` mode (pass, 133 winners, 7 shapes).
Prepared and reviewed against the pin; not executed on a GPU here. Review
points addressed: no GPU float64 GEMM; sampled CPU reference with recorded
indices; compile_workers default 20; cpasync proven by the pinned predicate
and one exact error, not a catch-all; consumed layouts verified at run time
against the pinned source; prepare call is a real binding.

What it runs: every `gemm.block_fp8_linear` record of the given receipt
(default `receipts/combined-tuning.json`), enumerated with the record keys
from `claude_tuning_key_recon.py` over capacities x {KS, 576} x NS. Each
record is its own prepared program (`bfl.plan(Caps(...), override=
DenseGemmConfig(**assignment))`, one `PreparationSession(autotune=False,
compile_workers=20)`). The prepare call mirrors the production Engram layer
(vLLM 1794dcf1 `deepseek_v4_1/b12x_layers.py` `make_call`): scratch from
`state.scratch.scratch_specs()`, `state.bind(scratch, source, packed_weight,
output)`, `PreparedCall(run=state.run_binding(binding), produce=source.normal_)`,
so the session prime launch (`types._prime` -> `call.invoke()`) executes the
real quantize + GEMM under the declared-program check. Synthetic seeded E4M3
weights with E8M0 32x32 block scales are packed once per shape; seeded bf16
activations per row count go through `bind` and `run(binding=...)`.

Checks per case, fail-closed:

- finite: full output pre-filled with NaN, every element finite.
- bitwise: >= 64 launches, SHA-256 of the full output identical to the first.
- layout (against the pinned source, `_shared/block_fp8.py`, `_shared/
  wo_mxfp8.py`, `block_fp8_linear/_preparation.py`): `x_q.values` (M, Kphys)
  e4m3, `x_q.scale_rows` (1, M, Kphys/32) e8m0, `x_q.scale_mma` (32, 4,
  ceil(M/128), 4, Kphys/128, 1) with index [r%32, (r//32)%4, r//128, c%4,
  c//4, 0]; the same three planes for the packed weight with N rows; on the
  sampled rows/columns scale_mma maps exactly onto scale_rows; packed weight
  values are the verbatim E4M3 input; K padding (576 -> 640) is zero; weight
  scale_rows equal the 32x32 block scale expanded 32 rows x 1 chunk with 127
  padding. The GEMM consumes `values.view(M, Kphys, 1)` + `scale_mma` for
  both operands (`_run`), so the reference reads the consumed planes.
- accuracy, sampled and recorded, not a full oracle: rows = {0, M-1, M//2}
  plus (b-1, b) at boundaries of tile_m, tile_n and 128 (up to 24 boundaries,
  thinned evenly), full width; columns likewise over N, full height. CPU
  float64 product of the consumed E4M3 values dequantized with the E8M0
  scales read through the scale_mma mapping, tolerance 2^-7 |ref| + 2^-7
  rms(ref). The receipt stores `sampled_rows`, `sampled_cols`, the tiles
  used, `reference_elements`, and `reference_k` (Kphys, or K if the
  activation padding columns were non-finite, which would poison only the
  reference since the weight padding is zero).

Any exception fails the case. The receipt records identity, applied
environment, installed `dense_gemm.py` digest, every program key, every
case and the per-shape weight layout check; markers
`CLAUDE-DENSE-REGRESSION-PASS` / `-FAIL`.

Coverage at the combined receipt: 133 programs; K x N in 576x5120 (shared
down), 1280x4096, 1280x8192, 5120x512, 5120x1152, 5120x1792, 6144x25600;
capacities 1..8 (split-K 2 and 4 decode tactics, M <= 8), 12..32, 128, 256,
384, 512, 8192; swapped and unswapped tiles from 16x64 to 128x128; tile_k
64 and 128; unroll both. Row counts per capacity: capacity and capacity-1
(`--rows-per-capacity 3` adds capacity/2).

cpasync (`--cpasync-attempt`, on the largest winner, 6144x25600 at 8192),
three exact checks and anything else fails the run:

1. `knob_values(dense_query(plan.query))["load_path"] == ("tma",)`
   (`b12x/gemm/_tuning.py`: `("tma", "cpasync") if recipe == "nvfp4" else
   ("tma",)`; block-FP8 linear lowers to recipe `mxfp8`).
2. `DenseGemmKernel.can_implement` called with the exact arguments
   `validate_config` passes (ab E4M3, sf E8M0, sf_vec 32, bf16 out, the
   winner's tile, cluster (1,1), N, Kphys, batch 1, k/k/n) returns False for
   `load_path="cpasync"` (pinned 4900..4902: cpasync only for E2M1 with
   16-wide scales) and True for `"tma"` (positive control).
3. `bfl.plan(caps, override=cfg_cpasync).memory_requirements()` runs
   `contract.configure` -> `validate_config` and must raise exactly
   `ValueError("dense GEMM load_path is outside its recipe/caller domain")`.

No valid cpasync case exists for this recipe; the two fence lines serve that
path in the source regardless.

Environment: the split-K atomic codegen snapshot is read when b12x is
imported, so `--launch-env dusty` applies the launcher's compile-relevant
keys (B12X_*, CUTE_*, CUDA_HOME) first; the combined winners exist only
under B12X_DENSE_SPLITK_TURBO=1 (`test_turbo_off_changes_keys` shows zero
matches otherwise). Split-K 4 reduces through BF16 atomics; a bitwise
failure confined to those cases is a finding about that path.

Exact invocation, inside the candidate image on an idle node:

    podman run --rm --pull=never --network=none --device nvidia.com/gpu=all \
      -v $PWD:/diag:ro -v $PWD/receipts:/diag/receipts:rw \
      -e PYTHONUNBUFFERED=1 --entrypoint /opt/venv/bin/python <image> -u \
      /diag/claude_dense_regression.py --receipt /diag/receipts/combined-tuning.json \
      --launch-env dusty --repeats 64 --rows-per-capacity 2 --cpasync-attempt \
      --report /diag/receipts/dense-regression-<image-id-prefix>.json

Subsets for a quick pass: `--only 6144x25600 --capacities 256,384,512`;
decode: `--capacities 1,2,3,4,5,6,7,8`. CPU checks on the workstation:
`python claude_dense_regression.py --self-test` and
`python -m unittest claude_test_dense_regression` (needs torch and the
`~/git/b12x` checkout for the pinned-source tests, which read blobs with
`git show` only).

serving Dockerfile, not the immediate parent. The parent identity is enforced
by `build_dense_release.py` against the lock's `base_image_id` 30ef9c2d…, so
this is provenance wording, not a defect.

Findings, most important first:

1. The dense-release guard in `start_moe_repaired.py` checks the workstation
   environment, which never reaches the nodes. It refuses a local
   `B12X_DENSE_SPLITK_TURBO` or `VLLM_DS41_L2_PREFETCH`, but ssh forwards no
   environment here (no SendEnv/SetEnv in ~/.ssh/config or /etc/ssh). The
   effective values come from each node's non-interactive ssh shell through
   `render(node)`, and `runtime.py`'s in-container check is tautological for
   those optional switches (it renders from the container env it was given).
   Nothing positively proves the serving containers run the upstream turbo
   value and no prefetch override. Cheapest fix on the qualification side:
   `qualify_dense_release.py` already parses the container env; compare it
   with `render(node)['env']` plus `B12X_DYNAMIC_DETERMINISTIC_OUTPUT=1`, or
   at least require `B12X_DENSE_SPLITK_TURBO == '1'` and no
   `VLLM_DS41_L2_PREFETCH`.
2. `qualify_dense_release.py` checks identity once, before hours of probes.
   Record `State.StartedAt`/`RestartCount` per node and re-check running
   state, image, kit label and those fields at the end, so a restart
   mid-qualification cannot pass silently.
3. `freeze_dense_release.py` defers the build-lock, source-lock and recipe
   label checks to `run_node.py` at launch time. They match today, but
   checking them (and `local-inference.status`) at freeze time fails before
   any node is touched. Everything else is sound: build receipt and inspect
   Id agree, prior kit drift is refused, the overlay is dropped, the
   amendment is exclusive-create, and a crash between the two writes leaves
   kit drift that `kit_digest` rejects. `trace_attention_ops.py` stays in
   the kit manifest; harmless, since the pin has no overlay and
   `diagnostic_overlay.validate` would reject any overlay for this image.
4. `start_moe_repaired.py` launches node by node and discovers a missing
   image or failed preflight only at that node, after earlier ranks are
   already up. A `run_node.py --preflight-only` pass over all four before
   the first launch would avoid partial starts.
5. `qualify_original_needle.py` keeps the prompt identity (both digests) and
   the exact-answer rule. The request adds `logprobs`, `top_logprobs=20`
   and a per-repeat `cache_salt` to the historical body. The salt is what
   makes the three repeats cold and the metrics window proves it. The
   logprobs are extra relative to the original 524K failure request. At
   temperature 0 token choice should not depend on them, but I have not
   verified the pinned sampler and speculative path for that. Its
   `identical` compares logprob floats bitwise, which is stricter than the
   answer; the failure message already separates the two. A response with
   `logprobs: null` raises AttributeError rather than the intended message
   (still fails closed).
6. Receipt provenance: `qualify_dense_release.py` records each sub-script's
   digest but not `cache_metrics.py`, which all three accounting probes
   import. Recording it closes the loop.

Conversation gate: `claude_gate_conversations.py` (a0ff998c…) with
`claude_test_gate_conversations.py` (19 tests, pass on the system python3,
stdlib only). Adapted from `spark/ds41/r38/probe-conversations.py`
(4c58b0a1…, unchanged; the gate records its digest). A provenance test
asserts the historical prompts, answers, 0.95 threshold, archive-identity
regex and overlap rule appear verbatim in both files.

- Serial prefix phase: cold, exact repeat, conversation extension, divergent
  suffix. One request per idle window through `cache_metrics.idle_snapshot`
  and `finish` (queries == prompt tokens, one success, no preemption). Cold
  needs zero hits; the others need hits/prompt >= 0.95. The hit count is the
  scheduler's admission record (`record_prefix_cache_stats` with the local
  computed tokens, scheduler.py 1745), the quantity usage `cached_tokens`
  would report. Request-boundary checkpoints are off for this model type at
  vLLM 1794dcf1, so hits come from the block-hash path.
- Concurrent phase: four three-turn conversations with exact answers, plus
  the optional mixed needle (fresh archive identity, historical expected
  answer), overlap evidence by the historical rule. Aggregate accounting
  from an idle baseline to a settled idle end: successes and queries must
  equal exactly this phase's requests (foreign traffic fails), no
  preemption. With a needle, aggregate hits must be zero: per-request hits
  are nonnegative, so zero proves every request cold, including the needle.
  Any positive aggregate fails closed, because counters cannot attribute it;
  the receipt then records the only provable bound (needle prefilled at
  least prompt tokens minus aggregate hits). Without a needle, conversation
  hits are reported, not judged, as in the historical probe.
- Tests drive the real `main()` and the task tree's `cache_metrics.py`
  against an in-process loopback fake of `/v1/chat/completions` and
  `/metrics` (labelled counters), covering pass with and without the needle,
  wrong answer, stale cold hit, no reuse, needle hits, legitimate
  conversation reuse with a needle (fails closed by design), foreign request,
  preemption, no overlap, HTTP error receipt, and existing output directory.

Residual: the gate needs a quiet endpoint for the whole concurrent phase
(the 524K prefill), so any other client, including a completion watchdog,
fails it closed. If DS4.1's hash block is small enough that turn 2 or 3
reuses an earlier turn, the mixed run fails with "coldness cannot be
proven"; the receipt's per-turn prompt tokens show whether that happened.
Invocation, from the workstation against the serving head:

    python3 claude_gate_conversations.py --out receipts/conversations-<tag> \
      --mixed-needle-input ../../ds41/r38/receipts/20260916/admission-k7-1m-u80/needle-524288-input.json

### Pre-live review: identity edits, dense control scope, conversation gate (2026-09-25)

Read-only. Reviewed `start_moe_repaired.py` (5d7fb29e…), `freeze_dense_release.py`
(0ba512bc…), `qualify_dense_release.py` (b77bbf4a…) and the dense receipts
`dense-release-shared-3413799408e0{,-nonatomic}.json`.

Edits: all four earlier findings are closed. The dense-release launch clears
remote-shell overrides with `env -u` and sets deterministic MoE 1 and turbo 0
explicitly; `runtime.py` enforces every non-optional key against the kit. All
four hosts pass `--preflight-only` before the first launch. Freeze now checks
the inherited build-lock, source-lock, recipe and not-qualified labels.
Qualification requires deterministic 1, turbo 0 and no prefetch key in each
container and compares Id, Image, RestartCount and StartedAt before and after
the suite. Minor: a failed preflight raises before its log is written, and a
failed probe skips the after-identity record; both only lose diagnostics.

Dense receipts: broad run (turbo 1) 227/227 split-K 1 pass, 27/27 split-K 4
fail bitwise, 4 of 5 atomic split-K 2 fail sampled accuracy (ratios 1.04 to
1.38; the passing one 0.87). Non-atomic control (turbo 0) 105/105 pass, max
ratio 0.40, split-K 2 max 0.38. Reading: two-slice atomic BF16 is repeatable
(zeroed output, one commutative add) but rounds each partial to BF16 before
the sum, so its error exceeds a single-rounding tolerance; four-slice atomic
is order dependent. Neither is the fence. Split-K 1 programs have the same
lowering under both turbo values (`split_k_atomic_bf16 = slices > 1 and
TURBO` at dense_gemm.py 8292; the kernel's atomic branch sits under
`split_k_slices > 1`), so the broad split-K 1 passes carry over to turbo 0
for the same configurations. I did not confirm that the program keys are
identical.

Coverage gap in my harness: `combined-tuning.json` holds 152 records with
dense-GEMM configurations; the harness enumerated 133. The other 19 are
`gemm.block_fp8_linear` K=15360 N=5120 at all 19 capacities (brute-forced
over K and N multiples of 32; 8 of them split-K 4 decode). K 15360 was not in
my K table, and the harness skips unknown receipt records silently. 15360 is
3 x 5120, consistent with the DSpark draft `main_proj` over three
concatenated target hidden states (inferred, not traced). Neither the broad
run nor the non-atomic control exercised this shape.

Evidence still needed before calling the dense side qualified under the live
profile:

1. Harness fix (mine, not yet applied because this review is read-only):
   add 15360 to the enumerated K set and fail when any dense-config record in
   the receipt is not enumerated. Then the control grows to 64 decode
   programs and 120 cases, and the broad run to 296 cases; the fixed counts in
   `run_dense_regression.py` must follow.
2. The live profile autotunes fresh under turbo 0, so its winners are not the
   hand-mapped control. Retain the live boot's selection cache as a tuning
   receipt, compare winners with the combined receipt as planned, and run the
   harness on that receipt with turbo 0 on an idle node after the window. The
   completeness assertion in item 1 is what makes that run trustworthy:
   `record_key(turbo=False)` has only been checked against a receipt it
   generated itself.
3. Record that the dense selections were tuned in this boot, not read from a
   cached namespace.

Conversation gate under the live profile: all turn prompts sit under one
256-token block, so aggregate hits should be zero and the mixed-needle
coldness proof should hold. Indicative only: in the R38 receipts identical
282-token and 47-token prompts recorded zero cached tokens, so a hash
granularity finer than the block is unlikely there. With `--max-num-seqs 4`
five concurrent requests means one waits, as in the historical probe. The gate
treats any preemption as failure, which the historical probe did not; a 524K
needle plus four conversations could preempt under KV pressure, and that
would fail the gate with the reason recorded rather than pass silently. Run
it inside the live window after the correctness suite, with no other client
on the endpoint.

### First-request divergence at length 128, live dense-release boot (2026-09-25)

Receipts `receipts/dense-release-20260925T140235Z`. Read-only; no requests or
node actions.

Observed:
- 128-0 was the first completion of the process (14:12:54.96 to 14:13:26.71
  UTC, 31.75 s). 128-1 took 5.49 s, 128-2..5 about 0.45 s. No other request
  in the sweep exceeded 1 s except the 16384 prefills (3.9 to 8.2 s, all
  identical outputs).
- Position 0 (from the prefill) is bitwise identical including all top-20
  logprobs. Positions 1 and 2 differ: the chosen tokens agree, alternates
  move by up to 1.6 nats and three of twenty top entries change. So the
  prefill step was exact and the divergence is confined to the verify step.
- Adaptive verification did not change depth: the first SpecDecoding line
  (14:13:34) covers exactly the six 128 requests (drafted 42 = 6 x 7), mean
  verification depth 7.00/7, per-position acceptance 1,1,0,... for all. With
  one verify step per request and a reset confidence of 1.0 for new requests
  the budget is always full here. This does not support the adaptive-shape
  lead for this failure.
- No JIT warnings, no Engram messages, nothing logged between startup and the
  first response on any rank.
- Telemetry, one-second samples. The engine counted the 128 prompt tokens and
  the first token in the 14:13:04-14:13:14 window, so the verify step ran
  after that. From 14:13:17 to 14:13:26 dusty (19.8 W, 2522 MHz) and toby
  (17.6 W, 2561 MHz) hold a flat elevated plateau with essentially no NVMe
  reads, ending when the response returns; rusty sits at 16.4 W; kirby stays
  near idle power (13.3 W) but reads about 120 MB from NVMe between 14:13:16
  and 14:13:20. The plateau lasts about ten seconds. 128-1 shows reads on
  every rank (14:13:27-31) and no plateau.

Source facts (vLLM 1794dcf1): with `VLLM_DS41_ENGRAM_OVERLAP` on (default),
each step's disk lookups for all Engram layers run as one host job on a side
thread and stream, which publishes the ready epoch only after every layer's
lookup; each Engram layer (1 and 14) spins in `_wait_engram_rows` with a
5000 ms limit, and on timeout stores `failed` and proceeds to all-reduce
`staged_rows` as they are. `failed` (epochs[2]) is never read anywhere in the
pin. Engram rows are sharded by TP rank and summed by that all-reduce, so one
late rank changes every rank's result.

Leading hypothesis, unproven: in the first verify step kirby's lookup job ran
past both 5 s waits, kirby's Engram layers consumed unready rows, and the other
ranks spun in the collective meanwhile (NCCL spin at higher power on
dusty/toby, the single-CTA wait at low power on kirby). This fits the ten
second plateau (two 5 s timeouts), the rank asymmetry, the exact prefill, the
verify-only divergence, and 128-1 being slow but correct (reads finished
inside the limit). It does not explain why kirby's reads stopped at 14:13:20
while the plateau continued to 14:13:26, or why an eight-token step read
about 120 MB. Earlier trace boots found `failed` = 1 on every rank in every
capture with the first transition never observed, which is consistent but
not specific.

Alternatives kept open: first-use host work on one rank (GIL or lazy
initialization) delaying the lookup thread, which is the same failure path
with a different trigger; different junk drafts after EOS changing MoE batch
composition (no latency signature expected, so it fits the plateau poorly);
a lazy first-use kernel path not covered by the JIT monitor.

Highest-yield next tests:
1. On the live, warm process (a request, your call): six repeats of a prompt
   with the same template but a new retrieval code and filler word, so the
   verify-step n-grams are cold while the process is warm. Divergence of the
   first repeat, with long latency and NVMe reads, points at cold lookups, not
   process-first initialization; identical repeats point the other way.
2. Next boot, instrumented: per rank and per step, record the lookup job's
   wall time and read then reset epochs (ready, expected, failed) after each
   forward. Prediction: `failed` flips on at least one rank in the 128-0
   verify step and never in 128-1..5. No flip with divergence still present
   rejects the hypothesis.
3. Boot A/B with `VLLM_DS41_ENGRAM_OVERLAP=0` (needs a launch-contract
   amendment) and the same sweep order: prediction is identical 128-0 with a
   long but correct first request.
4. A second unchanged boot repeats the order. Divergence again supports a
   cold-start mechanism; cross-boot equality of the divergent output would
   not discriminate, because stale staged rows can be deterministic.

Qualification implication: until this is resolved, any gate whose first
request runs cold after startup (including the original 524K replay) can
fail this way; a warm-up request would hide it and must not be used as
evidence of determinism. The silent 5 s timeout is a source defect
regardless of whether it caused this instance: a timeout should fail the
request or be surfaced, not proceed.

### Residuals: predecessor-dependent verify step at 385, rare 16K prefill outlier (2026-09-25)

Read-only analysis of `dense-release-full-20260925`, `dense-release-residual-20260925`
and `dense-release-transition-20260925` (all on the same live process). Per-request
metric deltas were recomputed from the retained scrapes, not from the log.

Facts:
- Every divergent 385 request differs only at positions 1 and 2 (the verify
  step); position 0 from the prefill is bitwise identical, and the chosen
  tokens never change. Maximum chosen-token logprob change 1.2e-7.
- The divergence is a deterministic function of the immediately preceding
  request. Across three cycles, the first 385 after two 384s is always
  `ac45789f`, after two 400s always `2b5abbd7`, and after 385s always the mode
  `ce415910`. The second and third 385 always return to the mode. The first
  385 after other predecessors gave further distinct signatures (`7f1e22b0`
  after 384 in the sweep, `f9933c15` after 16384, `feb0e0b8` at cycle 0).
  History depth is one request.
- These requests take 0.30 to 0.37 s with identical per-request spec-decode
  deltas (1 draft, 7 draft tokens, 2 accepted, 3 generated) and identical
  iteration shapes (one step of at most 512 tokens, one of at most 8). A 5 s
  wait cannot fire inside them, so the Engram timeout does not explain this
  class. 128-0 at startup has the same verify-only signature; the simpler
  reading is that it belongs to this class and its 30 s stall is a separate
  first-use cost. That supersedes my earlier leading hypothesis for 128-0.
- Only 385 (and 128 after startup) is sensitive among the lengths swept; the
  first repeat after a length change at every other swept length matched its
  mode, and each length's mode is the same across all three runs.
- 16384: one outlier in 46 requests (full sweep repeat 1), different at every
  position including the prefill, 8.06 s prefill. Prefill times of identical
  16K prompts range 3.5 to 10.4 s, and the slowest (10.4 s) matched the mode,
  so latency alone does not predict it. This is a separate, rare class and the
  only one where a timing race such as the Engram timeout remains in play.

Source facts relevant to history dependence (vLLM 1794dcf1):
- `BlockPool.free_blocks` puts blocks without a prefix hash (a request's
  partial tail blocks; with a per-request `cache_salt` nothing else matches)
  at the FRONT of the free queue, LIFO, while hashed full blocks go to the
  tail. The next request's first allocations are therefore the previous
  request's partial blocks, still holding its KV values in slots the new
  request has not written. Any read of an unwritten slot, or of a partial
  compression window, becomes a deterministic function of the previous
  request.
- DS4.1 decode metadata: `_write_decode_metadata` builds SWA indices and
  lengths per row, and `_pages` copies the main block table across the full
  `_main_width`, including entries past the request's own blocks; bounds rely
  on `top_lengths` and the indexer's top-k lengths.
- The verify step runs in the FULL CUDA graph for 8 tokens; the 385-token
  prefill does not. Persistent graph inputs are the other natural carrier of
  request-to-request state.

Ranked hypotheses for the 385 class:
1. A verify-step kernel reads KV slots or a compression window past the last
   written position in the request's tail block (stale content from the
   LIFO-recycled block of the previous request) without fully masking it,
   for example a partial c4 window or an indexer tile, and only at lengths
   where that read lands in a slot the previous request wrote differently.
2. A shape-only leak: stale per-request metadata or a cached split or
   scheduler plan in the graph's persistent inputs that is derived from the
   previous request's lengths and changes the reduction order.
3. Draft-side state carried by the DSpark drafter changing the junk draft
   rows after the end-of-sequence token, which alter rows 0 and 1 only
   through cross-row coupling (MoE expert grouping). Weakest: it needs both a
   stale drafter state and a coupling path.

Highest-yield next tests, requests only, on the warm process:
1. Content versus shape: prime with a 384-token prompt of different content
   (fresh code and filler, same length), then the standard 385. Same output
   as after the standard 384 (`ac45789f`) means the leak is shape-only
   (hypothesis 2); a different output means stale content (1 or 3).
2. Predecessor map: single primes at 383, 386, 387, 388, 391, 392, 393, 408,
   416 and 512, each followed by the standard 385. The set of predecessors
   that change the output, and which of them give identical signatures,
   exposes the boundary quantity (4, 16, 128 or 256 tokens, or a tile width)
   and points at the component.
3. Same length, different content: a fresh-content 385 followed by the
   standard 385. A shape-only leak predicts the mode (same-length
   predecessor); a content leak predicts a new signature, because the
   standard 385 inherits the fresh request's tail-block values. This is the
   mirror image of test 1 and checks the LIFO path directly.
4. For the 16K class: keep counting outliers; its independent test is the
   instrumented Engram epoch capture already planned.

Instrumented boot, once the above narrows it: for the verify step of the
first 385 after 384 and of a repeat, dump the request's block-table row and
the tail block's slots past the valid length for each KV group, plus the
decode metadata buffers. Equal inputs with different outputs point at
graph-persistent state; different tail-block content that is read points at
hypothesis 1.

Engram timeout, kept separate: the silent 5 s timeout with a never-read
`failed` word, and a lookup exception surfacing only at the next step's
`_finish_engram_job` (after the current step already proceeded on unready
rows), remain source defects worth an upstream issue regardless of whether
they caused any observed divergence.

### Content-dependent 385 residual: source inspection and minimal capture (2026-09-25)

Input: `receipts/dense-release-content-transition-20260925` (meadow/826493 385
twice, then original 385 three times, 3 cycles): first original always
`1b1f671559a5`, later ones `ce4159105f31`; the meadow prime itself gives
`ded32c5e5522` then `dfc17df44f21` each cycle. Previous content matters, not
only length. Model facts from the pinned config (fetched from the public repo
at fb2764a5, git blob 09917a91 equal to model-manifest.json): 40 layers,
compress_ratios 0,0 then 2 for layers 2 to 19 and 1 for layers 20 to 39,
kv_source_layer_ids 2, 8, 14, 20, sliding_window 128, index_topk 512, Engram
layers 1 and 14, so the CED decoder starts at layer 20.

Source inspection (vLLM 1794dcf1), what can carry the previous request into
the next one:
- KV blocks. `BlockPool.free_blocks` puts unhashed blocks (each request's
  partial tail blocks, and every ring block) at the front of the free queue,
  LIFO. The next request's first allocations are the previous request's tail
  and ring blocks with their old contents in unwritten slots.
- Ratio-2 compressor rings (layers 2 to 19). `CompressorStateCache` is a
  `CircularBufferSpec` of 16 slots (drafts 7 -> `max(8, 1 << 4)`), one block
  per request. Only the last 16 prefill rows write ring slots
  (`emit & (t >= end - PAGE)` in `_tokens`). At a step whose first position
  is odd (385 is), `_load_partial_state` reads the predecessor position's
  slot `(pos - 1) % 16` of the ring page from column 0. For 385 that is
  position 384, written by this request's prefill, so by reading alone this
  path looks correct; the per-step `_pending_*` buffers are fully rewritten.
- CED (layers 21 to 39 run only the last 128 prefill rows; layer 20 is the
  full-resolution KV source for them). `swa_replay_start` is `seq - 128` in
  the prefill but `prefix_start` (0 for a fresh request) in the verify step;
  the SWA window alone then bounds verify reads at `pos + 1 - 128`, which for
  385 starts at 258. Not a stale read by itself.
- Verify inputs. The target's verify rows 0 and 1 give positions 1 and 2.
  They can only depend on the previous request through (a) stale cache slots
  they read, or (b) other rows of the same verify batch, i.e. the drafts
  after the end-of-sequence token, through batch-coupled kernels (MoE expert
  grouping, tactic choice by per-expert row count). The DSpark drafter has
  its own paged caches with the same LIFO recycling, so a stale drafter read
  would change the junk drafts without changing any accepted token, which
  matches the counters (always 1 draft, 7 tokens, 2 accepted).

I could not close the question by reading alone: nothing I read in the target
path obviously consumes an unwritten slot for 385 and not for 129, 257 or
513. The capture below is designed to answer it directly.

Minimal capture, prepared, not installed or run:
- `claude_stale_state_probe.py` (sha 39243779...) and
  `claude-stale-state-model_state.patch` (sha 031b1a72..., two added lines at
  the end of `DeepseekV41ModelState.__init__`; applies to the pinned blob
  7004e7fc..., result ba4a27d5...). Tests: `claude_test_stale_state_probe.py`
  (15 stdlib tests, pass; pure logic plus pinned-source checks of the slot
  mapping, hook signatures and order, the patch, and LIFO reuse).
- Hook: `prepare_attn` stores this step's block tables, KV-cache config and
  metadata; `prepare_inputs` (runtime order: metadata, inputs, forward)
  matches a single-request step with 8 scheduled tokens whose prompt token
  ids hash to the control digest, then acts before the forward is launched.
  No graph node is added: the FULL graph for the verify step is captured and
  replayed unchanged. Observe adds a device sync and host copies before the
  matched step only; without the control file it is a stat per step.
- Observe snapshot per rank: the verify step's input ids and positions (the
  drafts), and for every KV-cache layer in every group (target and drafter)
  the request's block-table row, the bytes at each position [0, seq_len)
  (None where no block backs it), the bytes of the unwritten tail slots of the
  last touched block, plus the step metadata tensors, CED state, lookback ids
  and every compressor's pending buffers. Caches are accessed as an in-place
  `(blocks, page, bytes)` view; a layout that cannot be viewed raises instead
  of silently copying.
- Transplant writes the matched repeat's bytes into chosen layers and
  positions of the first original before its verify step; poison fills them
  with a byte (255 is NaN for fp32, bf16 and e4m3).

Procedure (all ranks need the same control file in /cache):
1. Control digest for the original 385 prompt (frozen corpus tokens):
   `68a1a5493c155cadc1c6762976a8668a0394d1b8815482dbf34caf35d12995bc`.
   Actions `[observe orig-first, observe orig-repeat]`. Run one
   content-transition cycle (meadow twice, original twice).
   Reproducer gate: the two originals must still hash `1b1f671559a5` and
   `ce4159105f31`; otherwise the capture perturbed the path, stop.
2. `python3 claude_stale_state_probe.py diff orig-first-<node>.pt
   orig-repeat-<node>.pt` inside the image, per rank. Reading:
   step_inputs differ -> drafts differ, look at the drafter layers first;
   per-position values in [0, 385) differ -> the prefill itself wrote
   different state; only tail offsets or unbacked positions differ -> stale
   slots that exist, not yet shown to be read.
3. Transplant bisection on later cycles: the first original gets
   `transplant` from orig-repeat on the candidate set from step 2; a result
   hash of `ce4159105f31` means the set is sufficient. Halve with
   `bisect_selects` (by layer regex, then position range) until minimal.
   Each round is one cycle of four 0.3 s requests.
4. Confirm with poison on exactly the minimal set (output must change) and on
   its complement (output must not change). This names the consumed stale
   slots and the cache layer that holds them.

Limits: this locates stale state that is read, and the layer group holding
it; it does not record per-layer activations. If the diff shows only
drafter-side differences, the target-side mechanism is batch coupling, and a
second step is needed (the same transplant applied to the drafter caches,
then the verify rows with drafts pinned). A per-layer activation capture
inside the FULL graph would add copy nodes to the graph and must re-pass the
external reproducer before use. The helper must be installed hash-gated in a
diagnostic image derived from 3413799408e0 (not the attention-trace image,
which lacks the fence).

Engram timeout, separately (no change proposed as a fix): the failure
handling in the pinned source is unsound in three ways. The device wait gives
up after 5000 ms and proceeds with whatever `staged_rows` holds; `failed`
(epochs[2]) is written and never read or reset anywhere; and a lookup
exception on the side thread surfaces only at the next step's
`_finish_engram_job`, after the current step has already used unready rows
and possibly returned its response. Earlier trace boots found `failed` set on
every rank in every capture, so at least one timeout happened in each of
those boots, at an unknown step. A correct repair has to make a timed-out or
failed lookup fail the step before its output leaves the engine (for example
an epoch-tagged failure word checked at the step's existing sync point) and
propagate lookup exceptions in the same step. A longer timeout, a warm-up
request or disabling overlap would each hide the symptom and must not be
reported as a fix.

### Stale-state probe: fail-closed coverage and tail-domain writes (2026-09-25)

Review points addressed in `claude_stale_state_probe.py` (sha dd2a2c15...):
- Layer classification is fail closed. Every layer of every KV-cache group
  gets a status (captured, `not_in_forward_context`, `no_cache`,
  `empty_cache`, `unsupported spec`, `no_block_table`, `layout: ...`); any
  omission aborts the matched step unless the control's `allow_omitted` regex
  names it, and omitted layers are recorded in the snapshot either way. Group
  count must equal block-table count (no silent zip truncation); an empty
  group or zero captured layers fails.
- Required coverage (default, overridable but never empty): `.swa_cache` for
  layers 0..42 (target 0..39, draft 40..42 per `layers.{num_hidden_layers +
  i}` in dspark.py), `.compressor.state_cache` for 2..19, `.attn` main caches
  and `.indexer.k_cache` for sources 2, 8, 14, 20.
- Byte layout from the spec, not guessed: record = `page_size_bytes //
  num_states` (SWA 528, main 288, index keys 68, ring 4096); the payload is
  the first `page * record` bytes of each block row (allocator padding kept
  out), viewed in place; any layout that would copy or is short fails.
- Writes take one domain per select item: `positions` or `tail` (offsets into
  the captured unwritten tail). Transplant validates the source layer's
  layout (spec, page, ratio, circular, record bytes, backed-position pattern,
  tail length); each physical slot is written once; a slot reached from
  several positions (ratio 2) or items must have identical source bytes; an
  action that writes no slot raises. Observe takes no extra keys.

Tests: `claude_test_stale_state_probe.py` (27 stdlib tests, pass: parsing,
classification, coverage, record layout, write planning for both domains,
uniqueness, layout keys, bisection, plus pinned-source checks of prefixes,
record sizes, padding, slot mapping, hook order, patch and LIFO reuse).
`claude_test_stale_state_probe_torch.py` (sha b789e6df..., 8 tests: in-place
padded views, float ring view, gather, poison and transplant for positions
and tail, layout and shared-slot refusals, zero-write refusal, fail-closed
classification) is written for CPU torch inside the pinned image and skips
here; it has not run yet. The patch is unchanged (031b1a72...).

New evidence noted, source follow-up kept open: the predecessor map repeats
each first-385 signature exactly per predecessor length, and one second 385
after 392 differed from the first token on at 0.49 s
(`dense-release-predecessor-map-20260925/1-392-target/385-1.json`), so fast

   the engine step (fail closed, but it ends the serving session). Operator
   rules: validate each control offline with `parse_control` before copying;
   write it atomically (temp file plus rename) because the engine can read a
   half-written file; place it on all four ranks before the first prime
   request and do not change it mid-cycle (each rank keeps its own action

4. Snapshot interpretation: the snapshot is taken before the verify forward,
   so positions seq_len-8..seq_len-1 are backed but not yet written by this
   request; differences there are expected and a transplant there is
   overwritten by the forward unless the step reads them first. Ring caches
   are captured per position, so each of the 16 ring slots appears many
   times with identical bytes.
5. Matching assumes a full verify of 8 scheduled tokens; if adaptive

### Local capture driver for the stale-state probe (2026-09-25)

`claude_stale_capture.py` with `claude_test_stale_capture.py` (7 stdlib tests,
pass; nodes and HTTP mocked). `preflight` passed locally: lock inputs match,
candidate.json pins the built image 5eb584a6..., original 385 digest
68a1a549..., prime (meadow/826493) digest 0901c349..., control validates with
the helper's own `parse_control`.

Request identity: the helper matches on prompt digest and an 8-token single
request step, not on request id, so a request with two verify steps would
consume two actions. For this capture that cannot happen unnoticed: every
prime and original in the content-transition receipts had exactly 1 draft,
7 draft tokens and 3 generated tokens, i.e. one verify step, and the driver
requires those deltas per request and exactly one new observe marker per rank
after each original (two markers after the first original fail the run).
Request-id gating in the helper is recommended before any multi-step use.

Arming: the helper reads the control only at a real engine step, so no rank
can prove it is armed before the first request. The driver deploys and
re-reads the control digest on all four ranks first, then uses the first
prime (which cannot match: different digest) as the arming probe and stops,
disarmed, before any original if a rank lacks the `control loaded` line. A
strict "armed before any request" proof would need a helper change.

`diff` runs the helper baked into the image under `/opt/venv/bin/python`,
CPU only, no network, on each rank, and refuses to run beside a live serving
container unless `--alongside-serving` is given (one job per node).

### Alternative interpretation: drafts, batch coupling, logprob row association (2026-09-25)

Read-only source and receipt review, vLLM 1794dcf1.

Row association (weakest alternative). The V2 rejection sampler
(`vllm/v1/worker/gpu/spec_decode/rejection_sampler.py`) passes the device
`cu_num_logits` to `compute_topk_scores` under adaptive verification because
compaction can shrink the verified rows. In these requests compaction does
not happen: the per-request counters show 7 of 7 drafts verified, and
`compact_batch` returns early when the budget equals the scheduled drafts, so
device and host boundaries are both [0, 8] for the single request. The
receipts also argue against a row swap: in every divergent 385 the chosen
tokens (184, then EOS) keep logprobs within 1e-5 of zero in both runs and
most top-20 entries are shared; only low-probability alternates move (up to
1.6 nats). A swapped or misassigned row would move the chosen token itself.
A layout bug would also be deterministic per request shape, not dependent on
the previous request's content.

Draft suffix plus batch coupling (strongest alternative). Positions 1 and 2
come from verify rows 0 and 1 (inputs 739 and 184). By the metadata builders
those rows are causally isolated from rows 2..7: the SWA window, the indexed
top-k length `(pos + 1) // ratio` and the ratio-2 pair visibility are all
computed per row. They can still depend on rows 2..7 (the junk drafts after
EOS) through batch-coupled kernels, for example MoE expert grouping where a
tactic or accumulation path depends on each expert's row count. The drafts
come from the DSpark drafter (DFlash-style), whose context KV is inserted
into its own SWA caches (`precompute_and_store_context_kv`); those caches are
paged and recycled LIFO like the target's, and the draft `_chunk` branch reads
a 128-position window before the request position. Stale drafter slots would
make the junk drafts depend on the previous request's content, which gives
the same signature: exact prefill, verify-only change, content dependence,
one-request memory, unchanged acceptance counters (EOS stops acceptance at
position 2). Nothing in the receipts distinguishes this from stale target
state.

Distinguishing evidence:
1. Free, already in the capture: `step_inputs.input_ids` of orig-first versus
   orig-repeat are the 8 verify inputs, i.e. 739 plus the 7 drafts. Different
   drafts are necessary for the coupling explanation; identical drafts
   exclude it (same batch composition), leaving target or other persistent
   state.
2. Transplant at the verify step can only test target and drafter caches as
   they are read by the verify forward; the drafts were produced one step
   earlier. To test the drafter itself, act at the prefill step
   (`scheduled_tokens: 385`, same prompt digest): observe there, then
   transplant only layers 40..42 caches into the first original before its
   prefill. If that alone restores the normal drafts and logprobs, the drafter
   state is causal.
3. Cheap, requests only: meadow twice, then the original 385 twice with
   `prompt_logprobs: 1`. That keeps every prefill row in the CED decoder
   (`keep_full`), so decoder layers 21..39 and any drafter context taken from
   their rows cover the whole prompt. If first and repeat then agree, the
   history dependence lives in state that CED compaction leaves unwritten; if
   they still differ, CED is not the carrier. It changes numerics for both
   runs equally, so only first-versus-repeat is compared, not the old hashes.

### Probe geometry audit after the UniformTypeKVCacheSpecs failure (2026-09-25)

Read-only, against vLLM 1794dcf1 and the in-progress helper (sha 6bd03a99...,
per-layer spec from `UniformTypeKVCacheSpecs.kv_cache_specs[name]`). The
failed capture did prove the rest of the path: arming, the control, the
prompt-digest match and the verify-step trigger all worked; it stopped in
`layers()`.

Remaining defects, each able to crash the next boot the same way or to
capture the wrong bytes:
1. Page padding. `unify_kv_cache_spec_page_size` (kv_cache_utils.py 1436-1470)
   gives smaller-page non-MLA attention specs `page_size_padded`, and
   `SlidingWindowMLASpec` and `CircularBufferSpec` are not MLAAttentionSpec, so
   the SWA and ring layers can be padded. `page_size_bytes` then returns the
   padded size. The bound cache row holds only the unpadded `H * N * C` bytes
   (`create_kv_cache_views` builds `[B, H, N, C]` with C =
   `state_content_size_bytes`; padding lives in the block stride), so
   `record_layout` would raise ("block row has X bytes, payload needs Y") or,
   if it passed, compute a wrong record. Use C = `state_content_size_bytes`,
   require `num_heads == 1`, never `page_size_bytes`.
2. States per table block. The same unification can multiply a layer's
   manager `block_size`, and `create_metadata_builders` (worker/utils.py 271)
   builds every builder from `copy_with_new_block_size(kernel_block_size)`
   (or `storage_block_size` for MLA), with block tables in kernel-block units
   (`BlockTables.blocks_per_kv_block`). The DS4.1 builder's page is that
   kernel spec's `num_states`, published as `metadata.block_size`. The helper's
   `spec.num_states` is the manager value, which differs whenever a block is
   split. Take the page from `metadata[layer].block_size` (the value `_tokens`
   and `_chunk` use), and require the bound row to equal `page * C` exactly.
3. Block table rows. Take the row from `metadata[layer].block_table[0]`, which
   is the table the kernels read; keep `block_tables[group][0]` only as a
   recorded cross-check. A layer without metadata must be a named omission.

Unchanged and correct against source: circular detection must use the
per-layer spec (the fix does); ratio is `tokens_per_state` of the per-layer
spec, which the builder also uses; the DS4.1 `bind_kv_cache` view
`cache.view(B, -1)` of a `[B, 1, N, C]` BLHNC layer view is exactly one row
of N records.

Suggested real-geometry torch regression (CPU, in the image): build specs
with vLLM's own `unify_kv_cache_spec_page_size` so a SWA spec is padded and an
index-key spec has its block size scaled, allocate views with
`create_kv_cache_views` (BLHNC, shared layer stride) and a kernel block
smaller than the manager block, bind them the DS4.1 way, give the helper a
fake metadata object carrying the kernel `block_size` and a kernel-unit
table, write a distinct byte pattern into `view[b, 0, n, :]`, and assert the
helper reads and writes exactly those records.

To avoid paying a boot for the next geometry surprise: a non-raising
inventory action (log per layer: spec type, H, N, C, ratio, circular,
metadata page, row bytes, table width) run on the first matched step before
any observe action would validate geometry in the same boot without killing
the engine.

### Review of the repaired probe (helper sha 1740a345..., torch tests sha 9163e1b1...) (2026-09-25)

The three geometry repairs are right against source: record = per-layer
`state_content_size_bytes`, `num_heads == 1`, page from the layer's own
metadata (`block_size`, the kernel page `_tokens` uses) with the bound row
required to equal page * record exactly, table row from that metadata and the
group row kept for comparison. The new real-geometry test genuinely exercises
vLLM's unification (a padded SWA page with a shared layer stride, and a scaled
manager block split into kernel blocks through `create_kv_cache_views`), and
the metadata table deliberately differs from the group table, so it proves
which one the helper uses.

Remaining integration problems:
1. The layout `try` catches only ValueError and RuntimeError. A metadata object
   without `block_size` or `block_table`, or a table that is not 2-D, raises
   AttributeError, TypeError or IndexError and escapes classification, killing
   the engine even under a non-raising observe. Catch those and record a
   `metadata:` status.
2. No host-side bounds check before device indexing. Block ids come from the
   table and are used directly in `view[blocks, offsets]`; an id at or beyond
   `view.shape[0]` triggers a device-side assert, which poisons the CUDA
   context and cannot be caught. Check `0 < block < view.shape[0]` and
   `0 <= offset < page` for every slot on the host before any gather or write.
   This is what makes a non-raising observe actually safe.
3. Write modes validate and write layer by layer (`apply_write`), so a layout
   or source mismatch on a later layer raises after earlier layers were
   modified. The engine then dies, so nothing leaks into a response, but a
   two-phase write (plan, bound-check and validate every layer and every
   source row first, then write) removes partial mutation entirely.
4. Not covered by the real-geometry test: a real `CircularBufferSpec`
   (float32, head 1024, `head_size_v` 0, record 4096, block 16) through
   `create_kv_cache_views`, and an MLA spec with `storage_block_size`. Both
   are cheap to add alongside the existing test.

Non-raising observe design (write modes stay fail closed):
- Split `layers()` into an inventory that never raises (per layer: spec type,
  heads, record, ratio, circular, metadata page, row bytes, table width,
  status; plus the group/table count result) and a requirement step
  (classification and coverage) that raises.
- Observe: take the inventory, then run requirement, bounds checks, gathers
  and `torch.save` inside `try/except Exception`. On failure write
  `<run>-<node>.inventory.json` with the exception text and print a distinct
  line, for example `[DS41-STALE-PROBE] observe-failed run=<run> reason=...`,
  and return normally. Observe only reads device memory into new tensors and
  writes a host file, so a Python-level failure leaves engine state unchanged;
  item 2 is required so the failure is a Python exception, not a device
  assert. Write the inventory JSON on success too.
- Control loading: an unreadable or invalid control should log
  `control-rejected` and leave the probe disabled instead of raising, since no
  action has run yet.
- Transplant and poison keep raising, after the two-phase validation in item 3.
- Driver: treat `observe-failed` explicitly as a failure (stop and disarm).
  The current generic check only catches it if the line contains "Error".

### Review of the probe safety edits (helper sha be3cfa8c..., torch tests ea39edb7..., driver 8cafe1ee...) (2026-09-25)

Correct and sufficient for the stated goals:
- `_slot_index` bounds every (block, offset) on the host before building index
  tensors: block 0 (null) and blocks at or past the view are refused, offsets
  must be inside the kernel page, and the page must equal the view's page
  axis. Gathers and writes both go through it.
- Writes are two-phase: every layer is planned, bounds-checked and (for
  transplant) layout-, source-row- and consistency-validated, replacement rows
  are materialised, and the zero-write check runs, before the first
  assignment. A failure in planning leaves the caches untouched; write modes
  still raise.
- Observe failures are caught after the synchronise, report `observe-failed`
  and an inventory JSON, and the driver treats `observe-failed` and
  `control-rejected` as failures and disarms.

Nothing found that would invalidate a successful capture or allow an unsafe
write. Four small gaps:
1. `re.error` (a bad `layer_regex` or `allow_omitted`) is not caught by
   `_load_control` (it derives from Exception, not ValueError, on the image's
   Python 3.12), so an invalid regex still kills serving. The driver validates
   controls offline, so this is only the safety net, but it should be caught.
2. The observe catch-all also swallows device errors that surface in
   `.cpu()` (an asynchronous fault from an earlier serving kernel, or a
   `torch.AcceleratorError`). The engine will still die at its next CUDA call,
   but the log will blame the diagnostic. Re-raise device errors
   (AcceleratorError, or a message containing "CUDA error").
3. `last_inventory` is instance state set part-way through `layers()`. If a
   later observe fails before that assignment, the failure JSON carries the
   previous action's inventory under the new run name. Reset it at the start
   of each action. The inventory holds names and statuses only; adding the
   per-layer geometry (page, record, row bytes, ratio, circular) would make a
   failure diagnosable without another boot.
4. `torch.save` writes the final path directly, so a failure mid-write leaves a
   truncated `.pt` beside an `observe-failed` marker. The driver never diffs
   after a failure, but a manual diff could pick it up; write to a temporary
   name and rename.

### Capture r2: two matches per request are the async look-ahead step (2026-09-25)

Evidence (`stale-capture-r2-observed-20260925/dusty-container.log`, report in
`stale-capture-r2-20260925`): `original-0` counted exactly 1 draft, 7 draft
tokens and 3 generated tokens, yet two `observe-failed` lines fired for it, the
second after the request's HTTP 200. Both failed only on the default ring
coverage.

Mechanism (source, vLLM 1794dcf1). `AsyncScheduler._update_after_schedule`
gives every non-prefill request `num_sampled_tokens_per_step + spec` new
output placeholders and sets `request.spec_token_ids` to 7 placeholders
right after each scheduling pass. With async scheduling the engine schedules
step N+1 while step N runs, so the sequence for one short request is:
prefill (385 tokens); the real verify (1 + 7 drafts, seq_len 393); then,
before the verify's output with end-of-sequence is processed, a look-ahead
8-token step for the same request (optimistic `num_computed_tokens` 393, so
seq_len 401). The early-stop guard in `schedule()` only skips a step that
would pass `max_tokens` (here 393 + 2 - 8 = 387 < 385 + 8), not end-of-sequence.
The look-ahead is already dispatched when the request finishes, so the worker
executes it and its output is dropped as stale, which is why the spec-decode
counters never see it. Both steps have one request, 8 scheduled tokens and
the same prompt, so the helper matches both, in order: verify first, look-ahead
second. The look-ahead also writes KV for positions 393..400 into the
request's tail block just before that block is freed and, being unhashed,
reused first by the next request; that is one concrete way the previous
request's content lands in a recycled tail block.

Recommended control and driver without rebuilding:
- `coverage` override for rings: `.compressor.state_cache` on layers 2, 8 and
  14 only (compressors exist only on KV source layers, and layer 20 has ratio 1,
  so no ring). Keep swa 0..42, main and indexer 2, 8, 14, 20.
- Two actions per original request, in order: `<stamp>-first-verify`,
  `<stamp>-first-lookahead`, `<stamp>-repeat-verify`, `<stamp>-repeat-lookahead`.
- After each original, poll the logs (bounded, for example 30 s) until both
  markers appear on every rank, and only then send the next request; a
  missing or extra marker fails closed, since it would shift every later
  action.
- Before diffing, confirm the snapshot `seq_len` values: 393 for the verify
  pair and 401 for the look-ahead pair (the diff prints them). The primary
  comparison is first-verify against repeat-verify; the look-ahead pair shows
  what the discarded step wrote.
- For later transplants, pair each transplant with an observe on the
  look-ahead, since a poison or transplant with nothing to write raises.
- A helper change (a `seq_len` or per-request match field) would make this
  independent of scheduling order; only worth a rebuild if the pairing ever
  proves unstable.

Also recorded: with `prompt_logprobs: 1` (CED keeps every prefill row) the
first-after-transition request still differs, so CED compaction is not what
carries the history.

### r4 capture: the ratio-2 compressor ring is never written (2026-09-25)

Evidence (`stale-capture-r4-20260925`, all four ranks identical): at the
verify step of both originals (seq_len 393) every captured layer is identical
except the compressor rings of layers 2, 8 and 14. Reading the dusty ring
bytes with the task-local CPU venv (read-only): in both snapshots ring slots
2..15 of layer 2 are all zero, slot 1 is partly zero, and slot 0 holds
finite values around 1e27 (as float32). A prefill that saved its last 16 rows
would have filled all 16 slots with ordinary projections (positions 369..384).
The nonzero region is 5808 bytes, which is 11 records of 528 bytes, the SWA
record size; this is consistent with leftovers from another cache group that
previously owned the same physical block, but that attribution is inferred.
`_pending_tags` is [384] in both snapshots with zero pending values.

Source chain (vLLM 1794dcf1):
1. `CircularBufferSpec.uses_slot_mapping` returns False (kv_cache_interface.py
   955). `GPUModelRunner` records it per group (model_runner.py 619-627), and
   `_compute_slot_mappings_kernel` stores PAD_ID (-1) for every token of a
   group with mapping disabled (block_table.py 383).
2. The DS4.1 metadata builder's `_tokens` treats a negative input slot as
   invalid (`valid = ... & (input_slot >= 0)`), so for the ring group no token
   is emitted and every output slot is -1.
3. `_save_partial_states` writes only `slot >= PAGE`, so it never writes the
   ring.
4. `_load_partial_state` does not use the slot mapping: at a step whose first
   position is odd it reads `(pos - 1) % 16` of the ring page taken from the
   block table and feeds it to `mla_compress` as the pending half of the pair.
   For a 385-token prompt the verify step loads slot 0 for position 384.
5. The ring block is a fresh allocation from the shared pool, not zeroed; its
   bytes are whatever the previous owner, possibly another group, left.

So the first decode step after an odd-length prefill compresses the last
prompt token's pair from memory the request never wrote. That matches
everything observed: exact prefill, verify-only change, dependence on the
previous request (block recycling), one-request memory, and sensitivity only
at some lengths (the garbage enters through per-channel gates; with large
negative gates the stale half gets near-zero weight and changes nothing). It
also means the "normal" repeat output is computed with stale bytes too; it is
only stable because the same allocation history repeats. Even prompts do not
load the ring at their first decode step, so the 128-at-startup divergence is
not explained by this and stays open, as does the rare 16K prefill outlier.

Transplant scope review: copying the repeat's ring slots into the first
original is the right causal test, but narrow it so it proves which bytes
matter:
- T1: layers 2 and 8, ring slot 0 only (select positions [384, 385); the
  helper writes the slot once). Prediction: the first original's signature
  becomes the repeat signature.
- T2: layer 14 slots 1 and 2 only (positions [385, 387)). Prediction: no
  change, since slot 1 and 2 are not read at pos 385 and the verify's own save
  never happens either.
- T3: poison slot 0 of layers 2 and 8 with byte 0 (all-zero pending state).
  Prediction: a third, stable signature independent of the predecessor.
T1 and T3 together make the ring slot causal; T2 is the negative control.
One singleton action per request keeps the look-ahead step from matching.

### Review of the intended circular `_tokens` fix (2026-09-25)

Causality is now established by the user's full-ring transplants (repeat rings
into the first original gave the repeat signature 2/2; first rings into a
stable original gave the first signature 2/2).

The intended fix matches the pinned contract. `KVCacheSpec.uses_slot_mapping`
documents that "specs that address their pages themselves (raw storage, ring
buffers) take no slot mapping row", and the sibling package
`vllm/models/deepseek_v41/compressor.py` already computes its own ring slots
(`_ring_slot_mapping_kernel`: block of the request's first table column times
capacity plus `pos % capacity`). Only `deepseek_v4_1/sparse_mla.py` `_tokens`
still gates ring tokens on the generic `InputSlots`, so for the ring group
every token is invalid and nothing is saved. The sibling kernel is a reference
for the slot formula but not for validity: it accepts every row below
`num_actual_tokens`, padding included, which the DS4.1 version must not copy.

Points to keep exact:
1. Gate only the circular branch: `if not CIRCULAR: valid &= input_slot >= 0`
   as a constexpr branch, so the noncircular specialisation compiles to the
   same code and stays bitwise identical.
2. Padding stays excluded without `InputSlots`: the runner fills
   `query_start_loc[num_reqs + 1:]` with the actual token total (padded
   requests have zero length and padded `seq_lens` are 0), so a padded token
   has `t >= Starts[num_reqs]`, the binary search returns `lo == nr`, and
   `lo < nr` fails; `pos >= 0` is a second guard. Keep both.
3. Keep `block > 0` (unallocated or capture-time zeroed tables write nothing),
   `page_col = 0`, `(pos + 1) % RATIO == 0` and the last-PAGE writer filter per
   request (`t >= end - PAGE`).
4. With the fix, the ring metadata's `Positions`, `Reqs` and `Visible` become
   valid too. The compressor reads only the block table, `query_start_loc`,
   `request_positions`, `live_counts` and `slot_mapping` of that metadata, so
   this is harmless, but no other consumer should rely on them being -1.
5. Speculative and async steps: the verify writes V..V+7 into ring slots, and
   the discarded async look-ahead writes V+8..V+15. The next real step starts
   at V+a (a <= 8) and reads V+a-1, which the look-ahead cannot have
   overwritten modulo 16. This disjointness needs capacity >= 2 * (1 + drafts):
   16 for 7 drafts holds, but the capacity formula
   `max(8, 1 << (drafts + 1).bit_length())` gives 16 for 8 drafts, where
   2 * 9 = 18 would let a look-ahead row overwrite a needed slot. Not this
   deployment, but worth an assertion.
6. Odd step starts after a prefix hit would read a fresh ring. Rings are not
   prefix-cacheable, but hits are block-aligned and every group block size is
   even, so the first step after a hit starts even. Assert the evenness rather
   than rely on it.
7. Metadata builders run eagerly and write persistent buffers that the FULL
   graphs read, so the fix needs no recapture; capture and dummy runs use
   zeroed tables and write nothing.

Regression coverage (GPU, real kernels):
1. `_tokens` kernel: randomised ragged batches (query lengths 1, 8, 15, 16,
   17, 385, 8192) with FULL-graph padding (padded tokens, zero-length padded
   requests, zero padded seq_lens), a null ring block, and `InputSlots` all -1
   for the circular case: expect `block * 16 + pos % 16` on exactly the last
   `min(q, 16)` rows of each request and -1 elsewhere. For the noncircular
   case, compare every output tensor bitwise with the pinned kernel, including
   inputs with -1 slots.
2. Split-versus-whole equivalence: compress a prompt as an odd-length prefill
   L plus a decode step, and as a single prefill of L + 1; the pair
   (L - 1, L) latent must match. Repeat with an 8-row verify and partial
   acceptance, and with an odd chunked-prefill boundary.
3. Stale-memory independence: fill the ring pool with NaN or random bytes
   before the request and with zeros in a second run; outputs must be
   bitwise equal. This is the direct test of the bug class, and the stale
   probe's poison mode can do it live on the ring block.
4. Async look-ahead: EOS inside a verify and a partial acceptance with async
   scheduling on, checking the next step's pending load equals the row the
   verify wrote.
5. End to end on the rebuilt image: the content-transition cycle at 385, its
   prompt-logprobs variant, the ten-length predecessor map, odd lengths 129,
   257 and 513 with even controls, then the repeatability sweep and the
   original 524K replay. All first-after-transition requests must match their
   repeats.

Still open and not addressed by this fix: the even-length 128 divergence on
the first request after startup, and the rare 16K prefill outlier.

### Review of the ring-fix packaging and regression (2026-09-25)

Files: `prepare_ring_fix.py`, `install_ring_fix.py`, `Dockerfile.ring-fix`,
`build_ring_fix.py`, `run_ring_regression.py`, `test_compressor_ring_mapping.py`.
