- **Digest.** Two int64 lanes per tensor: the sum of raw 32-bit words, and
  a position-weighted sum with weights `(i mod 65521) + 1`, with
  wraparound. It matches a pure-Python reference, is independent of
  reduction order and layout, and a single-bit change moves lane 0. It is
  computed in chunks of 16 x 65521 words with one fixed 8 MiB weight
  buffer and about 16 MiB of temporaries per chunk. The chunk count is
  known on the host.
- **No host sync.** Digests go into a preallocated device slot. At step end
  a side stream waits on an event and copies the slot to pinned memory,
  and a writer thread appends JSONL to
  `/cache/ds41-act-trace/<node>-<pid>.jsonl`. With all 8 slots busy the
  step is dropped and counted; the forward never waits. `seq` counts every
  eligible step, dropped ones included, so it aligns across ranks. GPU
  step time comes from events and is read only by the writer.
- **Analyzer.** It groups by rank and step key; the modal vector is the
  reference, and fewer than 3 records or no majority means undecided. It
  reports the first differing module per divergent rank, aligned by
  `seq`: divergent ranks, clean ranks, earliest module, and GPU ms against
  the clean median and p90. It also reports structure differences.

**Tested invariants:**

- **Digest.** Reference equality across chunk boundaries and dtypes
  (bf16, fp32, int32, bool, odd bytes, empty); bit-flip and permutation
  sensitivity; stride independence; bounded weight buffer.
- **Hook lifecycle.** Nothing registered when disarmed; call-order records;
  hooks removed after disarm.
- **Filtering and determinism.** Row window and pattern selection;
  identical steps give identical records.
- **Skips and drops.** Capture and compile skip entirely without advancing
  `seq`; busy slots drop without blocking and advance `seq`; overflow is
  flagged; invalid triggers are ignored.
- **No host sync.** An AST check confirms no item, cpu, tolist, numpy,
  synchronize, elapsed_time or query call on the forward path.
- **End to end.** A one-layer, one-rank perturbation in a toy model is
  localized as rank [2], clean [0, 1, 3], earliest `layers.2.ffn`.
- **Packaging.** The patch adds exactly the intended lines, the lock and
  Dockerfile are consistent, and a simulated install is confined.

**Perturbation, to publish with any result:**

- About six small kernels per hooked tensor per 1M-word chunk.
- For 40 layers x (5 + attn + ffn) + 2 Engram + wo_b inputs, that is on the
  order of 1,500 to 2,000 extra launches per traced prefill, plus Python
  hook time.
- It changes timing and therefore possibly the race rate. It changes no
  operand, output or stream dependency, and adds no host synchronization.

**How to use it on a diagnostic boot:**

1. **Verify coverage.** Check on the first armed step that the names
   include `>layers.N.attn.wo_b:0`. If they do not, the b12x path does not
   call `wo_b` as a module, and rank-local attention coverage must come
   from other patterns.
2. **Arm.** Set `min_tokens=100` and `max_tokens=1100` and run the
   interleaved map. Alternate trigger-present and trigger-absent blocks of
   about 50 requests to measure how tracing changes the divergence rate.
3. **If the rate collapses under tracing,** narrow the patterns to
   checkpoints: layers 0, 1, 2, 8, 14, 19, 20 and 39, plus Engram 1 and 14.
   Then refine around the first divergence.
4. **Collect enough samples.** At about 1 percent per request, 1,000
   traced prefills give about 10 events.
5. **Join to responses.** Match the analyzer's divergent steps to probe
   receipts by `wall` time.

### Review: probe_interleaved_repeatability.py

**Sound:**

- Seeded per-cycle random permutations, recorded in the manifest.
- The unchanged probe, with full receipts.
- A signature over message, finish_reason and full logprobs.
- Per-request server timings and an unexplained-TTFT flag.
- An abort on any correctness failure.

**Limits:**

- **Low statistical power.** With 50 cycles at about 1 to 3 percent, the
  expectation is 0.5 to 1.5 events per length, too few to compare rates
  across lengths. Report a pooled rate with confidence bounds, and test
  for time clustering (runs within slow windows) rather than per-length
  rates.
- **Low load.** Requests are sequential, with idle gaps between them, so
  the driver cannot provoke the slow windows. Pair it with the
  timing-perturbation arm.
- **Mode needs repetition.** With few trials a "modal" signature needs at
  least 3 repeats per length before a non-modal count is meaningful. The
  summary reports counts only, so read it that way.

### Activation-kit revision 2 (2026-09-25, after user review)

The lock is now 4d45a44abec8b042...; tests: 29 CPU-torch, passing. The
patched model.py is unchanged (1051b15f...).

**User evidence.** The interleaved map finished with 7 non-modal results in
1100 (0.64 percent; exact 95 percent interval about 0.26 to 1.3 percent),
one each at 248, 250, 254, 257, 258, 271 and 1023. All answers were
correct. This is a general low-rate prefill race.

**Changes:**

1. **Arming identity.** The trigger now requires an `arm` token, written
   identically on every node. Every record carries arm, a per-process
   session id, and the target model's `_engram_epoch` at the start of the
   step. A new arm restarts `seq`.
2. **Analyzer.**
   - It refuses mixed arms unless `--arm` is given, and refuses more than
     one session per rank.
   - It joins ranks on the epoch, not `seq`: a test with a one-step
     arming skew on one rank still aligns all steps.
   - A step counts only if every expected rank (`--ranks`) has exactly one
     record and all step keys agree. Incomplete, duplicate, misaligned and
     no-epoch steps are counted, never used.
   - Records that overflowed, or that hold only `<input>`, `<positions>`
     or `<output>` digests, are never coverage.
   - The verdict is no-coverage, no-divergence-observed or
     divergence-localized.
3. **Sparse defaults.** Checkpoints are the outputs of layers 0, 1, 2, 8,
   14, 19, 20 and 39, plus Engram 1 and 14, plus a `<output>` digest of the
   model's return value. There are no pre-hooks. A `name:k` suffix digests
   only tuple member k. Estimated cost: about 250 to 300 extra launches at
   255 rows and 500 to 700 at 1100 rows, against 1,500 to 2,000 for broad
   patterns.
4. **One tracer per process.** A second DeepseekV4Model is refused and
   logged. At 1794dcf1 the DSpark draft does not build one.

**Call path (source- and log-verified):**

- **Eager prefill.** vLLM logs "`torch.compile` is turned on, but the
  model ... does not support it", so the DS4.1 target runs eagerly.
  Piecewise compilation does not apply, and FULL graphs cover only sizes
  up to 32 tokens; traced prefill steps are larger, so they are eager.
- **Hooks that fire.** The causal-LM wrapper calls DeepseekV4Model
  through `Module.__call__` (the stack dumps show `_call_impl` frames).
  Layers are called through `layer(...)`, and attention, FFN and Engram
  through `self.attn(...)`, `self.ffn(...)` and `self.engram(...)`.
- **Hooks that never fire.** `attn.wo_a` and `attn.wo_b` are never called
  as modules: `setup_wo_projection` packs their weights into a fused WO
  kernel, and there is no `self.wo_b(` call. Hooks on them never fire.

**Blockers and limits before a diagnostic boot:**

- **B1, rank attribution.** No module hook sees a rank-local tensor before
  a tensor-parallel reduction: WO is fused, and attention and MoE
  internals are B12X custom ops. Layer localization works. Rank
  attribution is possible only if a checkpoint shows divergence on a
  subset of ranks. If all ranks diverge at the same checkpoint, the origin
  rank needs instrumentation inside B12X ops, which is out of this kit's
  scope.
- **B2, epoch source.** The join depends on `_engram_epoch` advancing every
  step. That holds with Engram overlap on (the current profile). With
  overlap off every step would be a duplicate, giving no-coverage: safe,
  but useless.
- **B3, not verified on GPU.** The CUDA transport (events, pinned copy,
  side stream, writer thread), the real hook names and counts, and the
  effect on the divergence rate. On the first armed step, check that the
  names include `layers.N:0` to `:4` for each checkpoint,
  `layers.1.engram`, `layers.14.engram` and `<output>:0`, and that the
  analyzer reports covered steps.
- **B4, operational.** Arm only after readiness, with the same token on
  all four nodes. Alternate traced and untraced blocks of about 50
  requests to measure the tracing effect on the rate. At 0.64 percent, a
  handful of events needs 1,000 to 1,500 traced prefills.

## Review: probe_activation_localization.py, its test, and the qualification envelope (2026-09-25, read-only)

Context (user): diagnostic image 0b5c65a8 was built, and a GB10 transport
gate passed: 40 asynchronous records by 5 tensor cases, including M255,
M256 and non-contiguous tensors, all matching CPU digests through slot
reuse.

**Correction to my own note.** `DeepseekV4Model.forward` returns a plain
tensor, `hidden_states` after the HC collapse and final norm. It returns a
tuple only when aux hidden-state layers exist. The tracer names a plain
tensor output `<output>`, not `<output>:0`. My revision-2 note saying
"`<output>:0`" was wrong.

### Blockers (fix before the live run)

1. **The coverage gate will reject a correct boot.** `check_coverage`
   requires `<output>:0`; with the plain-tensor return the actual name is
   `<output>`. Accept either `<output>` or `<output>:0`, or better, assert
   the exact observed set against the source-derived expectation.
2. **Records can be collected before they are written.** `collect()` scps
   the JSONL right after the block. The writer thread appends after each
   step's side-stream copy completes, so the last step's records can be
   missing and show up as incomplete. Gate each traced block on
   completeness before analysis: every rank has exactly
   cycles x lengths new records for the arm within the block's epoch
   range, with a short bounded retry.
3. **Dropped steps are not checked.** Neither the probe nor its test
   rejects records with `dropped_before > 0` or `overflow`. A dropped step
   is a missing observation, not a clean one. Require `dropped_before == 0`
   on the last record of each rank, and no overflow.

### Findings (non-blocking)

- **Analysis is cumulative.** Every traced block reuses one arm token and
  copies the whole per-process JSONL, so each block's analysis also
  includes earlier traced blocks. That is correct pooling, but the
  per-block labels overstate independence. Either use per-block arms
  (`arm-bNN`) with a final pooled analysis, or label the analyses
  cumulative.
- **Too few requests to compare rates.** Four blocks of 10 cycles x 5
  lengths is 200 requests: about 1.3 expected events at 0.64 percent. That
  verifies coverage but cannot compare traced and untraced rates. For the
  experiment, use many more blocks in randomized or ABBA order, since a
  plain T, U, T, U alternation is confounded with time drift, and pool
  about 1,000 or more requests per condition.
- **No join between response divergence and trace verdict.** This is the
  highest-yield missing step. For every non-modal response signature,
  find the traced step with matching rows and a wall time inside the
  request window, and report its verdict:
  - divergent at checkpoint X: localized;
  - covered and all modal, including `<output>`: the divergence is after
    the model body, in LM-head logits, TP logits gather,
    logprob/top-20 computation or sampling. That is outside every hook;
  - uncovered or missing: inconclusive.
- **Engram layer 14 is not bracketed.** Sparse checkpoints bracket Engram
  layer 1 exactly (`layers.0`, then `layers.1.engram`, then `layers.1`),
  but not Engram layer 14: there is no `layers.13`. Add `layers.13` to the
  trigger's `post` list. This is a trigger-content change only.
- **The test covers only `check_coverage`.** It should also cover the
  `<output>` naming, dropped and overflow rejection, the completeness
  count, and the disarm arm-match assertion.
- **Arming mechanics are sound.**
  - All nodes are armed while the endpoint is idle, so the first request
    sees armed ranks.
  - Re-scp changes the mtime, and the same arm keeps `seq`.
  - `disarm` asserts arm ownership and retains the trigger file.
  - The before and after identity check includes Env, Cmd, RestartCount
    and StartedAt.
  - A missing JSONL makes scp fail loudly, which is good; the message
    could name the likely cause (hooks never fired).

### qualify_engram_repair.py (identity envelope)

- **Sound.** Container identity is snapshotted before and after all gates
  in `finally`, comparing Id, Image, RestartCount, StartedAt, sorted Env,
  Cmd and Labels. Env is validated against `render(node)` plus the
  deterministic keys, and the prefetch override is rejected. The candidate
  kind excludes diagnostic images.
- **Note.** If a gate fails and the after snapshot also raises (a dead
  container), the snapshot error is raised with the gate error only as
  context. It is acceptable because the chain keeps both, but log the gate
  error first.
- **Not yet in this file.** The targeted residual-stress stage and the
  per-request TTFT, prefill and queue guard. The final sticky check still
  depends on fail-closed, which is unverified on GPU until the injection
  test runs.

### Higher-yield localization ideas

1. **Output-first triage (above).** One comparison decides model body
   versus post-model. If post-model, extend tracing to the causal-LM
   wrapper's logits path, keyed by the same epoch. The LM head and
   `logits_processor` sit outside DeepseekV4Model, so the current tracer
   cannot see them.
2. **Raise the event rate before tracing.** The events cluster in slow
   windows, so a timing-perturbation arm such as steady background
   traffic, if it raises the rate, gives more events per traced hour.
   Keep an untraced control at the same load.
3. **Prove the scheduler inputs were identical.** Record per-step batch
   composition (request count, chunk boundaries, spec tokens) so a
   divergent step can be checked for truly identical inputs. idle_snapshot
   makes this likely but does not prove it.
4. **Use the collective boundary.** When all ranks diverge together at a
   checkpoint but not at the previous one, the suspects narrow to the
   module between them, including its internal all-reduce. Divergence on
   a subset of ranks points to rank-local work.

## Layer-0 FFN source analysis for the first localized residual (2026-09-25, read-only)

**Evidence (user, traces-00).** Epoch 474, 254 rows. On all 4 ranks the
first divergence is `layers.0:0`, the FFN output. Layer-0 members 1 to 4
(residual, post_mix, res_mix, ffn_pre) equal their modal digests on every
rank, and all ranks carry the same anomalous member-0 digest. That is 50
aligned covered steps with 49 modal and 1 divergent; no drops, overflow
or missing records. GPU time was 244 ms against a clean median of 248 ms.

Scope of this conclusion: this event diverged in layer 0's FFN, before any
Engram layer. That does not exclude Engram for other events.

### Call path at 1794dcf1

1. **Model to experts.** The DS4.1 layer 0 `self.ffn` is
   `vllm.models.deepseek_v4.nvidia.model.DeepseekV4MoE`, built with a B12X
   gate (`B12xLinearMethod`, fp32 output). It is a hash-MoE layer when
   `num_hash_layers` > 0: expert ids come from `tid2eid[input_ids]`, and
   weights come from the gate scores. `forward` uses the fused path, not
   MegaMoE, which needs EP: `self.experts(hidden_states=h,
   router_logits=h, input_ids=ids)`.
2. **The experts runner (moe_runner.py `_forward_impl`).** The gate is held
   by the runner, applied as `router_logits, _ = self.gate(h)`, and the
   module call fires hooks (unless `_fse_fuse_gate` routes through
   `F.linear`).
3. **Shared experts on an aux stream.** Shared experts
   (`DeepseekV4MLP`, B12X linears, not reduced inside) use
   `SharedExpertsOrder.MULTI_STREAM_OVERLAPPED` whenever
   `rows <= VLLM_SHARED_EXPERTS_STREAM_TOKEN_THRESHOLD` (default 256) and
   `VLLM_DISABLE_SHARED_EXPERTS_STREAM` is unset. The sequence is:
   - `maybe_sync_shared_experts_stream`: the aux stream waits on main
     before the gate.
   - The gate runs on main.
   - `prepare_workspace` leases routed and shared scratch in one arena
     call ("disjoint by design").
   - Routing and routed experts (`routed_experts.forward_modular`, a method
     call, not hookable) run on main.
   - `_run_in_aux_stream`: the shared MLP runs on the aux stream under
     `use_preallocated_workspace(shared_workspace)`. Main then waits on the
     aux stream, and the runner combines and TP-reduces.
4. **Concurrency window.** The aux-stream shared MLP overlaps the
   main-stream gate, router and routed experts.
   - `shared_experts_input` is `hidden_states` itself, not a clone.
   - The gate's block32 linear gets `scratch=None` and falls back to
     `current_workspace_manager().get_simultaneous(...)`, the same manager
     that backs `prepare_workspace`'s arena.
5. **CED.** Layers from 20 onward keep `min(q, CED_WINDOW=128)` rows per
   request, so every prefill longer than 128 tokens runs decoder-layer MoE
   with at most 128 rows, inside the overlap window. For prompts of 256 or
   fewer, layers 0 to 19 are inside it too.

### Hypotheses (open; the race itself is not proven)

- **H1, cross-stream hazard in the shared/routed overlap** (the
  highest-prior class). Candidates:
  - (a) arena scratch overlap between the aux-stream shared MLP and any
    main-stream consumer of the workspace manager during the window, such
    as the gate's `get_simultaneous` fallback or extra scratch leased
    inside the routed kernel;
  - (b) an in-place write by the routed path into `hidden_states` while
    the aux stream still reads it as the shared input. Not verified: there
    is no `inplace` flag in the modular kernel at this pin, and the B12X
    routed kernel's output aliasing still needs checking.

  It fits the timing correlation and the 254-row event at layer 0. It also
  explains events at 257 to 1023 through the CED decoder layers.
  **Testable prediction:** events on prompts longer than 256 first diverge
  at `layers.20` or later; events on prompts of 256 or fewer can first
  diverge at any layer.
- **H2, intra-kernel nondeterminism in the routed B12X fused MoE** under
  `B12X_DYNAMIC_DETERMINISTIC_OUTPUT` ordered reduction, which has the
  earlier declaration-only dependency repair. This would not follow the
  256 threshold.
- **H3, a gate GEMM race.** B12X dense block32 at M=254 with the
  release-fence patch.
- **H4, TP all-reduce of the MoE output.** 254 x 4096 x 2 bytes is about
  2.08 MB, under 2 MiB, so this used the RoCEnante one-shot; the 1023 event
  used NCCL. Identical anomalous digests on all 4 ranks weakly disfavor an
  arrival-ordered reduction, which would tend to make ranks differ.

### Highest-yield next boundaries (trigger only, no rebuild)

Keep the sparse checkpoints and add:

```
"pre":  ["layers.0.ffn", "layers.20.ffn"]
"post": [..sparse.., "layers.0.ffn.gate", "layers.0.ffn.shared_experts",
         "layers.20.ffn.gate", "layers.20.ffn.shared_experts"]
```

- `pre layers.N.ffn` digests the exact FFN inputs (x and input_ids), which
  confirms identical input.
- `ffn.gate` gives the router logits: replicated, fp32, main stream.
- `ffn.shared_experts` gives the rank-local shared partial before
  reduction. Its hook runs on the aux stream when overlapped, so its digest
  kernels extend the aux work and perturb the window.
- The routed partial and the combine/reduce are not module-hookable. They
  are inferred by elimination: input, gate and shared all modal while the
  FFN output is divergent points to the routed experts, router or
  combine/reduce.
- Verify on the first armed step that these names appear. The gate would
  be missing if `_fse_fuse_gate` is on.

### Decisive discriminator arm (needs restart and the launch-contract env; user's decision)

`VLLM_DISABLE_SHARED_EXPERTS_STREAM=1`, or equivalently
`VLLM_SHARED_EXPERTS_STREAM_TOKEN_THRESHOLD=0`, forces NO_OVERLAP. The
baseline is 7 in 1100. Zero events in about 1,100 or more requests would
have probability about e^-7, roughly 0.1 percent, under an unchanged rate,
which makes this far more decisive per GPU-hour than more hooks. Pair it
with an untraced control on the same image. If H1 survives, the B12X-level
follow-up is to prove arena-view disjointness (the gate's fallback scratch
against `shared_workspace` offsets) and in-place writes, in source.

## Shared-expert workspace hypothesis: source proof result (2026-09-25, read-only)

Verdict: not supported. The frozen sources give no arena alias, no lifetime
gap and no write to the uncloned shared input inside the shared/routed
overlap window. No code fix is proposed for this mechanism, and disabling
the overlap is not proposed as a fix.

Frozen inputs: vLLM 1794dcf1 plus the runtime overlay (PR865 patch touches
only qwen4_exp, cudagraph_utils and input_batch), the ring fix (sparse_mla)
and the Engram repair (engram, model, model_state, async_utils,
model_runner). moe_runner.py, shared_experts.py, modular_kernel.py,
workspace.py, b12x.py and deepseek_v4_1/b12x_layers.py are therefore pristine
1794dcf1. B12X a7d7d29b plus the MoE declaration patch, tiled top-k selector,
dense fence and native PLE batch. Config blob 09917a91 (matches the pinned
manifest): hidden 5120, 40 layers, 384 experts, top-6, moe_intermediate 2304,
one shared expert (576 per rank at TP4), no hash layers, sliding_window 128.
Launch: capture sizes 1..32, so every prefill here runs eager; the serving
profile forces B12X_DENSE_SPLITK_TURBO=0 and deterministic MoE output 1.

Order of work in one MoE layer with rows M <= 256 (moe_runner.py 905-975,
612-700; shared_experts.py 166-236):

1. main: `maybe_sync_shared_experts_stream` records the input on aux and
   makes aux wait on main (181). Every earlier arena user on main (attention,
   mHC post_pre at offset 0) is complete before any aux kernel runs.
2. main: gate `B12xLinearMethod.apply` -> `bf16_gemv` (no arena).
3. main: `prepare_workspace` -> `_allocate_buffers` -> ONE
   `get_simultaneous` call (modular_kernel.py 1199).
4. main: `select_experts`, `_prepare` (unquantized pass-through), B12X routed
   run with scratch=workspace2, a=hidden_states, output=fused_out, then the
   no-op finalize.
5. aux (enqueued after 4 on the host, runnable since 1): shared MLP under
   `use_preallocated_workspace(shared_ws)`: gate_up block32, SiluAndMulWithClamp
   (caching allocator, aux pool), down block32 with reduce_results=False (no
   collective on aux).
6. main waits aux (192) before `shared + routed` and the single all-reduce.

Exact lease (get_simultaneous: consecutive views, 256-byte aligned, one
slot per (ubatch, lane), locked in serving, growth synchronizes the device):

| rows | [0, a) fused_out bf16 | [a, a+W2) routed tp_moe.scratch | shared_ws |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 254 (layers 0-19) | a = 2,600,960 | W2 = plan bytes at capacity 8192 | 1,392,640 at a + round256(W2) |
| 255 | 2,611,200 | same W2 | 1,392,640 |
| 256 | 2,621,440 | same W2 | 1,392,640 |
| 128 (CED layers 20-39) | 1,310,720 | same W2 | 696,320 |

workspace13 is (0,), so buffer 0 is fused_out alone. shared_ws is the larger
block32 scratch of the 256 (or 128) bucket: gate_up 5120->1152 needs
1,392,640 (696,320 at 128) and down 576->5120 (K padded to 640) needs
174,080 (88,064), plus split_k*m*n*4 only if a split-K winner is selected
(all observed prefill winners are split 1). W2 was not computed offline (it
needs the B12X planner on a device); disjointness does not depend on it.
`VLLM_DEBUG_WORKSPACE=1` would log it.

Why nothing aliases:

- Gate: bf16_gemv, no arena.
- Routed: reads `a`, writes only fused_out and workspace2. No Python-level
  in-place op on `a`. Its per-call state lives inside workspace2 and is
  initialized every call: the dynamic kernel's phase 0 zeros counters and
  queues, and the barrier scalars are zeroed by the wrapper
  (volatile_launch_state=True on the eager vLLM bind, _impl.py 12752/12791)
  or by `_dynamic_route_plan_kernel` (stores 0 first) on the external route
  plan branch. So reuse of the same bytes by attention or mHC between layers
  is safe.
- Shared: `_block32_linear` takes the reserved view from the ContextVar
  (the MoE custom op is opaque to Dynamo, so is_compiling() is False at run
  time) and only falls back to the manager when none is set. block_fp8 bind
  places the MXFP8 rows for the live M inside the bucket layout and the
  split-K fp32 workspace after the full bucket layout, all inside the spec.
- The shared input is not cloned, but no main-stream op writes it in the
  window, and record_stream keeps it alive for aux.
- Collective: none on aux. Engram's side stream writes only Engram rows.
  DS4.1 attention has no aux streams (aux_stream_list=None).

Residual unaudited edge: kernel-level store bounds (a routed epilogue writing
padded M-tile rows past row M-1 would land in workspace2, never in
shared_ws, since W2 separates them).

Deterministic routed output and arrival order (dynamic.py 3600-3625,
4050-4075): the row inside an expert's tiles comes from an atomic arrival
counter, but under deterministic_output the token map stores pair_idx, and a
fixed-order top-k kernel does the reduction. GEMM rows are independent
(per-row MXFP8 scales), so arrival order changes placement, not values.
That is by construction only; `claude_probe_routed_moe.py` has still not run.

Collective per layer (hidden 5120, bf16, RoCE cap 2 MiB inclusive,
roce_oneshot.py 426-427):

| prompt rows M | layers 0-19 | layers 20-39 (CED, 128 rows) |
| --- | --- | --- |
| <= 128 | overlap, RoCE | overlap, RoCE |
| 129-204 | overlap, RoCE | overlap, RoCE |
| 205-256 | overlap, NCCL | overlap, RoCE |
| >= 257 | sequential shared, NCCL | overlap, RoCE |

Predictions that use existing traces. If the overlap were causal, a
divergence at M >= 257 could first appear only at layers >= 20. If NCCL
were causal, it could appear only at layers 0-19 for M >= 205, and never for
M <= 204. Both localized events (M254 layer 0, M256 after layer 0) are in
the overlap-plus-NCCL cell, so they do not separate the two.

Act-trace side-stream review (claude_act_trace.py): stream-correct. Hooks on
`ffn.shared_experts` run their digest on aux with aux-pool temporaries and
write only to their own acc row. Main waits on aux before the step-end event
that the copy stream waits on. `_WEIGHTS` is created on main by the
`<input>` digest before any aux use, and is never freed. The tracer never
writes model-visible memory. It does change timing (extra aux and main
kernels, host hook latency) and caching-allocator placement, so it can shift
how often a timing-dependent fault shows up, but it cannot create an alias.

## MoE seams: rank-local partials and collective input/output (2026-09-25; proposal, not built)

Correction to the previous section: `layers.N.ffn.experts.routed_experts` is
not a module-call boundary (the routed kernel runs inside the MoE custom op
via forward_modular), so no module hook can see it. The v1 kit ignored that
pattern silently.

Seams, all in MoERunner.forward at vLLM 1794dcf1 (moe_runner.py 723-830),
the only two callsites that bracket the rank-local partials and the
collective:

- `result = self._forward_entry(...)` (773). `_forward_entry` is an instance
  attribute set in `__init__` (304) to `torch.ops.vllm.moe_forward_shared`.
  It returns (shared_output, fused_output) after the op has made main wait
  for aux (shared_experts.py 192). fused_output is the arena view
  `workspace[2]`, the raw routed kernel output. The runner's
  routed_scaling_factor is 1.0 for DS4.1, because FusedMoEFactory passes the
  1.5 factor to the router unless apply_routed_scale_to_output is set.
- `result = self._maybe_reduce_final_output(result, ...)` (826). Its argument
  is the combined rank-local `shared + routed` input of the TP all-reduce.
  Its return value is the post-collective output. SP is off for DS4.1 and
  DeepseekV4MoE leaves reduce_results at True, so the only MoE collective is
  inside this call.

Implementation (claude-moe-seams-act_trace.py, installed as the v1 helper's
path; the v1 kit stays byte-identical): a new trigger key `"moe"` selects
runners by name pattern. While armed, `MoeSeams` shadows both callsites with
instance attributes that call the original objects with the original
arguments and return their results unchanged. They record NAME#shared,
NAME#routed, NAME#pre_reduce and NAME#post_reduce through the existing
`_record` (stream-ordered digest on the current stream, existing slot and
copy-stream transport, no host synchronization). Disarm restores the
original attributes by identity. Arming is refused, with every partial
wrapper rolled back, if any post, pre or moe pattern matches nothing or a
moe pattern names a module without both callsites. Records carry
`seam_runners`.

Scope: installed only by the DS4.1 target DeepseekV4Model (the existing
install call), only on selected `layers.N.ffn.experts` runners, only while
armed. The draft model is not traced. Unarmed runners run the pinned code
path with no extra Python.

Concurrency and lifetimes of the digests:

- All seam digests run on main after `_forward_entry` returns. The op's
  internal main-waits-aux precedes them, so #shared (aux-produced) is
  complete in stream order.
- #routed reads the arena view before the combine and before any later
  arena lease (next layer, same stream). Digest temporaries come from the
  caching allocator, not the arena.
- #pre_reduce is enqueued before the collective kernel and only reads its
  input. #post_reduce is enqueued after the collective, on the stream the
  collective used: RoCE and pynccl both run on the current stream
  (roce_oneshot.py 541-590, pynccl.py 288-297).
- Perturbation: at M254 each seam tensor is 2.6 MB, one chunk, about six
  kernels. That is about 960 extra launches for 40 layers. The combine and
  collective start later on each rank by the digest time, which changes
  cross-rank arrival timing at the collective. Compare armed and unarmed
  rates on the same boot, as with v1.

Reading (claude-moe-seams-act_analyze.py): `--require
'layers.*.ffn.experts#post_reduce=40'` is the coverage gate. Per divergent
rank, `differing_names` lists every changed name in call order. At the first
divergent layer:

- a rank whose #shared or #routed differs is a rank-local partial;
- a rank whose #pre_reduce differs while both partials are modal is the local
  combine (a plain bf16 add; would point at memory corruption);
- #post_reduce differing on ranks whose #pre_reduce is modal everywhere is
  the collective.

An all-rank divergence of `layers.13.ffn` (epoch 2955) fits both a single
rank's partial and a collective fault; the seams separate them.

Collective output lifetime (read-only review):

- vLLM layer: all-reduce is out of place. RoCE reads the input and writes
  the output inside one kernel on the current stream, with an aligned
  scratch copy only for unaligned pointers. pynccl uses the current stream
  and NCCL's stream-completion contract. The input (`shared + routed`, a
  fresh caching-allocator tensor) is freed after return and reused only by
  later same-stream work. No race found.
- RoCE transport (b12x/comm/roce/_roce_proxy.c 1-36, 380-470;
  _oneshot_cute.py):
  - each stripe's payload and its flag write go on the same reliable QP,
    flag after payload;
  - the kernel waits with a system-scope acquire on every stripe flag;
  - the two-slot send and receive reuse is causally safe: a rank reuses a
    slot two collectives later only after every peer consumed the earlier
    use, which required our payload to be delivered.
- Not audited: the payload load path after the acquire (relaxed
  system-scope loads, `ld_relaxed_sys_v4_u32` imported) under GB10's coherent
  host memory, and NCCL internals. At M254 layer 13 the transport is NCCL
  (2,600,960 B > 2 MiB).

Deterministic routed path: it still needs isolated testing. By construction
the output does not depend on arrival order (pair_idx token map,
fixed-order top-k sum, per-row MXFP8 scales). The persistent dynamic kernel,
however, publishes tasks across CTAs through atomics and counters
(task_ready, tile_write_count, producers_done_count, all_work_published,
grid barrier). That is the same bug class as the dense fence-before-release
defect already found in dense_gemm. A missing fence there would give rare,
scheduling-dependent wrong values, and would be most exposed when the aux
stream competes for SMs. Suggested isolated arm, for when a GPU is
authorized: `claude_probe_routed_moe.py` at M 128/254/256 with real top-k ids
(from a trace), bitwise repeats with scratch poison, with and without a
concurrent block32 GEMM loop on a second stream. In situ, a routed-first
seam finding at a layer would point here.

Files: claude-moe-seams-act_trace.py, claude-moe-seams-act_analyze.py,
claude_prepare_moe_seams.py, claude_install_moe_seams.py,
Dockerfile.claude-moe-seams (FROM 0b5c65a81b25), claude_test_moe_seams.py
(19 CPU tests), claude-moe-seams.patch (helper-only diff),
claude-moe-seams.lock.json (sha256 7afb601bd0d8...), claude-moe-seams.ignore.
The lock also pins the four runner-contract sources (moe_runner.py,
shared_experts.py, fused_moe/layer.py, deepseek_v4/nvidia/model.py) to the
runtime manifest hashes; the installer refuses an image that differs.

## Routed-MoE isolation probe: audit and rework (2026-09-25; CPU-checked, not run)

Audit of the previous `claude_probe_routed_moe.py` against B12X a7d7d29b and
vLLM 1794dcf1:

- Blocker: it opened `PreparationSession(autotune=False)` without
  cache_only. In this B12X that is warm-up-only
  (`_warmup_only = not cache_only and (not autotune or stopped)`), which
  assigns default selections without consulting the cache. Its "cached"
  gate would therefore have refused every run; it could never have
  exercised the frozen production plans.
- The session could also write selections into the boot's cache directory.
  The runner diffs tuning-before/after, but the probe must not rely on that.
- Scratch was bound as the variant's own specs. vLLM binds the composite
  plan with one flat workspace2 of 2*ceil(sum(plan.scratch_specs())/2) bytes
  (b12x.py 704-706, 758) inside the MoE lease, after fused_out.
- Side-stream load: operands were initialized on main and read on the side
  stream with no wait (a diagnostic hazard, now fixed).
- Every repeat was cloned and kept, and a stacked copy was built at the end:
  memory grew with the repeat count.
- It hard-required backend "dynamic". The production backend is whatever the
  boot cached, so it is now reported, not assumed.

Production resolution: `variant_for` uses the exact variant or else the
8192 capacity variant. DS4.1 fixed counts are capture sizes and planned
decode counts, all <= 32 (b12x_prepare.py 131-190; the target is not
compiled). So every 33..8192-row prefill, including 128/254/255/256, runs
the capacity variant. Each variant is keyed by its own MoeDecodeQuery
(num_tokens, geometry, deterministic_output, controls, ...), so an exact
cache hit also proves those env-dependent fields.

Reworked probe (same CLI entry as run_routed_isolation.py uses:
`--tuning-receipt <boot selection file>` plus pass-through args):

- Identity gates:
  - the file name must be the digest of its identity;
  - its identity must equal the live `cache_identity(namespace, device)`;
  - the file must sit in `_cute_compile_cache_dir()/preparation` (the same
    boot's compiled programs);
  - the namespace must be TP4.
- Session: autotune=False, cache_only=True, compile_workers=0, against a
  private temp copy of the file. A missing selection or compiled program
  raises LookupError and the probe exits REFUSED (2). Every variant must
  report "cached", and probed rows must resolve to the capacity variant.
  The `--allow-*` escape hatches are removed.
- Inputs: switches DETERMINISTIC_OUTPUT=1 and FORCE_A8=1 are required, and
  all B12X_* variables are printed. Top-k ids default to int32
  (fused_topk_bias no-EP default); int64 is optional.
- Layout: one arena per row count, with fused_out at 0 and workspace2 at
  round256(rows*10240).
- Repeats: the output is NaN-filled each repeat (unwritten rows fail).
  Optional `--poison` and `--side-stream-load`: the side operands are
  initialized on main, the side stream waits once and again after each
  repeat's fills, and the side matmuls are enqueued before the MoE launch.
  Comparison is incremental against repeat 0 with a bounded row mask. The
  first failing output, the reference and the inputs are saved once to
  `claude-routed-moe-first-failure-m<rows>.pt` in the working directory.
  `--stop-after N` is optional; `--routes-file` accepts captured routing.
- Defaults: rows 128,254,255,256, 1000 repeats.
- Limits: synthetic weights, hidden states and routing; isolated occupancy
  and timing differ from in-model; bitwise repeatability, not accuracy. A
  clean run does not exonerate the kernel in situ.

CPU checks: `claude_test_probe_routed_moe.py`, 13 tests covering layout,
capacity resolution, file-name/identity gate, comparator bounds, first-failure
retention, non-finite handling, side-stream ordering and strict-session AST,
plus pinned B12X/vLLM semantics read from the existing checkouts.

dynamic.py consumer_release inventory (a7d7d29b): nine sites (5493, 5559,
6938, 6941, 7092, 7095, 7256, 7351, 8153). None has a fence between the
last shared-memory fragment reads and the release, the same shape as the
dense defect repaired by `fence_acq_rel_cta()` before `consumer_release`
(dense-fence-before-release.patch). 8153 is under `if not self.is_w4a8`, so
it is off the production w4a8 path. Which of the others the production
dynamic W4A8 plan instantiates was not resolved. No causal claim. If the
baseline probe reproduces, a fenced diagnostic build of those sites is the
discriminating arm.

## Frozen routed-MoE specialization: which code executes (2026-09-25, source only, B12X a7d7d29b)

Frozen 8192 selection (user-reported): dynamic, internal route planner,
grouped, tile_m 16, max_active_clusters 48. DS4.1 geometry: w4a8_mx,
fp4_e8m0_k32, E=384, K=5120, n=576 per rank, top-6, silu, deterministic.

Derivation:

- `_preparation._dynamic_program_arguments` (455-527) sets the flags:
  - `prepared_w4a8` is True, so `w4a8_repacked` is True;
  - `n64_repacked` is True (fp4_e8m0_k32 and 576 % 128 == 64);
  - `direct_routing` is False (grouped);
  - `external_route_plan` is False (internal planner).
- `_impl` launch (11290-11440, 10760-10800):
  - `materialize_intermediate |= w4a8_n64_repacked`, and n64 also forces
    share_input;
  - `swap_ab` is forced False for any W4A8 recipe (impl 10796-10797);
  - tile (16,128) comes from planned_tile_m 16 (impl 1766-1774).
- dynamic.py constructor:
  - `w4a8_m16_materialized` (965) = repacked and n64 and materialize and
    (16,128), which is True;
  - so `w4a8_split_materialized` and `external_materialized_fc1/fc2`
    (981-1015) are True;
  - `w4a8_named_pipeline` (1298) and `w4a8_fused` (1572) are irrelevant here.

What runs, all on one stream, in order, with no programmatic dependent
launch anywhere in dynamic.py, w4a8_phase1.py, w4a8_phase2.py or impl.py:

1. dynamic.py front end: routing histogram, prefix, placement, shared input
   quantization and task metadata, separated by resident-grid barriers
   (3459, 3487, 3520). `consumer_live = 0` when external_materialized_fc1
   (5037-5039), so the whole in-kernel consumer loop (5040 onward) never
   iterates.
2. W4A8MaterializedPhase1Kernel (FC1 plus activation and per-row MXFP8
   requant).
3. W4A8MaterializedPhase2Kernel (FC2, deterministic single-producer
   scatter).
4. The fixed-order top-k sum (`_launch_dynamic_topk_sum`, impl 13299-13307).

consumer_release sites: none of the nine executes in this specialization.

| Site | Guard | Why inactive |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 5493, 5559 | `const_expr(swap_ab)` | swap_ab is False for W4A8 |
| 6938, 6941, 7092, 7095, 7256, 7351 | `(not swap_ab) and (not is_w4a8)` | is_w4a8 is True |
| 8153 | `not is_w4a8` | is_w4a8 is True |

All nine are also inside the never-entered consumer loop. A fenced
diagnostic build of them would be a no-op for this plan.

Cross-CTA and ordering review of the code that does run:

- Phase 1 and phase 2: static grid-stride task walk (task_slot += gdimz),
  no atomics, no mbarrier pipelines, no cross-CTA flags. Handoff between
  kernels is plain stream order. The cp.async ring (phase1 676-906, phase2
  531-697) does commit, wait_group(stages-1), fence.proxy.async and
  bar.sync before reading a stage, and bar.sync at the end of each
  iteration before that stage is restaged; the final wait_group(0), fence
  and bar.sync come before the epilogue aliases the ring. This is the
  canonical correct pattern. Padding rows gather token 0 (phase1 388-418)
  and are never stored: phase 2 stores are masked by `row < valid_rows`
  (phase2 718, 744), and the per-row requant by `tid < valid_rows`
  (phase1 1160).
- Deterministic placement (front end 3600-3625, 4050-4075): a row's slot
  within its expert comes from an atomic arrival counter, but token_map
  stores pair_idx.
  - Phase 1 decodes the token as pair_idx // topk (phase1 400-417).
  - Phase 2 writes scatter[pair_idx, col] exactly once with a plain store
    (phase2 726-737).
  - The top-k sum reduces in fixed order.
  - MMA rows are independent and activation scales are per row per K32, so
    placement changes the location, not the value.
  - Counts, tile bases and valid_rows are order-independent (integer
    atomics).
  - The top-k sum kernel body (w4a16/kernel.py) was not read beyond its
    compile entry.
- Resident-grid barrier (dynamic 1789-1810):
  - Arrival: bar.sync, membar.gl by every thread, a relaxed atom.add by the
    leader.
  - Last arriver: plain store count=0, then st.release.gpu of the epoch.
  - Waiters: ld.acquire.gpu spin, then bar.sync.
  - Formal gap: the last arriver has no acquire after its atom.add. Its only
    acquire, the old_epoch load, precedes the atom, and st.release is not an
    acquire. Cooperative groups fences after the wait on every leader.
  - Practical exposure looks nil here: the post-barrier reads (row_counts by
    CTA 0 after the histogram, expert_tile_base after the prefix) were not
    loaded earlier in the kernel. L1 is invalidated at kernel start and
    stores do not allocate in L1, so the last arriver's loads reach L2,
    where the counted atomics and fenced stores already landed.
  - Recorded as a formal PTX-model gap, not a suspect.
- Residency: the n64 M16 front end runs a 48-CTA resident grid (one per SM
  on GB10; impl 11355-11359). Concurrent aux-stream kernels can delay
  co-residency, which is a spin/latency effect with no value effect found.

Conclusion (no causal claim): no unfenced shared-memory release, no
order-dependent reduction and no cross-CTA publication without ordering was
found in the executed routed path. The remaining unread parts are the
top-k sum kernel body and the front end's shared-input quantization
details beyond the rendezvous structure.

## Remaining executed path and collectives: source review (2026-09-25, source only)

Scope: the rest of the frozen routed path (top-k reduction, shared-input
quantization), the per-layer collectives, and NCCL transport facts from
retained logs. Demonstrated defects: none. Hypotheses are listed separately
at the end.

### Routed path, remaining pieces (B12X a7d7d29b)

- Fixed-order top-k sum. The deterministic dynamic path calls
  `_launch_dynamic_topk_sum` with no weights or ids (weights were already
  applied in phase 2). It takes the plain branch of W4A16TopKSumKernel
  (w4a16/kernel.py 9016-9037):
  - one thread per output element;
  - routes 0..5 summed in fp32 in constexpr order through
    `_materialize_w4a16_topk_route_f32`;
  - one store per element.

  It reads route rows that phase 2 wrote in an earlier launch on the same
  stream. Deterministic by construction, with no shared memory and no
  cross-thread communication.
- Shared-input quantization (dynamic.py 3525-3830; topk 6 takes the
  non-8-route branch):
  - Token claims come from an atomic `pair_head` (arrival-order token to CTA
    assignment); values do not depend on that assignment.
  - Each token is handled by one warp pair. The two warps split the K32
    blocks by index parity, so each block is quantized and stored once, into
    token-indexed packed_a and scale storage (materialized specialization).
  - Route slots in shared memory are written by the lane-0 threads of the
    pair, read after the pair named barrier, and not rewritten until the next
    CTA-wide `sync_threads` at the top of the producer loop.
  - Row placement uses the atomic `expert_write_rows`; its values are
    order-independent as noted before.
- Front-end grid barrier: formal last-arriver acquire gap only (see the
  previous section); not a suspect.

Taken together, the executed routed path contains no order-dependent
reduction, no multi-producer location and no cross-CTA handoff without an
ordering edge that source reading can find. Kernel-internal store bounds and
hardware memory semantics are not established by reading.

### Per-layer collectives in DS4.1 (vLLM 1794dcf1)

- Two TP all-reduces per decoder layer of identical shape (rows x 5120
  bf16): attention output (`deepseek_v4_1/attention.py:1772`) and the MoE
  runner's final reduce. There is one more on Engram layers
  (`common/engram.py:902`). All-gathers are only sequence-parallel (off) or
  logits and vision; none is per layer.
- Transport per call: RoCE one-shot if bytes <= 2 MiB (M <= 204, and every
  CED layer 20+ at 128 rows), otherwise NCCL.
- Every localized first divergence so far (M254 layer 0, M254 layer 13,
  M256 around layer 3) sits where both all-reduces of layers 0-19 use NCCL.
  That is an observation about the sample, not an inference.

### RoCE one-shot (b12x/comm/roce/_oneshot_cute.py, _roce_proxy.c)

Protocol read end to end:

1. Blocks stage into the pinned send slot.
2. After bar.sync, each block's tid0 does fence.sc.sys and a relaxed atomic
   stage count; the last one writes the per-slot nbytes, fence.sc.sys, then
   the doorbell seq.
3. Flag threads spin with acquire.sys on every peer/HCA stripe flag, then
   bar.sync.
4. The reduce uses ld.relaxed.sys for peer slots in fixed rank order.
5. Tail count, fence.sc.gpu, then a release of the epoch.

Two-slot reuse is causally safe. The proxy registers its MR with LOCAL_WRITE
and REMOTE_WRITE only (no IBV_ACCESS_RELAXED_ORDERING), so on the wire each
stripe's payload precedes its flag on the same RC QP. Not established by
reading: the GB10 guarantee that a PCIe-written payload is visible to the
iGPU's system-scope loads once the flag is observed with acquire. This is a
platform property.

### NCCL 2.30.7 facts (source plus retained INIT logs, receipts/nccl-repeatability-stock/*.log)

- `NET/IB : Using [0]rocep1s0f0:1/RoCE [1]roceP2p1s0f0:1/RoCE [RO]`: PCIe
  relaxed ordering is ON for NCCL's MRs (`NCCL_IB_PCI_RELAXED_ORDERING`
  default 2, net_ib/init.cc 11, 141-150).
- `GPU Direct RDMA Disabled` for every HCA, `use ring PXN 0 GDR 0`: NCCL
  stages through host memory. So `ncclTopoNeedFlush` (paths.cc 575-597,
  FlushNone for cc >= 90) does not apply.
- LL128. The default gate is `typeInter <= PATH_PXN` (6) when
  LL128_C2C=1 and cc >= 90 (tuning.cc 517-534). GDR is disabled only when
  distance > netGdrLevel (P2C = 7 by default for C2C, or PXB = 5), so the
  GPU-to-NET path is P2C, PHB or SYS, all > PXN. LL128 is therefore
  disabled on this deployment, inferred from source plus the GDR log; a
  NCCL_DEBUG_SUBSYS=TUNING table would confirm it directly.
- 64 channels, rings and trees connected. Algorithm and protocol choice is
  a deterministic function of size, so it does not by itself create
  run-to-run variation.

### Hypotheses (not demonstrated; each with a discriminator that does not change numerics)

1. NCCL host-staged receive under relaxed ordering on GB10: the GPU reads
   a recv-slot line before the NIC's reordered data write to it is
   visible, or through a stale cached copy from the slot's previous use
   (NCCL_STEPS reuse). NCCL's ordering relies on the strictly ordered CQE
   plus host coherence. Discriminator: NCCL_IB_PCI_RELAXED_ORDERING=0 on
   one boot (identical math), untraced rate against baseline.
2. NCCL protocol-specific exposure (LL and Simple remain enabled; LL's
   16-byte flag lines vs Simple's tail handshake). Discriminator:
   NCCL_PROTO=Simple (math unchanged). NCCL_PROTO=LL,Simple, the GLM
   qualified contract, would not change anything here if LL128 is already
   off.
3. A timing-coupled fault anywhere in the layer (compute or transport),
   exposed by stream concurrency. Discriminator: the MoE seams (pre/post
   reduce, both partials) now distributed as image 14abc3e6. Note they
   bracket only the MoE collective; the attention all-reduce at
   attention.py:1772 is not seamed.

Priority, source-only view:

- The seam trace is the decisive split.
- If it shows #post_reduce first with #pre_reduce modal on every rank,
  hypotheses 1-2 are the next A/B: environment only, no rebuild, math
  unchanged. At the current ~0.5% untraced rate, about 1500 requests per
  arm separate "unchanged" from "gone".
- If it shows a rank-local partial first, the routed or shared kernels need
  in-situ capture, since the isolated arms (16,000 calls) were clean.

## Seam localization receipts: independent review and the next capture (2026-09-25)

Receipts: receipts/moe-seams-localization-20260925/ (analysis.json, raw
traces-00/*/*.jsonl, telemetry). Checks performed here, read-only.

Demonstrated by the raw records (arm localize-1790367871420031962, 500
aligned steps, 5 prompts x 100 at rows 254/256/258/271/1023):

- Three events, all first divergent on toby (rank 1) at
  `layers.{12,11,12}.ffn.experts#routed`; ranks 0/2/3 first differ at the
  same layer's #post_reduce.
- Supporting evidence, not direct input equality (corrected 2026-09-25 on
  user review): in each event the previous layer's #post_reduce and this
  layer's #shared are modal on toby. Attention and mHC run between the
  previous #post_reduce and this MoE input, and the shared-MLP mapping is
  not injective, so neither proves toby's current MoE input bytes were
  identical. The v3 seam #input (digest of the op's hidden_states) is the
  direct check. Every other rank's #routed at that layer is modal.
- Not bistable: epochs 906 and 1914 are the same 254-row prompt and give
  two different alternate #routed digests (toby layer 12: 98 modal and 2
  distinct singletons).
- Events occur only at 254 and 256 rows (3 of 200 steps), none at 258, 271
  or 1023 (0 of 300); only in layers 0-19, where shared and routed overlap
  when rows <= 256. Fisher one-sided p is about 0.06, so this is suggestive,
  not established.
- The same trace files hold the earlier session's arms. The two arms that
  hooked `ffn.gate` and `ffn.shared_experts` (digest kernels inside the
  overlap window) had 0 events in 1250 steps. The seam arm, whose digests
  all run after the op returns, had 3 of 500, close to the untraced rate. At
  the seam arm's rate, 0 of 1250 has probability of about 5e-4 if the rate
  were unchanged. This is an association between instrumentation inside the
  window and suppression, not a proof.
- Telemetry: MemAvailable about 0.6 to 1.3 GB on all four nodes, flat swap,
  zero allocstall/compact_stall/pgscan_direct over the run and within 10 s of
  each event. Memory reclaim is not implicated for these events.
- Earlier single-rank localizations (older images, other kernels) were
  rusty (160 rows), dusty (129) and toby (192, block-32 GEMM). Rank
  specificity is therefore not established across builds; 3 of 3 on toby
  here has chance probability 1/16 under a uniform-rank null.

Not demonstrated: that the routed kernel alone is at fault. The seams do not
record the router logits, top-k ids and weights, or whether the op wrote its
input.

Stream facts checked for this question: B12X launches every routed kernel on
the caller's current stream at run time (`b12x/_lib/utils.current_cuda_stream`
wraps `torch.cuda.current_stream()`; `_impl` 13052), so the routed sequence
is stream-ordered after the gate and router on main.

### Built (not deployed): v3 MoE capture layer, claude-moe-capture-*

Over seam image 14abc3e66a45; the v2 kit stays byte-identical. Lock sha256
f80f00a35742... Files:

- claude-moe-capture-act_trace.py (installed as the helper)
- claude-moe-capture-act_analyze.py
- claude_prepare_moe_capture.py and claude_install_moe_capture.py
- Dockerfile.claude-moe-capture
- claude_test_moe_capture.py (16 CPU tests)
- the .patch, .lock.json and .ignore files

The lock also pins fused_moe_router.py. The preparer checks that
`_forward_impl` calls `self.gate(hidden_states)` once, that
`_apply_quant_method` calls `self.router.select_experts` once, that the DS4
MoE hands `gate=self.gate` to the runner, and that
`FusedMoERouter.select_experts` is concrete.

- Seams per armed runner, in call order: #input (before the op), #logits,
  #topk_weights, #topk_ids, #input_after, #shared, #routed, #pre_reduce,
  #post_reduce.
  - Gate output and router selection are only referenced inside the window
    (a gate forward hook and a router instance wrapper, no kernels).
  - Their digests run after the op returns.
  - Disarm removes the hook and wrapper and restores identity.
  - The trigger is refused if a router is already wrapped.
- Capture ("capture": {"rows": [...], "moe": [...]}):
  - A per-process device latch: the first routed call whose digest differs
    from the (rows, runner) reference, and whose step has the reference's
    `<input>` token digest, copies the call's input, logits, top-k weights
    and ids and routed output into preallocated device buffers
    (torch.where on a device predicate, after the op returns).
  - A raw `<capture>` [latched, epoch] word in each record tells the writer
    thread. The next step start freezes the buffers and copies them on the
    transport stream to pinned memory, and the writer saves
    `<out_dir>/<node>-<pid>-capture.pt`, including the reference digests.
- Costs:
  - one-time device buffers (about 5.7 MB at 256 rows);
  - one-time pinned allocation at the first save (may stall the host once);
  - per selected runner and step, a few small kernels plus five
    torch.where copies after the op;
  - the #input digest before the op, and two extra 2.6 MB digests per
    armed runner.

### Recommended order

1. No build: run the existing isolated probe on toby (the synthetic arms
   ran on dusty), then on the other three nodes, with the same arms. A
   toby-only failure would point to a node or hardware property; four clean
   nodes keep the in-situ path open.
2. v3 capture, one untraced-equivalent block, trigger:

   ```
   {"arm": "...", "min_tokens": 100, "max_tokens": 1100, "post": ["layers.39"],
    "moe": ["layers.*.ffn.experts"],
    "capture": {"rows": [254, 256],
                "moe": ["layers.[0-9].ffn.experts", "layers.1[0-9].ffn.experts"]}}
   ```

   Decision table at a toby-style event:

   | Observation | Implication |
   | --- | --- |
   | #logits, #topk_ids, #topk_weights and #input_after modal, #routed differs | routed kernel sequence with identical real inputs |
   | #logits differs | gate |
   | ids or weights differ with modal logits | router |
   | #input_after differs from #input | something wrote the op's input |

   The saved capture gives the exact failing routed output plus its real
   inputs on the failing rank.
3. Only then, replay, not yet built. Once serving is stopped, load only the
   captured layer's expert shard for the captured rank (about 1.7 GB for one
   layer on one rank; no full-model load, nothing while serving). Run the
   frozen plan through the probe machinery with the captured real inputs,
   thousands of repeats, with and without the real block-32 shared-expert
   GEMM concurrently on a second stream, on toby and on another node. The
   modal output from replay against the captured failing output also gives
   the corruption pattern (which rows, 16-row tiles, N128 columns or
   experts), which localizes within the phase-1, phase-2 or top-k kernels.

Hypotheses kept open:

- (a) a timing-dependent fault in the routed kernel sequence exposed by
  aux-stream concurrency;
- (b) a node-specific hardware or firmware property of toby;
- (c) something outside the kernels corrupting its inputs or outputs during
  the window (v3 #input_after and the capture's inputs test this).

### v3 capture lifecycle tightening (2026-09-25, after user review; not built)

- Generations: every capture has a generation (incremented on each arm or
  trigger rewrite that configures capture); step records carry
  `capture: {generation, state}`. The writer marks a capture ready only when
  the record's arm, session and generation match the live capture and that
  capture is allocated. A latch word for any other generation is written as
  a `latched-discarded` status line and never readies the new capture.
- Explicit status: `type: capture` lines record fetching, saved (with path,
  epoch, rows, runner), error (with the failing stage) and discarded
  (disarmed while allocated or latched). Observe, fetch and save failures are
  caught, recorded as error and never raise into the forward. Files are
  named `<node>-<pid>-g<generation>-capture.pt`.
- Analyzer v3 reports `capture` per (rank, session, generation) and
  `capture_incomplete` for any generation with a latch word or error but no
  saved status, so a lost capture cannot read as no divergence.
- Real-CUDA gate: claude_gate_moe_capture_cuda.py (packaged in the image).
  It uses the real CudaTransport (device latch, side-stream pinned copies,
  writer thread, pinned capture fetch):
  - phase 1: injected fault at epoch 4; exact bytes of input, logits, top-k
    weights and ids and routed output; latch words 0,0,0,1; status fetching
    then saved;
  - phase 2: records held to model a delayed writer, a re-arm while a
    generation-2 latch is queued; generation 3 is not readied, generation 2
    shows discarded and latched-discarded with no file, then a
    generation-3 fault saves its own file.

  `--device cpu` runs the same assertions through SyncTransport. That mode
  passes locally; the CUDA mode has not been run (no GPU work authorized
  here).
- CPU tests: claude_test_moe_capture.py 21 tests (stale-generation delivery,
  readiness before allocation, status lines, save failure flagged by the
  analyzer, observe failure contained), 83 across the four suites.
  Lock sha256 4d62f99bcb71...

### Review of the primary's moe-capture wiring (2026-09-25, read-only, nothing changed)

Files read: probe_activation_localization.py, test_probe_activation_localization.py,
build_activation_trace.py, freeze_activation_trace.py,
distribute_activation_trace.py, start_moe_repaired.py, candidate.json,
runtime-files.json, moe-capture-02b046d3ac02-candidate-amendment.json.

Blocking finding: a latch late in a traced block cannot be saved before
`collect` runs, which then aborts the run and loses the capture.

- The helper freezes and fetches a ready capture only at the next model
  forward's `_begin`, while the trigger is still present. It becomes ready
  once the writer thread has processed the latched record.
- `collect` runs right after the block's last request. It drains records,
  analyzes, and raises on `capture_incomplete` (latch word seen, no `saved`
  status). With no further forward, the save never starts.
- The finally/untraced disarm then renames the trigger. The helper's next
  `_begin` discards the capture (`discarded`, later `latched-discarded`).
- Any latch in the block's last request, or in a request whose successor
  arrived before the writer delivered the latch, hits this.

Suggested fix, probe side, no rebuild: in `collect`, after the record drain
and before analysis, if any record of this arm carries a set `<capture>`
word, send one short prompt below min_tokens (for example 50 tokens,
max_tokens 1). `_begin` performs the freeze before the row-window check, so
the prompt adds no trace record and leaves the expected record counts
unchanged. Then poll the status lines for `saved` or `error` of that
generation within a deadline, and only then analyze. Keep raising if it
stays incomplete.

Optional helper hardening, needs a rebuild and review: on disarm, fetch an
allocated capture instead of discarding it (save only if the fetched latch
word is set). That alone does not fix `collect` ordering.

Other findings, non-blocking:

- On `capture_incomplete` the probe raises before downloading
  already-saved captures of other generations. Download and hash first,
  then raise.
- Each traced block re-copies the trigger (new mtime), so every traced
  block is a new capture generation. Untraced blocks log `discarded`
  (unlatched) for the previous one; the analyzer treats that as complete.
  This is correct; note it when reading receipts.
- The downloaded capture is hashed but not validated. After fetch, an
  offline torch.load check should confirm `meta.latched == 1`, rank and
  generation, and that the saved #routed digest equals the analysis
  digest of that (rank, epoch, layer) and differs from the modal one. If
  it equals the modal digest, the reference itself was the faulty first
  occurrence and the capture holds a modal call.
- `check_coverage` does not check the per-record capture status. Optional:
  require every record in moe-capture mode to carry
  `capture.generation` (capture armed on every rank) and no `error` state.
  The analyzer's incomplete list covers errors only after a latch or error
  line.
- Verified sound: identities (image, kit label), kind mapping, all 9 seams
  x 40 layers on every rank, seam_runners == 40, status lines passed to the
  v3 analyzer (strip_capture keeps latch words out of comparisons), analyzer
  file pinned to the lock, download path allowlist and rank/node mapping,
  build runs the capture CUDA gate and freeze requires its PASS, start arm
  profile (deterministic output 1, turbo 0), lock inputs include the gate
  script.

Rate caution: this boot's untraced blocks (500 + 250) were clean, versus
5/960 earlier. At 0.5%, 0 of 750 has probability of about 2%, so this boot's
rate is likely lower. Rates may differ between boots: dense/attention
tuning winners are reselected per boot, and the routed variant is cached.
Plan the capture run's length accordingly, and record each boot's selection
cache alongside the receipts.

### Review of the settle_capture delta (2026-09-25, read-only) and the offline verifier

Blocking finding: a 16-token flush may never reach the tracer.

- The DS4.1 model state sets `max_cudagraph_query_len = CED_WINDOW = 128`
  (engram-progress-model_state.py 79-84). Batches up to 128 query tokens
  are not forced eager.
- The v2 runner dispatches any batch whose token count has a captured
  candidate to that graph when compatible (vllm/v1/worker/gpu/cudagraph_utils.py
  789-813, model_runner.py 1918-1944). Capture sizes are 1-8, 12-32,
  FULL_AND_PIECEWISE, so 16 has candidates.
- A replay never runs Python, so `_begin` and the freeze would not run on
  any rank.
- Use a flush length strictly between 32 (the largest capture size) and 100
  (min_tokens). 64 has no captured graph and no trace record, so the
  expected record counts are unchanged. Update the prompt_tokens check to
  match.
- Whether a compatible FULL candidate exists for a single 16-token prefill
  was not resolved by reading. 64 removes the question.

Otherwise sound:

- settle_capture is bounded (60 s deadline, at most three flushes one
  second apart). It flushes only while some pending generation is not
  already fetching, returns at once on an error, and retries cover a
  receipt copied just before the writer set ready.
- The random cache_salt avoids a prefix-cache hit.
- Saved files are downloaded and hashed before raising on incomplete.
- moe-capture coverage now requires a positive capture generation and no
  error on every record.
- A `latched-discarded` generation cannot be completed by flushing; it
  exhausts the flushes and raises, which is the intended outcome.

Offline verifier: claude_verify_moe_capture.py plus
claude_test_verify_moe_capture.py (12 CPU tests on receipts produced by
the real v3 helper through SyncTransport). CPU torch.load(weights_only=True).
The tracer and analyzer must match the v3 lock.

Checks per capture:

- file name `<node>-<pid>-g<gen>-capture.pt` against saved node, session pid
  and generation; optional capture-downloads.json bytes and sha256;
  latched meta; runner name and index;
- exactly one matching `saved` status line (path, node, epoch, rows,
  runner);
- layout: input and routed shape and dtype, first dimension equal to rows,
  float32 logits and weights, int ids in range;
- digests with the tracer's own function against the same
  arm/session/rank/epoch record: input vs #input_after (both after the
  op), logits, top-k weights and ids, routed, and routed vs the meta
  digest;
- a saved status line without its file, and a listed download without its
  file, both fail.

Classification against the modal #routed of aligned records with the same
step key on that rank: nonmodal (fault captured), modal (the latch
reference was the faulty first occurrence), or undecided. It also reports
`reference_is_modal`, per-seam `route_inputs_modal` (#input, #input_after,
#logits, #topk_weights, #topk_ids, #shared) and `input_changed_by_op`.
Exit 1 on any mismatch; the classification never passes or fails a
capture.

## Router output to routed result: ownership map of the executed plan (2026-09-25, source only)

Scope: the frozen DS4.1 selection (dynamic, internal route planner,
grouped, tile 16, MAC 48, w4a8_mx, n64 repack, split-materialized,
deterministic output), vLLM 1794dcf1 and B12X a7d7d29b. Established in
earlier sections, cited here, not re-derived.

Proven executed chain, all on the caller's current (main) stream, with no
programmatic dependent launch anywhere in it:

1. Gate: `router_logits, _ = self.gate(hidden_states)` (moe_runner.py 956),
   a fresh caching-allocator fp32 tensor on main.
2. Router: `self.router.select_experts(...)` (moe_runner.py 660-666);
   fused_topk_bias defaults to int32 ids with no indices_type. Fresh
   tensors on main.
3. `B12xExperts.apply` (b12x.py 690-760):
   - `_normalize_topk_weights` returns its input for fp32 contiguous
     weights;
   - `bind(plan, scratch=workspace2, a=hidden_states,
     output=workspace[2])`, where both are views of the one
     `get_simultaneous` lease;
   - `run` uses `current_cuda_stream()`, i.e. `torch.cuda.current_stream()`
     (b12x/_lib/utils.py 187-189; _impl 13052).
4. Kernels in stream order:
   - the dynamic front end: routing histogram, prefix, placement,
     per-token MXFP8 input and task metadata, all written into workspace2;
   - W4A8MaterializedPhase1 (FC1 into the workspace2 intermediate);
   - W4A8MaterializedPhase2 (FC2 into route_output in workspace2);
   - the fixed-order top-k sum into fused_out.

   Every handoff between them is a kernel boundary on one stream.

Ownership boundaries crossed, each with its evidence:

| Boundary | Status |
| --- | --- |
| gate/router tensors -> front end | same stream; freed by Python after the op; main-pool reuse is stream-ordered |
| hidden_states -> aux shared MLP | record_stream(aux) in maybe_sync; read-only on both streams |
| workspace2 / fused_out vs aux shared_ws | disjoint consecutive views of one lease; locked arena; growth synchronizes |
| front end -> phase 1 -> phase 2 -> top-k sum | separate launches, same stream |
| within the front end | resident-grid barriers; formal gap: the last arriver has no acquire after its atom (dynamic.py 1789-1810); no stale-L1 exposure found for the post-barrier reads |
| within phase 1 and phase 2 | canonical cp.async ring (wait, fence, bar before reads; bar before restage); padding rows guarded |
| Engram side stream during layers 2-13 (for layer 14) and 0 (for layer 1) | writes only its persistent engram_io_rows / staged_rows, pinned ids_host and mapped row caches (claude-ple-batch-_impl.py 145-176, disk_table.py 419-445, engram.py 718-730); no alias with the MoE path |
| data-pointer-keyed caches | none on this path: `_WEIGHT_CACHE` keys persistent weight pointers; `_route_pack_cache` is a W4A16 compile key; others are compile or device keys |

Result: no ownership or lifetime defect between router input and routed
result was found by reading. That is not proof of absence: kernel-internal
behaviour under contention and the hardware/firmware layer cannot be
established by reading.

Open mechanisms, all speculative:

- (a) Front-end synchronization under partial residency. The M16 split
  front end launches min(policy 48, SMs) CTAs relying on a resident grid.
  Concurrent aux/Engram kernels can stagger CTA arrival. The barrier is
  correct under staggering except for the formal last-arriver acquire gap.
  Integer atomics decide only placement, not values.
- (b) A node or hardware property (three of three seam events on toby this
  boot; other nodes in older builds).
- (c) Something outside the traced chain modifying the inputs or outputs;
  v3 #input_after and the capture test this directly.

Positional observation (weak): every localized first divergence so far is
at layers 0, 2-3, 11, 12 or 13, each immediately before an Engram consumer
layer (1 or 14), i.e. inside a window where the Engram side stream is
working. Against uniform placement in the overlap-eligible layers 0-19,
six of six in 0-13 has probability of about 0.12. It fits both the
shared-overlap and Engram-overlap windows; it discriminates neither.

Best next test, if the capture shows modal logits, ids, weights and input
with a nonmodal routed output:

1. A real-input replay on toby and on one other node, built after the
   capture lands. Use the captured hidden, ids and weights with that
   rank's real shard of the captured layer only (about 1.7 GB, after
   serving stops), through the frozen cached plan (probe machinery,
   cache_only), thousands of repeats, comparing against the modal digest
   from the trace. New arm: forced partial residency. A Triton spin kernel
   that holds k SMs (large shared memory per CTA, so no front-end CTA can
   co-reside there), launched on a second stream just before each MoE
   launch and released after a variable delay, sweeping k over 1, 8, 24 and
   47. This deliberately staggers front-end CTA arrival at its grid
   barriers, which no earlier isolated arm guaranteed; also run one arm
   with an Engram-like side load.
   - Reproduces on real inputs: a kernel-level fault with a reproducer, and
     the diff pattern localizes it.
   - Clean on both nodes: the in-situ factor lies outside the kernel's own
     inputs and scheduling.
2. In parallel, untraced (instrumentation suppresses the event), across
   boots in ABAB order to handle per-boot rate variation: baseline,
   VLLM_DS41_ENGRAM_OVERLAP=0 (lookups synchronous on main before the
   forward, per prepare_disk_engram), and VLLM_DISABLE_SHARED_EXPERTS_STREAM=1.
   At about 0.5%, roughly 1500 requests per arm separate unchanged from
   gone. These are concurrency-removal discriminators, not fixes, and both
   variables are outside the launch-contract allowlist.

## Router projection capture: data-level evidence of a stage-reuse race in bf16_gemv prefill (2026-09-25)

Receipts: receipts/moe-capture-localization-20260925/probe/traces-00.
Three events (882 M256 dusty layer 8 #logits; 906 M254 dusty layer 6
#logits; 1590 M254 rusty layer 11 #logits). One latch per generation, so
only 882 was captured at its source layer; the other ranks' captures are
downstream (layer 9).

Executed gate kernel (demonstrated):

- DS4.1 gate: `ReplicatedLinear` + `B12xLinearMethod`, bf16 weight
  [384, 5120], fp32 output (b12x_layers.py 146-152, 218-302). `apply`
  picks the plan for (x.dtype, x.shape[0]), else the max_tokens (8192)
  capacity plan, and calls `bf16_gemv.mm`.
- Backends eligible for this query: simt, mma, and prefill (prefill only
  for (384|512|1024, 5120); torch is excluded by the fp32 output).
  bf16_gemv/_tuning.py 60-100.
- Selection, decoded offline from the retained B12X selection caches
  (receipts/routed-isolation-toby-20260925T204843Z/tuning-before.json,
  stale-probe-tuning-toby.json, trace-tuning-*.json; key rebuilt as
  session._choice_key does):
  - planned bf16 row counts are 1-8, 12-32 step 4, 128, 256, 512, 1024,
    2048, 4096, 8185 and 8192;
  - rows 128, 256 and 8192 select backend "prefill" in every cache;
  - M256 uses the exact 256-row plan and M254 the 8192 plan, both prefill;
  - so do M258, M271 and M1023 (no events there).
- Correction to the user's note: M256 is not a capacity fallback; only
  M254 is.

The prefill kernel (bf16_gemv/_prefill.py) is a 2-stage PipelineTmaAsync:
producer warp 4 issues TMA into stage kt % 2; 4 compute warps run
`_warp_gemm` (ldmatrix per 16-wide K step, prefetching k+1 before the MMA
of k), then `load_pipeline.consumer_release` immediately (lines 311-324)
with no `fence_acq_rel_cta()`, unlike the repaired dense kernel
(dense-fence-before-release.patch, dense_gemm.py 2573/2768). Tile 64x64x64.

Forensics (claude_gate_capture_forensics.py, CPU):

- Inputs: the capture dusty-524-g1-capture.pt (verified by
  claude_verify_moe_capture.py) and the layer-8 gate weight, range-read
  from the pinned revision (model-00011-of-00048.safetensors, BF16
  [384,5120], sha256 b98a45639ac40ddb...).
- Compared against fp64 input @ W.T: everywhere p99 |err| = 2.8e-7,
  except one 64x64 CTA tile (m_tile 3, n_tile 5): rows 192-194, all 64
  columns 320-383, max |err| 0.156.
- For each of the three rows, the captured logits equal the correct result
  with exactly one substitution. The activation chunk K 48..63 (the last
  16-wide ldmatrix/MMA step of K-tile 0) was read from K-tile 2's bytes
  (K 176..191), the next occupant of the same pipeline stage. Residual
  about 6e-7 per row, at fp32 noise; the next-best hypothesis leaves
  0.26-0.28.

Status:

- Demonstrated at data level: the kernel consumed stage-0 A data after
  that stage had been refilled with K-tile 2, i.e. a write-after-read
  stage reuse. The bytes-level signature is the one expected when a
  consumer's final shared-memory reads are overtaken by the producer's TMA
  refill after `consumer_release`.
- Consistent with the observed conditions: only M <= 256 in layers 0-19,
  where the gate runs concurrently with the shared-expert MLP on the aux
  stream; suppression when kernels are added inside that window;
  rank-random; clean isolated MoE arms (they never ran the gate).
- Not yet causal: that a fence before `consumer_release` removes it
  (replay and fenced arm below).

Same unfenced consumer loop elsewhere (a7d7d29b; executed status):

| Site | Executed for DS4.1? |
| --- | --- |
| gemm/bf16_gemv/_prefill.py 324 | proven (gate, M in {128, 256, 8192 plans}) |
| norm/mhc/_kernels.py 2509, 2988 (the projection _prefill.py says it follows) | not established; decode its selection from the same caches |
| attention/_shared/contiguous/forward.py 1098/1130; attention/paged/* | not established |
| quantization/mxfp6/bf16_to_fp6_tma.py 364; sequence/mtp_feedback/_cute_prefill.py 375 | not established |
| moe/_shared/kernels/dynamic.py (9 sites) | not executed (earlier section) |
| _lib/dense_gemm.py 2573/2768 | fenced in the image by the dense-release patch |

Minimal real-input replay (not built; needs only about 4 MB of weight):

1. On one Spark node with serving stopped, inside the capture image, load
   the captured `input` (256 x 5120 bf16) and the layer-8 gate weight on
   GPU.
2. Build the gate plans exactly as B12xLinearMethod does (GemvQuery bf16 ->
   fp32, max_rows 256 and 8192) with a cache_only PreparationSession on a
   private copy of the boot selection file (same gates as the routed
   probe). Require backend prefill and source "cached".
3. Run `bf16_gemv.mm` 1e5+ times at rows 256 (256 plan) and 254 (8192
   plan), NaN-filling the output and comparing bitwise to the first run.
4. Arms: idle; concurrent block-32 shared-expert-shaped GEMM on a second
   stream; SM-occupancy pressure.
5. Classify any mismatch with claude_gate_capture_forensics.py (expected
   signature: A chunk from kt+2).
6. Fenced arm: a diagnostic B12X derivative adding
   `cute.arch.fence_acq_rel_cta()` before `load_pipeline.consumer_release`
   at _prefill.py 324 (same form as the dense fix); expected zero
   mismatches at matched call counts.

### Real-input gate replay helper (built 2026-09-25, CPU-tested, not run)

Files:

- claude_replay_gate_prefill.py (sha256 33d245e3150f...)
- claude_test_replay_gate_prefill.py (12 CPU tests)
- claude-pinned-bf16_gemv-_prefill.py, a verbatim copy of B12X a7d7d29b
  b12x/gemm/bf16_gemv/_prefill.py (sha256 d92cf099...), used only to check
  a fenced derivative.

Baseline mode:

- The installed _prefill.py must equal the pinned blob.
- A cache_only session on a private copy of the boot selection file (name,
  identity and directory gates) must give every plan source "cached" and
  backend "prefill".
- The cache must hold 256 and 8192 records and no 254 record, so M256
  uses its exact plan and M254 the 8192 capacity plan, as in production.
- Records each plan's key, config and cached program identities.

Fenced mode:

- The installed _prefill.py must be the pinned text plus exactly one
  `cute.arch.fence_acq_rel_cta()` line, at the release's indentation,
  immediately before `load_pipeline.consumer_release(consumer_state)`.
- The plan is pinned by override (selection source "override") and
  compiled in process into a fresh `--compile-cache-dir`, set before b12x
  is imported. It never reads cached selections or programs.
- Receipts carry source_changed, the fenced sha256 and "override".

Replay:

- Inputs: the captured input (rows 256; 254 as its first 254 rows) and the
  layer gate weight, read from the local snapshot or a raw file with the
  sha256 required.
- Call: `bf16_gemv.mm(x, weight, plan=..., out=out, output_dtype=float32)`,
  the same prepared state.run launcher as production's allocating call.
  The out buffer is NaN-filled before the timed window.
- The reference must equal the captured logits bitwise outside the rows the
  forensics fit marks corrupted; otherwise refused.
- Repeats compare bitwise with the reference. The first --keep failures are
  kept and classified with claude_gate_capture_forensics.analyze
  (kt+2-style fit).
- Overlap arm: a shared-expert-shaped bf16 matmul chain on a second
  stream, released by an event recorded on main just before the gate.
  Overlap is measured per repeat with timed events (gate interval inside
  the side interval), with an overlap fraction and validity threshold, and
  gate duration compared with idle.
- Memory: counters, a 2048-sample timing reservoir and --keep failures.

### Review: run_router_isolation.py and prepare_router_release_probe.py (read-only, nothing changed)

Verified:

- router-prefill-release-before.py (sha256 ca888af4...) passes
  `check_fence_source` against the pinned a7d7d29b blob: exactly one
  `cute.arch.fence_acq_rel_cta()` at the release's indentation, directly
  before the single `load_pipeline.consumer_release(consumer_state)`. The
  patch is one added line. The preparer refuses more than one release site
