  and refuses to overwrite a different diagnostic.
- The wrapper pins the capture boot image and DS41_NODE=dusty, copies the
  boot's B12X/CUTE/CUTLASS/CUDA env, checks idle before and after, mounts
  the HF snapshot read-only, compares the production selection file before
  and after, keeps inputs and hashes, and (fenced) mounts the fenced file
  read-only over the installed _prefill.py with a fresh compile dir under
  /diag.
- B12X's disk compile cache keys include the b12x package fingerprint
  (compiler.py 1446-1459, 1684-1691), so a fenced _prefill.py cannot load
  or shadow an unfenced B12X artifact. The baseline's identity gate ties
  it to the boot's compile cache.

Findings (none blocking the baseline):

1. Exit-code collision: the helper now returns 2 for clean-but-invalid
   overlap, and 2 already means REFUSED (docstring line 59, `Refused`).
   Use a distinct code (for example 3) and update the docstring.
2. Fenced arm, CuTe DSL cache: the copied env keeps
   CUTE_DSL_CACHE_DIR=/cache/jit/ds41-engram-b3e4f0ada602fab6f3ce/cute-dsl,
   and /cache is mounted read-write. My helper redirects only
   B12X_COMPILE_CACHE_DIR, so the fenced compile will write into
   production's CuTe DSL cache. Collision is unlikely (the IR differs) but
   it is still a production-cache mutation. For the fenced arm, set
   CUTE_DSL_CACHE_DIR to a subdirectory of the fresh compile dir and mount
   /cache read-only (only the capture is read from it). For the baseline,
   either mount /cache read-only as a proof of no writes, or record a
   file-count and newest-mtime listing of the b12x and cute-dsl cache dirs
   before and after.
3. Capture identity is not checked on the node: the wrapper uses
   /cache/ds41-act-trace/dusty-524-g1-capture.pt without comparing it with
   the verified receipt (traces-00/capture-downloads.json). Add a remote
   sha256 check, or pass an expected sha to the helper.
4. Overlap evidence (agree with the concern): the events bracket queue
   positions, not execution. g0 completes when main reaches it, before the
   gate's CTAs necessarily start, so containment can hold with fully
   serialized execution when the side kernels hold every SM. Stronger
   evidence, cheapest first:
   - (a) the gate-duration ratio overlap/idle: near 1 plus small bandwidth
     contention means co-execution; close to the side duration means
     serialization;
   - (b) resource headroom by construction: gate grid at 256 rows is 4 x 6
     = 24 CTAs; keep the side kernel's CTA count at or below 24 of the 48
     SMs (the [256,5120]x[5120,1152] cuBLAS tile count should be recorded)
     so co-residency is possible;
   - (c) for a subset of repeats, CUPTI/nsys kernel activity timestamps
     (execution start and end per kernel), which is the direct proof of
     overlapping execution.

   Label the arm "measured interval overlap" and report (a); claim
   co-residency only with (c).

### Review of the next-test approach (2026-09-25, after 440k clean isolated baseline calls)

The inference is sound as stated:

- The captured nonmodal logits are explained byte-exactly by a
  stage-reuse (kt+2) read in the bf16_gemv prefill kernel (data-level
  evidence, independently reproduced).
- The isolated replay (440k calls, interval overlap above 99.99%, gate
  median 0.118 ms vs 0.076 ms idle) did not reproduce it, so isolation
  lacks the in-model trigger.
- A narrow full-model fence derivative is both the discriminator and the
  candidate fix. No cheaper discriminator with the same specificity
  exists: every in-model alternative needs a new boot too, and the
  selection-cache backend override (simt/mma) or
  VLLM_DISABLE_SHARED_EXPERTS_STREAM change more than the release
  ordering.

Required design points for a valid A/B:

1. Frozen selections. The selection-cache identity is namespace, compute
   capability, SM count, tuning-cache version and schema (_cache.py
   25-53), with no package fingerprint. So the fenced image should reuse
   the parent's selection file if it inherits the same
   B12X_COMPILE_CACHE_DIR. Compiled-program keys do include the package
   fingerprint (compiler.py 1446-1459, 1646-1651), so every B12X program
   recompiles; whether cached records still load when their recorded
   program identities change is not provable from reading. Gate: after the
   fenced boot, decode the selection file and require every key's config
   identical to the parent's (same method as
   claude_tuning_key_recon.py), and no new tuning measurements in the
   startup log. Any retune is a confound, because winners vary between
   boots and change timing.
2. Compiled evidence of the fence. As for the dense release, check the
   gate prefill kernel's SASS: a CTA-scope fence before the mbarrier
   arrive of the consumer release in the fenced build, absent in the
   parent. Offline on the build node.
3. Concurrent control. The rate varies between boots (5/960 untraced
   earlier; 0/750 on another boot; about 3/200 exposed requests traced).
   Run parent and fenced boots ABAB (at least A, B, A, B) with the same
   profile and schedule; do not compare with historical rates.
4. Exposure. Only 254/256-row prefills put the gate inside the overlap
   window in layers 0-19 (the gate uses the 256 plan or the 8192 plan;
   258/271/1023 run the same 8192 kernel without events). Weight the
   interleaved corpus toward 254 and 256, keeping 258 as a within-boot
   control, so 3000 requests carry about 3000 exposures, not 1200.
5. Decision rule, fixed in advance. For example, Fisher exact on exposed
   requests, fenced vs parent pooled. With a parent rate around 1% per
   exposed request, 3000 per arm gives about 30 expected parent events.
   If a parent boot shows 0 or 1 events, the comparison is inconclusive:
   extend it, do not call it clean.
6. Residuals. The fence covers only the gate prefill. Earlier
   localizations in older builds also hit other kernels, and the mHC BF16
   projection (norm/mhc/_kernels.py 2509, 2988) has the identical
   unfenced loop but is not yet shown to execute. Nonzero fenced events
   do not refute the gate mechanism; they call for the capture image on
   the fenced parent to localize the residual. In parallel, offline:
   decode the mHC selections from the same caches to establish whether
   those variants execute.

## mHC selections in the capture boot, and what the router fence orders (2026-09-25, offline)

### mHC: the unfenced TF32 TMA projection runs in every prefill layer (demonstrated)

Source: receipts/moe-capture-localization-20260925/probe/traces-00/dusty-tuning.json
(sha256 c3e25a5be07a...). All 54 norm.mhc records decode exactly:

- component norm.mhc, query schema 8, config schema 4, semantic 1,
  candidate contract 13;
- query as B12xMHC builds it (b12x_layers.py 700-760): lagged_mix, norm
  weight, rms/norm eps 1e-20, hc_eps 1e-6, 20 iterations, has_fn_bf16 False,
  split_k 80, default codegen constants, no mHC env controls,
  smem_limit 101376;
- the key's invocation field is the DS4.1 invocation mapping (plan_mhc
  passes it to Plan).

Planned mHC counts are 1-8, 10, 12, 14, 16, 20, 21, 24, 28, 32 and 8192
(max_tokens plus fixed counts only). `_plan_for` uses the exact plan or
the capacity plan, so every 254/256/258/271/1023-row prefill runs the
8192 plans.

| Operation | Rows | Selected |
| --- | --- | --- |
| post_pre | 8192 | tf32_tma, tile 192x32x64, 3 stages, k_splits 1 |
| post_pre | 20-32 | tf32_tma |
| post_pre | <= 16 | native (lagged prepare) |
| pre (expanded residual) | 8192 | tf32_tma, tile 128x32x128, 2 stages, k_splits 8 |
| pre (expanded residual) | 20-32 | tf32_tma |
| pre (non-expanded) | all | native |

Kernel mapping (_preparation.py 58-72, 170-215; _kernels.py 4176-4215):

- `tf32_tma` routes to MHCPrefillTf32ProjectTmaKernel, whose consumer loop
  ends in an unfenced `load_pipeline.consumer_release` (_kernels.py 2988).
  It is therefore executed in every decoder layer's post_pre for every
  prefill in the corpus, and in some pre invocations.
- The BF16 TMA variant (site 2509, MHCPrefillBf16ProjectTmaKernel) needs
  route bf16_tma, which requires has_fn_bf16. DS4.1 passes False, so it
  does not run.

Consequence for the router-only fence A/B: a second executed kernel with
the same unfenced consumer-release pattern remains, in every layer. Keep
the router-only arm for attribution. Residual events whose first divergence
is at mHC outputs (layer carried members or attention input) are the
expected signature if this site races too. A separate one-site mHC fence
derivative (site 2988 only) is the next arm; combining both in one arm
loses attribution.

### What the router fence orders (source and PTX semantics; no SASS inferred)

Source facts (bf16_gemv/_prefill.py 238-248, 310-325; CUTLASS DSL
pipeline/sm90.py 478-534, 640-700, 730-745; cute/arch/mbar.py 348-386; local
DSL copies 4.5.x and 4.6.0, image pins 4.6.2):

- PipelineTmaAsync with consumer group CooperativeGroup(Agent.Thread, 4)
  (one per compute warp) and cluster (1,1,1,1). `is_signaling_thread` is
  `tidx % 32 < 1`, i.e. lane 0 of each warp. The empty barrier expects 4
  arrivals per phase, one per warp, each made by lane 0 on behalf of its
  whole warp.
- consumer_release calls `mbarrier_arrive` only on the signaling thread:
  `nvvm.mbarrier_arrive(ptr, count=1, scope=CTA)`, i.e. a CTA-scope arrive
  on the shared-memory barrier.
- The inserted `cute.arch.fence_acq_rel_cta()` is unconditional (all
  consumer threads execute it) and lowers to `nvvm.fence_acq_rel_cta` or,
  in 4.6.0, `llvm.fence acq_rel syncscope("block")`, i.e. PTX
  `fence.acq_rel.cta`. It sits after the K step's last ldmatrix/MMA and
  before the arrive in program order.

What the capture says about ordering:

- The corrupted elements are warp 0's rows 0-2 (m-tile 3 rows 192-194) for
  the last 16-wide K chunk of K-tile 0, carrying K-tile 2's bytes, i.e.
  TMA(kt2) had partly overwritten stage 0 (its first rows) when that
  ldmatrix read it.
- TMA(kt2) is issued only after the stage-0 empty phase completes, which
  needs warp 0's own arrive. Warps cannot lap: a warp's next wait on stage
  0 needs TMA(kt2), which needs the empty completion.
- So warp 0's arrive became visible before warp 0's own last ldmatrix of
  that stage had been performed. That is exactly the reordering the fence
  is meant to forbid.

Assessment:

- In PTX-model terms, `fence.acq_rel.cta` executed by the arriving lane
  orders that lane's prior memory operations before its subsequent arrive.
  Hardware executes fences per warp, which in practice covers the
  warp-collective ldmatrix. This is the same construction as the empirical
  dense fix.
- Formal caveat: ldmatrix is warp-collective, and the other 31 lanes'
  portions are ordered before lane 0's arrive only through an intra-warp
  synchronization. A fence without inter-lane sync does not create one in
  the formal model. The formally complete variant at the same single site
  is `cute.arch.sync_warp()` followed by the fence before
  consumer_release; alternatively every consumer thread arrives.
- Recommendation: keep the one-line fence arm as specified (it matches the
  proven dense repair and keeps attribution). If it reduces but does not
  eliminate gate events, test the sync_warp-plus-fence variant at the same
  site before blaming another kernel.
- Whether the emitted arrive carries `.release` and how ptxas schedules it
  cannot be settled without compiling; this is a source-level assessment
  only.

## A vs seeded-B selection comparison helper (2026-09-25, offline)

claude_compare_selections.py (stdlib only) and claude_test_compare_selections.py
(10 tests, on retained real caches plus synthetic edits).

Facts from the retained caches, used by the helper:
- Records hold only assignment, config, coverage and programs; keys are
  opaque. The router and mHC keys are rebuilt from source and decode
  completely: 54 of 54 mHC and 31 router plans, and the router key matches
  claude_replay_gate_prefill.choice_key. The 9 config field signatures map
  to components.
- Between boots on the same node, the only new records are
  compressed-sparse-MLA ones. Their query carries KV capacity (max_kv_rows,
  page-table width, cache shapes), and there are 8 per boot at a new
  serving block count. No other family grows.
- The log's "N measured" counts candidate measurements, not choices. The
  8 new sparse-MLA choices sum to coverage.measured_count 20, which matches
  "20 measured, 160 cached" in the ee035 dusty startup. So 10 measured is
  about 4 choices, not 10.
- Same-node programs are unchanged across boots. A programs-only change is
  reported but is not drift.

Classification:
- Same key with a different config or assignment is drift, because the key
  fixes the query.
- A key lost from the seed, a new key in a capacity-independent family, or
  a changed identity is drift.
- A new capacity-family key counts as capacity-only only when the logs
  show different serving blocks. With equal blocks it is "unresolved: should
  have hit cache". Without logs it is unresolved.
- These new keys cannot be paired with the A query they replace.
  boot_new_winners gives unpaired winner multisets per boot.
All results are conditional until B's post-boot cache and logs exist.

## Router fence SASS observation, interpretation review

Reported: the baseline has the final LDSM, then the consumer SYNCS.ARRIVE.
The fenced build has the final LDSM, then MEMBAR.ALL.CTA, then SYNCS.ARRIVE.
- Structure matches the source (the fence lowers to MEMBAR.ALL.CTA directly
  before the release arrive). This is structural, not causal.
- The load-bearing question is whether the arrive can issue before the LDSM
  has read shared memory. The baseline order alone does not show a race.
  What matters is whether SYNCS.ARRIVE waits on the LDSM's scoreboard
  (the control-bits wait mask), or whether the MMA that consumes that
  fragment comes before the arrive. If neither holds, the baseline arrive
  can leave with the LDSM still in flight. That is a sufficient mechanism
  for the observed kt+2 bytes, but still a hypothesis.
- MEMBAR.ALL.CTA is a warp-wide instruction. In practice it resolves the
  formal intra-warp ldmatrix caveat, because all 32 lanes' LDSM precede it.
  Whether MEMBAR itself waits for outstanding LDSM data return is
  hardware-documented only as memory ordering. Stating "fence waits for
  the load" is an inference.
- Checks worth recording, none needing a GPU:
  - Every consumer release site in the compiled kernel gets the MEMBAR.
    Unrolled loops and the tail K step can each have their own arrive,
    so count the sites in both builds.
  - The arrive's barrier operand is the load pipeline's empty barrier.
  - The only other difference is register or scheduling noise.
  - The SASS comes from the program the image actually runs (the fenced
    source, the image's DSL and ptxas, sm_121a, the prefill config
    selected at 256 and 8192).
- The same static check on MHCPrefillTf32ProjectTmaKernel (site 2988) would
  show whether the residual candidate has the same arrive-without-wait
  shape. That too is structural only.

1. (Medium, ABAB) There is no symmetric revert. select only moves from

   If blocks differ from 81375, expect about 8 new sparse-MLA choices;
   that confound stays in the conclusions.
4. (Low) select writes candidate.json and runtime-files.json
   non-atomically, after the amendment. A crash in between leaves a
   manifest mismatch that the launch should reject. Confirm run_node's
   runtime-files check fails closed.
5. (Note) The mid-run snapshot should equal the final post-run snapshot,
   since serving does not write selections. A difference is a finding,
   not noise.
6. (Cosmetic) distribute prints ACTIVATION-TRACE-DISTRIBUTION-COMPLETE
   for the router image.

### B1 selection `programs` field is stale metadata, and how to anchor the B router binary (2026-09-25, offline)

Confirmed from pinned source (b12x a7d7d29b):
- session._lookup reads a cache hit's assignment, config and coverage.
  It re-lowers the assignment and requires the result to equal the saved
  config, then marks the selection "cached". It never reads `programs`.
- SelectionCache.save runs only when obligation.cache_pending is set.
  That happens only for "tuned" selections (session 1281, 1439), never for
  cached ones. So a hit never rewrites its record, and `programs` is the
  program set from the boot that first measured it.
- The executed program set is recomputed at install on every boot
  (_compile -> describe_compilation(job)). A cute program's key is the
  compile-cache key: `<B12X_COMPILE_CACHE_DIR>/<key[:2]>/<key>.o` plus a
  `.json` manifest (compiler 2046-2050, compile_plan 134-140). That key
  includes the package fingerprint, the toolchain, the device UUID, and
  the compile options and env (compiler 1644-1680). This explains the
  per-node program ids.
- Receipts agree. In A1-final vs B1, all 1082 common records have
  byte-identical `programs`. B's 8 new records carry 12 program keys, none
  of them parent keys. The router 256 and 8192 plans share one program key
  per node (dusty 31b8df52..., toby 1057c685...), consistent with rows being
  a dynamic dimension. Those keys are parent-era and stale in B.

Narrowest read-only anchor for the B router binary (per node, while or
after B serves; B's cache is bind-mounted on the host, so no podman exec
and no torch import is needed. Mind the thin MemAvailable):
1. On the host, list the manifests
   ~/.cache/vllm-jj-ds41-tp4/jit/<router fp>/b12x/??/*.json. Select those
   with kernel_id == "gemm.bf16_prefill", out_features/weight 384 and
   fp32 output in compile_spec_json. B's cache was fresh, so expect
   exactly one per node.
2. Record cache_key, object_sha256 (equal to sha256 of the .o),
   package_fingerprint, toolchain, compile_environment and mtime (inside
   the B boot window). Require one fingerprint across all B artifacts,
   different from the fingerprint in the parent cache's gemm.bf16_prefill
   manifest (the engram fp dir, same host).
3. Disassemble that .o and the parent's equivalent offline (cuobjdump or
   nvdisasm on the copied files). Require MEMBAR.ALL.CTA before every
   consumer SYNCS.ARRIVE in B, and none in the parent. This ties the fenced
   SASS to the object B's runtime resolved by key.
4. Optional, definitive fingerprint: in the B container, run a stdlib-only
   python that replicates _compute_b12x_package_fingerprint (sorted rglob,
   rel path + NUL + bytes + NUL, excluding __pycache__/.pyc) over
   /opt/jovian-judgement/b12x/b12x, and compare with step 2.
Caveat: this shows which object the key resolves to on disk, not a
process-level trace of the loaded module; the fresh cache makes this
unambiguous. Do not use record `programs` for the anchor.

## Router artifact on-disk provenance helper (2026-09-25, offline)

claude_router_artifact_provenance.py and claude_test_router_artifact_provenance.py
(10 tests pass; one runs the real extractor and cuobjdump on the retained
isolation objects).
- collect (stdlib only; runs on a node host against the bind-mounted
  ~/.cache/vllm-jj-ds41-tp4/jit/<fp>/b12x; writes only --out, which is
  refused inside the cache):
  - scans <2hex>/<key>.json compile_manifest.v3 files;
  - selects kernel_id gemm.bf16_prefill whose tensor facts are weight
    exact 384x5120 bf16, source dynamic x 5120 bf16 and output float32;
  - verifies manifest name == cache_key, the fingerprint against
    cache_payload[2], and the .o sha256 and size against the manifest;
  - summarizes the package fingerprints of every manifest in the dir, and
    lists other prefill manifests with the reason they were not selected.
- compare (parent vs candidate report, same node): passes only with:
  - exactly one router artifact each and no problems;
  - one fingerprint in the candidate dir, different from the parent's;
  - objects that are not byte-identical;
  - equal toolchain, options, device UUID, target and spec hash;
  - equal compile env, ignoring path-valued (*_DIR) entries.
- sass: runs the existing extract_dense_cubin.py, then cuobjdump. It
  classifies SYNCS.ARRIVE sites: .A1T0 is the consumer release, and the
  TRANS64 arrives with a register count are producer expect-tx arrives.
  It checks a MEMBAR.ALL.CTA lies between the nearest LDSM (within 8
  instructions) and each consumer release. --expect applies
  verify_router_sass.py's rule plus "all consumer releases fenced / none".
New fact from the retained isolation dumps: each build has exactly ONE
consumer release site. Baseline: LDSM at 0af0, then the arrive at 0b00.
Fenced: LDSM 09e0, MEMBAR 09f0, NOP, arrive 0a10. The other 8 arrives are
producer-side. So "every release site fenced" is satisfied by the one
MEMBAR.
Caveats:
- The tensor-fact layout of a real gemm.bf16_prefill manifest was inferred
  from compiler.py (tensor_compile_fact, legacy field specs); no real
  prefill manifest is retained locally. If the layout differs, selection
  fails closed ("tensor facts absent", visible under
  other_prefill_manifests). The first real collect must show exactly one
  selected artifact per node.
- Everything here is on-disk provenance and instruction order. It does not
  trace the module a process loaded.

## Holding the executed attention choices fixed across a paired comparison (2026-09-25, offline)

Source (b12x a7d7d29b, vLLM 1794dcf1):
- Why the regime moves: attention.compressed_sparse_mla tunes
  v41_compute_mode over ("fp8", "bf16") and v41_heads_per_block over
  (8, 16). The DS4.1 V4.1 cache format keeps the default fp8 but
  autotunes both. The winner is whichever measures faster, so precision is
  a timing outcome whenever a key is new.
- Why keys are new each boot: attention.py declares mla.plan with
  descriptor() of the live swa and indexed caches. shape is
  tuple(tensor.shape), which includes the serving block count. alignment
  is min(16, lowest set bit of data_ptr), so it is 16 for any
  allocator-aligned tensor and stable. So equal serving blocks give equal
  queries and keys.
- No narrower supported control exists:
  - There is no B12X env pin for sparse MLA (no B12X_* read in
    compressed_sparse_mla or preparation, other than tuning cache
    version, exhaustive, trace dir, workers and autotune).
  - The Plan `override=` / pinned path exists in b12x (session 923,
    source "override"), but vLLM's attention.py does not pass one. Using
    it is a source change.
  - B12X_AUTOTUNE=0 or enable_b12x_autotune=False applies defaults to
    every component. That is a broad numerical policy change, not narrow.
- Other per-boot measured choices: FlashInfer autotune runs, but it
  "Loaded 0 configs ... Saved 0 configs": nothing tunable is in use.
  dsa_indexer records did not grow across boots. The only boot-varying
  B12X choices in the receipts are the 8 sparse-MLA records.

Is fixed num_gpu_blocks plus exact seeding sufficient? For B12X selections,
yes by construction, and it is checkable:
- The same --num-gpu-blocks-override N in both arms gives identical cache
  shapes, so identical sparse-MLA and indexer keys. Seed both namespaces
  with the byte-identical selection file that contains records for N.
  Then 0 measured at bind, and the comparator returns
  identical-selections with no new keys.
- N must be <= the smallest feasible capacity on every rank in both arms
  (history 79435 to 82030). It is a declared launch-contract change for
  both arms, so A1/B1 are not members of that pair. On GB10 unified
  memory a smaller pool also shifts host MemAvailable (equal across
  arms, but it changes the thin-memory condition).
- Which winners get frozen is a choice. A calibration boot at N measures
  a fresh set, and that is one more random draw. The copied file is the
  control; the regime is whatever that boot drew.
- It needs a declared merge/replace seed mode. The current seeder refuses
  to overwrite a different file, and both namespaces must end
  byte-identical.

Narrower and more informative option (proposal, offline work, no runtime
change): decode the sparse-MLA keys, as done for mHC. Rebuild
SparseMlaQuery from the declaration (caps: mode decode/extend, q rows,
widths, pages; descriptors from the cache shapes at a given block count)
and brute-force the block count per boot. Each record then gets a role
(layer kind x mode). This would:
- (a) show which roles switched fp8 <-> bf16 between A1 and B1, and test
  the hypothesis on the capture boot, which shares B1's modal;
- (b) allow a seed at N built from the exact winners of a chosen boot, per
  role (A1's or B1's regime), for both arms. That enables a 2x2
  (fence x regime) without new measurement. Rekeyed records are
  hand-built selections and must be declared as such.

## Sparse-MLA key decoder: attention regimes explain the modal grouping (2026-09-26, offline)

claude_decode_sparse_mla.py and claude_test_decode_sparse_mla.py (7 tests
pass, on retained caches). Stdlib only. Labels come only from exact sha256
key matches.

Reconstruction, each part confirmed by exact matches:
- The query and invocation follow vLLM _declare_attention and b12x
  _query, with 16 local heads, extend 8192 rows, and decode 60 rows
  (4 seqs x (1 + 2 x 7), so parallel drafting is on).
- max_page_table_width is 2344 (600000/256). Swa width is 128; the DSpark
  draft width is 192 (135 rounded up to 64).
- Cache descriptors: SWA shape [blocks, 67584] (128 x 528). Indexed shape
  [blocks, 73728] for ratio 1 and [blocks, 36864] for ratio 2 (x288 per
  token). Every stride is [227840, 1]: the block-major allocation stride,
  constant across these boots.
- 8 plans per capacity: {swa, draft, ratio1, ratio2} x {extend, decode}.
  Every layer of a role shares its plan. Block count 32 is the profiling
  pool ("32 temporary KV blocks").
- All 56 sparse-MLA keys decode on all 4 nodes (7 capacities), and the
  ranks agree on every regime. B1's 8 new keys are exactly capacity 80927.

Regime vs modal (per-boot modal at lengths 254/256/258):

| Boot | Blocks | swa.e | swa.d | r1.e | r1.d | r2.e | r2.d | Modal 256 |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| engram-first | 80591 | fp8 | bf16 | bf16 | fp8 | bf16 | bf16 | 7f9dea26 |
| activation | 81418 | bf16 | bf16 | fp8 | fp8 | fp8 | fp8 | 0ce3fd8a |
| moe-seams | 81811 | fp8 | bf16 | bf16 | fp8 | fp8 | fp8 | e558f71a |
| capture | 81914 | bf16 | bf16 | bf16 | fp8 | bf16 | fp8 | c6b1d76b |
| A1 | 81375 | bf16 | fp8 | bf16 | fp8 | fp8 | fp8 | 79a34e12 |
| B1 | 80927 | bf16 | bf16 | bf16 | fp8 | bf16 | fp8 | c6b1d76b |

- There are 5 distinct target-model regimes and 5 distinct modal triples,
  with no violations. Heads-per-block is 16 everywhere.
- Capture and B1 share the regime and the modal at all three lengths,
  although their draft precision differs (capture bf16/bf16, B1 fp8/fp8)
  and their images differ (instrumented unfenced vs fenced).
- A1 vs B1 differ exactly in ratio2.extend (fp8 vs bf16) and swa.decode
  (fp8 vs bf16).
- This is correlational over 6 boots, but tight: the modal response is
  explained by the target-model sparse-MLA precision regime. The fence,
  the instrumentation and the draft precision do not change it.

Natural control for the fence claim:
- The capture boot ran B1's exact target regime, unfenced, and diverged:
  254: 3/200, 256: 2/200, 258: 0/200. B1 had 0/1000 at every length.
- Every unfenced regime seen diverged: 80591 4/350, 81418 9/2220,
  81811 3/1500, A1 27/3000.
- So B1's regime by itself did not prevent divergence. Caveats: the
  capture image was instrumented (stash hooks) and its probe design and
  request count differ. Supporting evidence, not a matched arm.

Fixed-capacity same-winner pair without hand-built records:
- --num-gpu-blocks-override N at an already-measured N reuses real,
  measured records by their true keys. No rekeying is needed.
- At N=81375 (A1 regime), both namespaces already hold the records: the
  parent from A1, the router namespace through the A1 seed.
- At N=80927 (B1 regime), the router namespace holds them; the parent
  needs B1's 8 records merged. That needs a declared merge mode.
- A 2x2 (fence x {A1 regime, B1 regime}) is possible from existing
  records.
- Constraints: N must be <= the available capacity of every rank in
  every arm (A2 may land lower; history 79435 to 82030, so 80927 is
  safer than 81375). It is a declared launch-contract change for all
  arms. The post-boot check expects 0 measured, identical selections,
  and the decoder showing only capacity N used.
- A2 as planned (unchanged profile): if it lands on an unseen capacity it
  measures a fresh regime. The decoder labels it, and relate() predicts
  whether its modal matches A1, B1 or neither.

## Review: fixed 79000-block matched pair (proposal, 2026-09-26)

The proposal: --num-gpu-blocks-override 79000, u0.85 and every model
setting unchanged. A parent calibration boot measures the 8 capacity keys
once; those exact records are copied to the fenced namespace; both
measured arms run at 79000 with the same choices and 0 fresh bind
measurements.

Verdict: this is the cleanest supported route. No narrower control exists.
- The b12x Plan override is not passed by vLLM; that would be a source
  change.
- B12X_AUTOTUNE=0 changes every component.
- The records are real, measured and correctly keyed, so none are
  invented. Going down to 79000 is safer than going up to 80927.

Checks and tradeoffs:
1. Calibration boot asymmetry (main concern). A boot that measures at bind
   compiles and benchmarks the losing candidates too, and allocates trial
   state. So the calibration arm differs from a cache-hit arm in resident
   programs, allocation history and startup timing, while the fenced arm
   is a pure cache-hit boot. For a timing-sensitive race, prefer
   calibration as a non-scored boot. Then score A and B (ideally A-B-A)
   as cache-hit boots, both showing "0 measured" at bind. If the
   calibration boot must be scored, add a later parent cache-hit arm to
   check that its rate is not a calibration artifact.
2. Memory condition changes for both arms. The KV pool is one block-major
   allocation of 227840 B per block: 17.27 GiB / 81375 = 227877 B,
   matching the decoded stride. So 79000 instead of ~81.6k holds about
   0.55 GiB less per node. On GB10 unified memory that raises host
   MemAvailable, relative to minima of 0.33 to 1.6 GiB. It is equal
   across arms, but it is a different memory regime from A1/B1/A2. State
   it, and do not pool rates with those arms.
3. The regime at 79000 is a fresh random draw, and the base rate may
   depend on it. Pre-register:
   - the decision rule;
   - a minimum parent event count at 79000 (for example >= 5 nonmodal in
     3000); below that, extend or recalibrate rather than read a
     fenced 0 as informative;
   - the request count. At p = 0.3%, 3000 requests expect 9, so
     P(0 | no effect) is about 1e-4.
4. Seed as a whole byte-identical file, not an 8-record merge. Make the
   fenced namespace file a byte copy of the post-calibration parent
   file, keeping a backup of the current router file. Then sha equality
   is the verification, and no other record can differ. This needs a
   declared replace mode; the seeder currently refuses to overwrite.
5. Post-boot gates for every scored arm:
   - serving blocks == 79000 on all ranks;
   - sparse-MLA ready line "0 measured";
   - claude_compare_selections identical-selections (no new keys);
   - claude_decode_sparse_mla: the regime at 79000 is identical across
     arms and ranks, and no other serving capacity appears after boot;
   - router artifact provenance as for B1;
   - a modal triple equal across the A and B arms. This is expected under
     equal regimes: capture and B1 matched, so it is now a meaningful
     gate, unlike across A1/B1.
6. Stable descriptors. The block stride and page bytes do not depend on
   the block count; only shape[0] does. Confirm with the decoder after
   calibration: all 8 keys at 79000 decode with stride 227840.
7. Contract. It is a declared diagnostic launcher delta: the override
   flag, a distinct arm label and kind, and a new kit digest. It must not
   pass through the unchanged-launch path, and the qualification driver
   must record it as a non-serving profile.

   one-shot consumed receipt appears in the same directory, and the token

   job. Classify with claude_classify_fault_inject.py (rank-0 log,
   response, prompt tokens); PASS requires the injected epoch to be the
   first model fault and the request to be refused.

   snapshot the router selection file before and after. Expect additions
   only, keyed by that boot's capacity, and confirm with
   claude_compare_selections and claude_decode_sparse_mla. vLLM and
   Inductor use the separate INJECT_CACHE directory.
6. Afterwards, restore and verify the router candidate pin, and remove
   or retain the trigger and consumed receipt as the record.

## B2 original-524K trial 0 wrong: review and separation plan (2026-09-26, offline)

Trial 0 (receipts/router-b2-full-qualification-20260926/gates/correctness/original-524k/0.json):
- The request content equals the historical input
  (ds41/r38/.../admission-k7-1m-u80/needle-524288-input.json, sha
  b410c6b1...), with a fresh cache_salt and 0 cached tokens; 524288
  prompt tokens.
- Answer: '510c94b1bb4f42d2998093bd6b9c3d99, 739184'.
- Slot 1: 510 at -0.127 vs 739 at -2.127, a logit gap of -2.0 (bf16
  multiples of 0.125).
- Slot 2, after the identity: 739 at -0.429 vs 482 at -1.054, a gap of
  -0.625. The late code is retrieved (482 at 35%); the choice between
  "initial" and "late" is ambiguous once the identity took slot 1.
- Identity copied exactly (every token about 0). End of sequence after
  739184 at -0.079.

History of this exact input under the original instruction:
- R38 (2026-09-16), cold: 2/16 correct over three arms (gap -7.5 to
  +1.4), and 0/6 under deterministic MoE (gap -4.5 to -0.8). Warm: 1/44.
- This task (historical-524k-600k): 1/3 cold (gaps +1.75, -3.75, -6.5).
- Total cold: 3/25 correct.
- The wrong answers were 'identity, 482617', with slot-2 gaps 482-739 of
  +1.25, +0.625 and +1.125. B2's slot 2 is the same magnitude with the
  opposite sign (orig-cold-3 has exactly 482 -0.429 / 739 -1.054; B2 has
  the mirror). So both slots are near ties, and B2's gaps lie inside the
  historical envelope.
- Replaced final instruction (single-needle wording): 12/12 cold and
  46/46 warm, gap about +4 to +8.
- So trial 0 alone is not evidence of new corruption. Under historical
  rates, 3/3 cold correct on the unchanged gate is unlikely even with
  correct numerics. The gate stays unchanged; that is the user's call.

Separating a stable instruction-selection error from residual numerical
corruption (separately labelled diagnostics, after the gate):
1. Bitwise agreement of the three cold trials (full response signature,
   all top-20 logprobs).
   - They differ: that is a long-context nondeterminism defect in its own
     right, whatever the answer. Candidates: the mHC TF32 projection at
     8192-row chunks, and DSA top-k ties if the tiled selector does not
     cover every width. Report the per-slot gap spread.
   - They are identical: 524K is deterministic on B2, which fixes the
     R38 reproducibility blocker. The answer is then a stable function of
     (build, regime), and rare transient races are excluded for this
     request, but systematic bias is not.
2. Instruction control: run the historical replaced-suffix input
   (historical-524k-600k/resuffix-cold-1-input.json or the R38 524,291
   variant), cold. Expect correct with a gap of about +4 to +8. If it
   holds, retrieval of both codes is intact and the original failure is
   instruction ambiguity. A collapse is a numerical red flag.
3. Fence neutrality: same prompt, unfenced parent, same attention regime
   (R_B1, via the fixed-capacity pair seeded with B2's measured records,
   or a matching boot). With no race, the fence changes no arithmetic, so
   the output should be bitwise identical to B2. Identical means the
   router change did not cause the answer.
4. Regime labelling: the 524K decision is sensitive to sparse-MLA
   precision (boots with different regimes have different modals).
   Compare gaps only within one decoded regime. A bias claim needs a gap
   outside the same-regime envelope. Optionally, an all-bf16-extend
   regime shows whether precision drives the tie (a precision-policy
   question, not corruption).
5. Proof of numerical correctness at 524K is still unavailable: there is
   no independent DS4.1 attention or indexer reference (R38 attribution).
   State that limit rather than infer correctness from passing shorter
   gates.

### Wording correction, and review of diagnose_router_long.py (2026-09-26)

Correction accepted: three identical repeats of one request do not exclude
rare races (a rare race may simply not fire in 3 trials), and a
single-code control gives diagnostic support, not proof that both codes
are retrieved. Report only the observed repeatability and the diagnostic
support.

diagnose_router_long.py (+3 tests pass): sound. Scope guard (3 cold,
identical, wrong), failed exit-code check, status.json stating the
original qualification remains failed with the failed report sha,
same-boot check against the failed run's identity-after (Id,
StartedAt), before/after snapshot equality, and a frozen corpus
(131072 and 524288 keys present, so the probe does not write the corpus;
cold cache_salt with a cache-hit check).
Suggestions:
1. check=True makes a 131072 failure skip 524288. For diagnostic
   coverage, run both, record each exit code, and fail at the end.
2. The final marker contains "PASS". A neutral
   ROUTER-LONG-DIAGNOSTICS-COMPLETE with per-length results avoids any
   misreading next to qualification markers.
3. Tee each child's output into args.out/<length>.log so the receipts do
   not depend on the outer run log.

## Attribution options for the stable 524K dual-needle miss (2026-09-26, offline review)

State: the original unchanged gate gives 3 cold, bitwise-identical, wrong
answers on B2. Historical cold correct on this input is 3/25 (a near tie,
first-slot gap about -3 +/- 3). The 131K long diagnostic was 8/8
identical and correct. Question: a systematic numerical bias versus
model behavior.

Findings that shape the choice:
- The pinned vLLM DS4.1 model has only the B12X attention backend.
  Upstream vLLM (release, and main 4bb804cc5b) has RAM Engram only, so a
  full-model reference does not fit and a port is low yield.
- B12X ships in-image PyTorch references:
  - _shared/mla/compressed_reference.py: pack/unpack of the DS4.1
    cache (SWA E4M3+UE8M0; indexed E2M1+E4M3) and
    compressed_sparse_mla_reference;
  - dsa_indexer/reference.py: paged_decode_logits_reference and
    contiguous_logits_reference, with index-key pack/unpack;
  - mla_compress/reference.py, moe/_shared/kernels/reference.py and
    sequence/engram/reference.py.
  They are CuTe-independent but share authorship and the cache-format
  definition, so they check kernel arithmetic, not the format.
- The decision is one row (the last prompt position) through 43 layers.
  Sparse attention reads only the top-512 selected compressed entries
  plus 128 SWA entries per layer, so exact per-layer inputs for that row
  are small (the indexer additionally needs the request's index keys in
  the 8 index-source layers, tens of MB).

Ranked options:
1. Decision-row reference audit (highest yield). A labelled diagnostic
   image, like the capture kit. For the 524K request's last prompt row,
   capture per layer:
   - the indexer inputs and the request's index keys, then the reference
     scores, top-512 set agreement, the score margin at rank 512, and
     whether the pages holding the identity (tokens about 3-12) and
     "739184" (about 20) are selected in each layer;
   - the attention q, the selected records, the sink and the SWA window,
     then compressed_sparse_mla_reference in fp32 against the kernel
     output (per-layer relative error and direction);
   - optionally the mHC inputs, against a torch mHC reference.
   Optional causal step: substitute the reference attention output for
   the last row only and read the first-token gap. A gap still near -2
   means the decision is not caused by kernel error at the decision row;
   a flip localises an implementation bias. Caveat: KV contents written
   during prefill by the same stack are not validated by this. Extend it
   by recomputing the cache records at the few needle positions from the
   pre-quantization latents captured at those positions.
2. Production-plan kernel conformance (cheapest first step: one GPU on an
   idle node, no model). For the 8 decoded sparse-MLA plans (extend and
   decode, swa/draft/ratio1/ratio2 at the B2 capacity), the indexer at
   524K widths, and the mHC 8192 plan, run kernel vs reference on
   synthetic and adversarial data at exact production shapes and configs,
   including fp8 vs bf16 v41_compute_mode. It shows whether the error is
   at the expected quantization level and unbiased. It cannot see
   data-dependent effects of the real prompt.
3. Kernel cross-implementation on the captured tensors from (1):
   FlashInfer DS4.1 SM12 (source support exists), SGLang DSA indexer
   (b12x already has an sglang boundary test) or DeepSeek's official
   kernels. These are independently authored, and run at kernel scale
   with no Engram.
4. Behavioral controls (support only, not discrimination), separately
   labelled on the current boot: the historical reworded instruction
   (+4 to +8 historically), and a variant that places the identity after
   the code. They show instruction or position sensitivity; they cannot
   exclude bias.
5. Precision-regime sweep at 524K (boots, or fixed-capacity seeding): the
   gap under differing target regimes. It is sensitivity, not a
   reference; useful if (1) localises error to fp8 compute.

Qualification work that can advance while attribution stays open (gate
unchanged, original qualification stays failed, separately labelled
continuations, same-boot identity checks as in diagnose_router_long):
- The remaining dense-battery gates that fail-closed skipped: context dual
  needles at 262000/500000/599936, the concurrent conversations and
  mixed-needle gate, and the final check.
- The Engram fault-injection (fail-closed) run on e06df11a: build, then
  the arm, amendment and restore.
- The cache/concurrency and mixed-load gates, which remain separate. The
  benchmark grid stays unchanged and later.
- The mHC residual: a standalone replay of the TF32 TMA projection at the
  8192 plan with overlap (same design as the gate replay), and a
  prepared, uninstalled one-site fence kit.
- The optional fixed-capacity router attribution pair.
- The R38 issue draft: add that 524K is now bitwise repeatable (3/3
  identical, wrong) on the deterministic image, and 131K 8/8.

## Decision-row audit, milestone 1 (2026-09-26, offline)

Design in claude-decision-row-audit-DESIGN.md.
claude_decision_row_audit.py plus claude_test_decision_row_audit.py
(11 CPU tests). Pinned inputs: claude-pinned-b12x-compressed_reference.py
(a7d7d29b verbatim, sha 123afd75) and claude-ds41-attention-config.json
(a subset of the HF fb2764a5 config, source sha 8be45ce0).

Key verified facts:
- 40 target layers (draft 40-42 excluded); ratios 0/0, 2 x18, 1 x20.
- KV owners 2/8/14/20; 8 index sources; layer 20 produces candidates
  that 24-36 consume.
- The indexer is replicated on every rank, so top-512 must be identical
  across ranks; attention has 16 local heads per rank.
- The MXFP4 score rounding stages come from the production kernel
  source.
- The shipped dsa_indexer/reference.py is the FP8 layout and not usable
  for MXFP4.
- FlashInfer's local SM120 sparse MLA uses a 656-byte layout, not 528;
  it is gated on a lossless repack proof.
- The indexed stream goes through the native map_indexed_pages mapper,
  so capture both the mapped and the logical slots.

Validity hinges on byte-identical response signature equality with B2 in
the same regime (a declared 80927-block override reuses B1's measured
records). Envelope thresholds are provisional until option-2
calibration. Open items O1-O6 must be resolved before any hook code.

### Decision-row audit v2 and hook kit (2026-09-26, prepared, not built)

All six review points fixed:
1. Exactly ranks 0..3 are required. Provenance and identity are split:
   capture meta holds what the process knows; the driver's observed JSON
   holds the rest (regime and full response must equal B2's, B12X tree
   equal). A missing validation is invalid-capture.
2. Selection structure is strict (cardinality, duplicates, range, pool,
   pure -1 tail). The exact deterministic top-k (lowest index wins exact
   ties) is kept separate from the approximate one-ulp band. An
   exact-order-only difference is "unresolved-boundary-order", not a
   pass.
3. The reference gets FP32 q holding the BF16 values; its output is
   asserted FP32.
4. Replicated indexer inputs are hashed per rank (q, scales, weights,
   key pages, cache length, candidates). Input divergence and selection
   mismatch are reported separately.
5. Wording: equal responses show no observed output perturbation.
6. Wording: the envelope is conformance/sensitivity; the verdict is
   within-conformance-envelope.
The scratch-config test was removed.

O1-O6 resolved from source:
- O1: mapped slots are in binding.scratch.mapped_indices after mla.run.
- O2: SWA slots are page*128 + logical%128 (installed ring-fix
  sparse_mla._chunk).
- O3: layer-20 candidates are 8-entry blocks with max, the last-visible
  block forced, top-2048 blocks, expanded.
- O4: index pages are block-table rows with page 256//ratio.
- O5: about 10 MB pinned per rank at arm, plus about 90 MB key pages
  after the latch.
- O6: an opaque custom op, eager at 8192 rows.
The decision chunk is detected from host metadata only.

- honest vLLM tree 2d7fc6ad; B12X tree 03a4e0b3 unchanged;
- installer derived from the fault kit (router base checks, preserved
  inventory).
Tests:
- the capture kit's 11: the unarmed path, targeting, a full simulated

### Decision-row kit: lifecycle and Dockerfile fixes (2026-09-26, prepared, not built)

- **Blocker fixed: the save depended on a later begin() poll,** and
  FULL-graph decode runs no Python hooks. New contract:
  - every byte, including the index-key pages, is staged inside the
    decision forward, on the current stream, after its producer, into
    preallocated pinned storage;
  - keys go through index_select into one preallocated device buffer and
    a non_blocking D2H per owner (layers 2, 8, 14, 20); stream order
    serializes the buffer's reuse;
  - layer 39 records an event and starts a waiter thread that blocks on
    that event only, saves from host memory, writes a one-shot receipt
    and disarms.
  No later forward is needed and no device cache is read afterwards.
  Tests: zero begin() calls after the forward; every cache overwritten
  (block reuse) right after, and the saved bytes equal the pre-reuse
  bytes; an AST check that the waiter touches no kv_cache, k_cache or
  index_select.
- **Dedupe:** by the complete used page-table row plus cache length
  against the owner. A mismatch goes to meta.problems, the entry has no
  key pages, and the audit rejects the capture (test).
- **Graph capture:** begin() checks is_current_stream_capturing() first,
  before any trigger read, arming or allocation (test: a trigger present
  while capturing leaves nothing armed or allocated).
- **Memory at arm:** about 150 MB per rank (10 MB pinned staging,
  100 MB pinned keys, 41 MB device buffer). Budget it against
  MemAvailable.
- **Dockerfile:** the generator template had doubled backslashes (a
  non-raw "\\\\"). Fixed at the source and regenerated. A test requires
  exactly one " \" continuation per line in both generated Dockerfiles,
  a two-line ENV, and a LABEL block ending at the title.
- **Current state:** lock 92287fe2, vLLM tree d1a57d24, B12X tree
  03a4e0b3. Suites: capture 14, audit 16, fault kit 25, all pass.
- **Noted from the user's gate results (policy, not in scope):** the
  mixed-load overlap failed. The first short turns waited 335 s behind
  long ones, consistent with the source default max_parallel_prefills=1.

2. Native/source ancestry: the strongest proof is already the installer

Runtime blockers found and fixed in my helper:
- Hook exceptions would propagate into the serving forward. begin,

Option-2 conformance harness: claude_conformance_mla.py plus
claude_test_conformance_mla.py (8 CPU tests).
- It reproduces each of the 8 production plans: the Caps as in vLLM, and
  a block-major pool of the production block count and stride 227840
  (about 18.4 GB at 80927), so offsets cross 2 GiB.
- It requires the harness plan's selection key to equal the decoder's
  production key (fail closed), and executes the decoded regime's config
  as an override with autotune=False.
- It compares sampled rows (always including row 8191) against the pinned
  FP32 reference, with physical slots resolved independently, and
  requires bitwise run-to-run equality.
- Envelope: E = max(2 x p99.9, max) per compute mode, written to
  <out>.envelope.json.
- Scope text: an empirical conformance/sensitivity envelope; not a model
  oracle; it cannot reveal a bias shared by kernel and reference.
- Its GPU path is untested here; start with --trials 1 --sample-rows 4.

### Conformance harness hardened for GPU execution (2026-09-26, local only)

claude_conformance_mla.py plus claude_test_conformance_mla.py (15 CPU
tests). The capture lock and image are untouched (4391f163, image
a3178f67); the harness is not a capture-kit input.

- **API, checked against a7d7d29b:**
  - _PreparedCompressedSparseMla.run fills swa and indexed page sizes and
    cache_format from the query and validates any explicit value;
  - passing indexed_page_size on SWA-only plans was a latent error; run
    arguments now carry only caches, sm_scale, the sink and out;
  - scratch_specs, bind_for_preparation, PreparedCall(run, output,
    owners) and plan.scratch_specs follow test_compressed_sparse_mla_v41.py.
- **Compile workers:** the production policy (vLLM b12x_prepare:
  B12X_STATE_COMPILE_WORKERS for the attention state stage, else
  B12X_COMPILE_WORKERS, else 8). The value is recorded. It changes
  compile parallelism, not program identity (assumed).
- **Inputs model the decision chunk:**
  - one request at positions 516096..524287; one shared page table of
    unique physical blocks, half beyond the 2 GiB offset, width 2344;
  - SWA windows as in production _chunk, valid entries first;
  - indexed selections are unique and ascending: vectorized stratified
    sampling (one draw per stratum, full coverage), plus 25% clustered
    runs;
  - 1/16 of rows are shorter, never the decision row;
  - records are generated on the device in chunks.
  8192 x 512 selections take well under a second.
- **Determinism:** CPU seeds per slot and trial, and device generators
  for records, all recorded. Each run is repeated on identical inputs
  and must be bitwise equal.
- **Fail closed:**
  - NaN-filled output before each run, requiring an all-finite result
    (catches unwritten positions);
  - non-finite reference or kernel values raise;
  - degenerate reference heads (norm <= 1e-12) are counted, and more
    than --max-degenerate (default 0) fails;
  - a key mismatch or config field mismatch stops the run;
  - on any failure no envelope file is written.
- **Envelope:** max(2 x p99.9, max) per compute mode, plus per plan, with
  sample counts. The scope text says empirical and synthetic (gaussian,
  wide-range, outlier, sink-dominant); not an oracle; blind to shared
  bias; does not bound errors on real activations.

Remaining assumptions:
1. Ratio semantics: visible entries = tokens / ratio and indexed page =
   256 / ratio. The key check confirms only query shapes; a real capture's
   cache_length confirms this.
2. Decode plans (60 rows) are modelled as one request; production decode
   mixes up to 4 requests. The decision uses extend plans.
3. Draft plans use target-style SWA windows (production DRAFT
   indexing differs); this is kernel arithmetic coverage only.
4. The pinned reference is shared with the audit, so the limitation of a
   shared format and rounding definition applies to both.
5. The GPU path has never run here: start with --trials 1 --sample-rows 4.
   It needs about 18.4 GB for the pool plus about 8 GB margin.
6. Cache isolation: run without the /cache mount, or with a scratch
   B12X_COMPILE_CACHE_DIR, so compiled programs and any selection-cache
   reads do not touch the serving namespaces.

### Conformance smoke review, select/restore and distribution review (2026-09-26, read-only)

Smoke (receipts/mla-conformance-smoke-20260926T022446Z): valid as a smoke.
- The harness sha equals the reviewed local file (1d029112). Isolated
  caches: no /cache mount, image ENV cache paths inside the container.
- All 8 production keys matched (fail-closed check passed). Configs =
  R_B1 at 80927. Deterministic, 0 degenerate heads, no failures, exit 0.
- Only trial 0 ran, so only the gaussian distribution. 4 rows x 16 heads
  = 64 samples per plan, and each "p99.9" equals the max.

Per plan, median / max relative L2:
- bf16 plans: swa.extend 0.0022/0.0026; ratio1.extend 0.0023/0.0025;
  ratio2.extend 0.0022/0.0025; swa.decode (chunks 11/12) 0.0027/0.0030.
- fp8 plans: draft.extend 0.024/0.031; draft.decode 0.024/0.028;
  ratio1.decode (chunks 54/12) 0.033/0.040; ratio2.decode 0.034/0.039.
- Floors: pure BF16 rounding of a 512-vector is rel L2 about 0.0017,
  and a single E4M3 rounding about 0.027. So bf16 plans sit about 1.3x
  the BF16 output-rounding floor, and fp8 plans are at the scale of one
  or two E4M3-quantized operands. Nothing exceeds quantization scale.

What follows:
- Under exact production plans, including >2 GiB offsets and split
  merges, the attention kernels agree with the pinned reference at
  quantization scale on synthetic inputs, deterministically.
- This rules out gross layout, offset, split-merge or tiling defects of
  the kind that would show on random data.
What does not follow:
- anything about real activations, the 524K row, the indexer (untested
  here), KV write paths, or a bias shared with the reference or the
  format;
- anything about the wrong answer.

Mode-level envelope obscures plan structure:
- bf16 mode merges single-pass extend (max ~0.0026) with chunked decode
  (0.0030), so E_bf16 = 0.00607 is about 17% looser than the extend plans
  alone need.
- fp8 merges draft extend (0.031) with split decode (0.040).
- The decision row uses only the three bf16 single-pass target extend
  plans. The audit should use the per-slot plan_envelope (layer -> role
  by ratio, mode extend), which the harness already reports; the
  mode-level value is only a fallback.
- Proposed audit change after the full run: accept an envelope keyed by
  slot and select by layer role. Not edited now.

select_decision_row.py (--restore) and distribute_diagnostic.py
(tests: 5 pass): sound.
- Select pins the image and diagnostic identity only, and checks labels

- Restore requires:

  - the parent is the router base and kind;
  - the recorded prior digest, and equality of the other runtime files;
  - the restored manifest == prior_manifest;
  - router labels on all nodes;

- Distribution: receipt inside the kit, a hex image id, BUILD-OK, lock

- Minor suggestion: assert locally that each per-node image json Id
  equals the build image, instead of relying on the remote

- Operational point: selecting the capture image without the declared

  Keep select pending until the capacity decision.

Next highest-yield steps, given attention conformance adds little beyond
known quantization:
1. MXFP4 indexer conformance at production scale (one GPU, no model):
   score and select for the 8 index layers' plans (page 128/256,
   width 2344, candidates at 20/24-36) against the exact rules (the
   production rounding stages, lowest-index tie order, the candidate block
   rule).
   - The selection at 524K is the long-context-specific mechanism.
   - BF16 scores make ties common: every synthetic draw here had an exact
     tie at rank 512.
   - The lowest-index tie rule is an implementation choice
     (determinism-tiled-topk), not the published model's.
2. The decision-row capture, when the capacity is approved, becomes most
   informative for mechanism rather than kernel error:
   - boundary density at rank 512 per index layer;
   - whether the identity and initial-code entries are selected;
   - whether any of them were admitted by tie-break (score == threshold)
     rather than by score margin.
   If the needles' presence depends on tie order, the answer depends on an
   implementation choice that no reference comparison would flag.
3. Cheap behavioral controls on a normal boot, separately labelled:
   identity and code order swapped; a fresh random identity; lengths
   516K/520K/528K for a gap-versus-length trend. These distinguish a
   stable instruction heuristic from length-sensitive numeric drift. They
   support, they do not prove.

## MXFP4 indexer conformance harness (2026-09-26, prepared, not run)

claude_conformance_dsa.py plus claude_test_conformance_dsa.py (12 CPU
tests). The capture lock is untouched (4391f163); neither harness is a
capture-kit input.

- **Plans:** the three production index roles of _declare_index_plan
  (full: layers 2/8/14, page 128; produce: layer 20, page 256, candidate
  blocks 2048; consume: 24-36, page 256, 16384 candidates), with
  num_q_heads 32, topk 512, width 2344, and the prefill plan of the
  decision chunk's final 256-row index chunk (positions 524032..524287,
  one request).
- **Exact production configs without decoding keys:**
  - PreparationSession(autotune=True, cache_only=True), with the
    production namespace and a private copy of the router selection file
    under its identity name (6115b03c...json, tested);
  - a plan whose query differs from production raises LookupError and is
    reported as "not a production plan" (fail closed);
  - the selection source and config are recorded.
  DSA queries are capacity-independent (Caps are width-based, and the
  records never grew across boots).
- **Inputs through production paths:** keys via
  quantize_write_index_k_mxfp4 (256 per call, int64 physical slots) into
  a production-shaped pool (offsets beyond 2 GiB); queries via
  quantize_q_mxfp4; bind/score/select as forward_mqa. The prepare call
  mirrors vLLM _index_call (state.bind, state.layout.scratch_specs(),
  score_mxfp4/select_mxfp4 with state._launchers). The consume role reuses
  the produce role's keys, page table and candidate outputs.
- **Checks:**
  - **layout:** written pages decoded with the audit's layout equal an
    independent E2M1 x UE8M0 quantization. This directly validates the
    audit's key-page assumption;
  - **full kernel score rows** (full/produce) against the published
    rounding stages: fraction bitwise equal, max BF16 ulps;
  - **selection:** structure, exact deterministic equality, outside-band
    count;
  - **layer-20 candidates:** exact against the block rule;
  - **tie exposure (the discriminator):** tie population at the rank-512
    threshold, tie-admitted selections, the change under the opposite
    (highest-index) rule, and position quartiles of tie-admitted entries.
- **Distributions:** gaussian; codebook (64 distinct keys, so heavy exact
  ties); outlier; low-magnitude.
- **Fail closed:** LookupError, non-finite kernel or reference scores, a
  layout mismatch, structural or out-of-band selections, and candidate
  mismatches.
- **Scope text:** synthetic; conformance to the format, rounding stages
  and the image's tie rule; not a model oracle; nothing about the 524K
  answer by itself.
- **Bug caught while testing:** select_reference resolved ties by pool
  order, not by logical index. Production pools are ascending, so it
  would not have shown there, but it is now sorted. The audit's
  deterministic_topk was already position-keyed.
- **Every reference function is cross-checked against the audit's:**
  decode, page layout, published score, deterministic top-k, candidate
  rule.
- **Runtime:** about 18.4 GB pool at 80927 blocks plus about 0.4 GB for
  keys; the reference runs per row on the GPU (FP32 keys held once). The
  GPU path is untested here; start with --distributions gaussian.

### Review: probe_engram_fail_closed.py, start arms, select --kind (2026-09-26, read-only)

Sound, confirmed:
- The probe pins image and lock from the build receipt and the kit digest
  on all 4 nodes, checks idle, uses a fresh 32-hex token (45 characters,
  inside the helper's regex) and a fresh cache_salt, and freezes a
  1024-token prompt (count checked).
- The trigger keys equal the helper's KEYS. It targets rank 3 on kirby
  (dusty0/toby1/rusty2/kirby3). The mtime after import is satisfied
  because scp sets now and podman cp keeps it.
- The receipt name equals the helper's receipt_path.
- The classifier gets the dusty (rank 0) log, the response dict and
  1024.
- Before/after inspects and kernel logs are kept.
- run_node.py has no --rm and no --restart, so exited containers and
  podman logs are retained.
- select --kind engram-fault: the fault lock's base is the router, so
  candidate and restore work unchanged. The fault image labels (kind,
  lock, trees, base-image, base-lock, status) match the checks.
- The start driver's arms add only kind mapping and the same repaired
  profile.

Findings, before arming:
1. (Medium) The receipt identity checks (token, rank, node) raise before
   per-rank logs, inspects and kernel logs are collected, so a
   mistargeted firing loses its evidence. Collect first, then classify;
   record a mistarget as a verdict.
2. (Medium) A client timeout is not always inconclusive. If the consumed
   receipt exists (the skip fired) and no HTTP answer arrives within
   120 s, the fail-closed contract (consumer times out after 5000 ms, rank
   0 refuses the step) was not observed. Report that as a distinct
   failure-indicating verdict (for example NO-REJECTION-WITHIN-DEADLINE).
   Keep INCONCLUSIVE for a timeout without a receipt.
3. (Medium) Evidence timing. After a rejection the engine may still be
   shutting down when logs are read. Poll podman inspect on all 4 until
   not Running (bounded, for example 180 s) or record "still running",
   then collect logs and inspect. A still-running engine after the
   refused step is itself a result to report.
4. (Low) Read the consumed receipt from the host bind path
   (~/.cache/vllm-jj-ds41-tp4/ds41-engram-fault-inject-<token>.consumed)
   as well as podman cp; it does not depend on container state.
5. (Low) Re-check idle immediately before sending. Any other client
   sending >= 1024 tokens after arming would take the one-shot skip.
   That gives a false FAIL or CONFOUNDED, not a false pass, but it wastes
   the token.
6. (Operational) Retaining failed containers: exited containers keep the
   name ds41-flash-karmic-main-tp4, and the next podman run --name fails.
   After archiving, `podman rename` each to ds41-engram-fault-<token> to
   keep them and free the name. Stop any still-running rank gracefully
   (podman stop -t 60, workers first, head last). Never rm -f.
False-pass exposure is low: PASS needs the receipt, the injected epoch as
the first model fault on rank 0, and a refused request (status or
connection error), which is the reviewed classifier.

Per-distribution MLA reporting (local, claude_conformance_mla.py; 17
tests pass):
- per-plan by_distribution summaries and per_mode_by_distribution;
- samples are tagged (distribution, value); raw tagged samples are not
  written out.
The indexer harness (a46beb70..., unchanged) and the capture lock (4391f163)
are untouched.

Probe as executed equals the working copy. All five pre-injection findings

Evidence so far (receipts/engram-fault-injection-20260926T0240, the

Observations to carry into the verdict:

  If they are still running at 180 s, they hold GPU memory and need the

Indexer harness (the user's valid_scores_finite change; 13 tests pass):
- The change is correct: the mxfp4 score clears columns beyond each row's
  cache_length to -inf, so only live prefixes are checked (candidate
  lengths for consume).
- Candidate expansion verified from source (_SortPositions with
  expand_blocks): blocks are bitonic-sorted ascending and expanded to
  block*8 + offset. -1 appears only at positions >= visible, in the forced
  last block (the highest index), so it is a pure tail. The length counts

Remaining risks (not blockers):
- The layout check cannot separate a page-layout error from a
