### Final Engram fault-injection review (2026-09-26, read-only)

The PASS is supported by the evidence (receipts/engram-fault-injection-20260926T0240):
- One firing, on target. Container and host receipts are equal: token
  engram-fault-44b3..., rank 3, kirby, epoch 402, 1024 rows. kirby logged
  "[DS41-ENGRAM-FAULT-INJECT] fired: skipped publication of epoch 402
  (1024 rows) on rank 3 kirby".
- Rank 0 (dusty) had no model fault before the injection. Its first model
  fault is "fault in this step (fault epoch 402, step epoch 402): Engram
  rows were consumed before publication on at least one tensor-parallel
  ...". The next step is also refused ("fault in an earlier step (fault
  epoch 402, step epoch 403)"), which shows the sticky rejection. Then the
  EngineCore sentinel raised, and the APIServer raised EngineDeadError.
- The client got HTTP 500 InternalServerError with no completion after
  11.26 s. The skip fired at 02:40:24.5, and rank 0 rejected at about
  02:40:35.
- Kernel logs were empty on all 4 nodes (no NVRM, Xid or OOM). The classifier
  is the reviewed one (sha 2a64bb7a).
Scope: one injected missing publication on rank 3, with a single 1024-row
prefill job. It says nothing about other ranks, concurrent requests,
decode-time Engram jobs or retrieval quality.

Separate shutdown-behavior finding (not a fail-closed failure):
- The head exited with ExitCode 0 about 24 s after the rejection, so an
  exit code cannot signal the failure.
- Workers toby, rusty and kirby were still running after 180 s and beyond
  (02:43:4x), looping ProcessGroupNCCL watchdog warnings "TCPStore check
  for dump key 'exception_dump' failed (store unavailable...)" once the
  head's store was gone. Remote workers do not self-terminate after the
  head dies; they hold GPU memory until stopped externally.
- When stopping, archive final worker logs again after the stop. The
  archived logs end at collection time and the tail keeps growing.

Indexer conformance: selection checks (claude_conformance_dsa.py, user
version):
1. (Medium) Pool membership is not checked. compare_row flags a
   difference only when it is out of range of the full score vector or
   beyond the one-ulp band. So a selected entry outside the pool whose
   score sits within the band of the threshold is accepted. That covers
   a consume-role entry not in layer 20's candidates, and a full or
   produce entry at a future position (>= visible, inside
   scores.numel()). Add the pool (candidate slice, or arange(visible)) to
   compare_row and fail any valid entry not in it, as the audit's
   selection_structure does.
2. (Medium) There is no repeated-run determinism coverage. score and
   select run once per role and distribution. Repeat bind, score and
   select on identical inputs and require bitwise-equal indices,
   live-prefix scores, candidate outputs and candidate lengths. That is
   the property the R38 atomic-order issue broke, and the deterministic
   tie rule is supposed to guarantee it.
3. (Low) The output canary is missing. indices (and candidate_output) are
   torch.empty, so unwritten slots hold arbitrary values. Fill with a
   sentinel (for example -2) before each run and fail if any sentinel
   remains.
4. (Low) The produce-role candidate check is exact equality with the
   reference, which implies structure, but it reports only a boolean. A
   first-mismatch index or block would make a failure actionable.

### Indexer harness: recorded-config override instead of cache_only (recommendation, 2026-09-26)

Cause of the smoke failure: in a7d7d29b, cache_only=True also requires
compiled artifacts. session._compile raises LookupError "missing required
compiled artifact", the same exception type as a missing selection ("no
completed selection"). So a cache-only session cannot compile in an
isolated cache, and cannot separate the two cases.

Recommended two-phase path, the same principle as the MLA harness (prove
the production key, then execute the recorded config as an override with
autotune off):

Phase A: resolve and verify the production selection (no session, no
compile). This mirrors session._choice_key and session._lookup exactly.
    from b12x.preparation import FrozenMapping, PreparationSession, detect_device
    from b12x.preparation._cache import cache_identity, digest
    from b12x.attention.dsa_indexer._tuning import TUNING as DSA

    payload = json.loads(Path(selection).read_text())
    ident = json.loads(json.dumps(cache_identity(payload['identity']['namespace'], device.index)))
    if ident != payload['identity']:
        raise SystemExit('selection file identity differs from this device/namespace')
    probe = dsa_indexer.plan(caps)                          # the production declaration, no override
    configuration = DSA.configure(probe.query, device=detect_device(device).identity,
                                  override=None, search=True)
    key = digest({'component': DSA.component_id, 'query_schema': DSA.query_schema_version,
                  'config_schema': DSA.config_schema_version, 'semantic_version': DSA.semantic_version,
                  'candidate_contract_version': DSA.candidate_contract_version,
                  'query': configuration.encoded_query.to_dict(),
                  'invocation': probe.invocation.to_dict(), 'pin': None, 'dependencies': []})
    record = payload['records'].get(key)
    if record is not None:                                  # session._lookup semantics
        assignment = FrozenMapping(record['assignment'])
        configuration.space.validate(assignment)
        config = DSA._lower(configuration.query, configuration.device, assignment)
        if DSA.config_payload(config).to_dict() != record['config']:
            raise SystemExit('cached assignment no longer lowers to its saved config')
        source = 'cached'
    else:                                                   # production runs a single legal candidate uncached ('fixed')
        iterator, found = DSA.iterate(configuration), []
        while not iterator.done and len(found) < 2:
            try:
                item = iterator.step()
            except StopIteration:
                break
            if item is not None:
                found.append(item)
        if not (iterator.done and len(found) == 1):
            raise SystemExit(f'not a production selection: no record {key[:12]}')
        config, source = found[0][1], 'fixed'

Phase B: execute the recorded config (compiles in the isolated cache).
    plan = dsa_indexer.plan(caps, override=config)
    with PreparationSession(device=device, autotune=False, compile_workers=compile_workers()) as session:
        session.prepare((plan.request(name=..., prepare_call=prepare),))
    assert plan.selection.source == 'override'
    assert DSA.config_payload(plan.selection.config) == DSA.config_payload(config)
Report per role: key, source (cached or fixed), the record's config and
coverage, and the executed selection source.

Notes:
- No cache_dir or namespace is needed in phase B; override selections are
  never looked up or saved. Keep the container without the /cache mount.
- The key needs the same env as production for query fields that capture
  env (DsaIndexerQuery.exhaustive reads B12X_AUTOTUNE_EXHAUSTIVE, unset in
  production). A mismatch shows up as "not a production selection", never
  as a silent fallback.
- The identity check prevents a selection file from another device,
  namespace or tuning-cache version from being used.
- Keep the user's pool-membership, repeat and canary fixes. They are
  independent of this change.

## Indexer harness: review of the applied two-phase, membership, repeat and canary diff (read-only, 2026-09-25)

Diffed receipts/dsa-conformance-smoke-20260926T025046Z/claude_conformance_dsa.py
(the failed cache_only copy) against the current file, and checked every
B12X call against the pinned tree a7d7d29b (git show, no checkout).
15 CPU tests pass in .venv-snapshot-cpu. No blocking findings; the smoke
can proceed on this file.

Verified against B12X a7d7d29b:
- Phase A key = session._choice_key field for field: component, query_schema,
  config_schema, semantic_version, candidate_contract_version, the encoded
  query from contract.configure (same DeviceIdentity: the session resolves a
  torch.device through detect_device, the harness passes
  detect_device(device).identity), invocation, pin None (override=None so
  configuration.pinned is None), dependencies [] (no request dependencies).
- Identity check = cache_identity round-tripped through JSON, so the tuple
  compute_capability compares equal to the file's list. A different device,
  namespace or B12X_TUNING_CACHE_VERSION fails closed before any lookup.
- Record path = session._lookup: space.validate, _lower, config_payload
  equality against record['config'] (dict to dict here, FrozenMapping to
  FrozenMapping in the session; equivalent, to_dict is plain nested dicts).
- Fixed fallback = the session's rule (iterator complete and exactly one
  candidate; the iterator dedups by equivalence key in both). Stopping at
  two found leaves iterator.done False, so two or more candidates without a
  record raise LookupError. Single-valued spaces never have a record in
  production (the cache loader refuses measured_count <= 1), so looking up
  first is harmless.
- Phase B: plan(caps, override=config) makes configuration.pinned non-None,
  which the session tests before lookup and before the autotune=False
  'default' fallback, so with no cache mounted the only reachable source is
  'override'. Required programs compile through _compile(required=True).
  The post-check on plan.selection.source and config_payload equality is
  the right anchor.
- Membership closes a real gap: the reference scores every decoded key,
  including positions the row must not see, so a future position with a
  high score used to pass the band check. It is now a structure problem.
- Repeats: indices, candidates and lengths bitwise, scores on the live
  prefix per row; the test is red-then-green per key. Canary -2 is distinct
  from the -1 padding.

Non-blocking notes (no edit needed before the smoke):
- Compile-worker policy differs between the two harnesses: the DSA harness
  defaults B12X_STATE_COMPILE_WORKERS to 16, the MLA harness falls back to
  B12X_COMPILE_WORKERS or 8 (the session default). run_conformance_smoke.py
  sets 16 explicitly, so both agree in the smoke; worker count does not
  change programs. Cosmetic.
- A zero-candidate space reports 'no production selection record'; the
  session would say 'no eligible configurations'. Still fail closed.
- Executions 2 and 3 run on scratch warmed by execution 1 (production also
  reuses scratch across layers), so the repeat gate is "identical under
  warm scratch". It does not test for a kernel reading uninitialized
  scratch; that would show as cold-versus-warm difference only on run 1.
- The canary assumes every output slot is written. That holds for these
  shapes (visible >= 524033 > TOPK, 65505 blocks > 2048, 16384 candidates
  >= TOPK), so no padding slot exists. With k < TOPK the check would depend
  on whether the sort kernel writes padding; keep the canary shape-specific.
- valid_scores_finite runs inside execute() and again on the first result
  at the reference step; redundant, harmless.
- selection_file still copies the file into the temp dir although no cache
  is mounted; only the name is used in the report.

MLA receipt mla-conformance-full-20260926T025241Z (per-distribution): FP8
large outliers concentrate in outlier and wide-range inputs, gaussian max
0.043; BF16 max 0.00373. Kernel conformance envelope only; it does not
qualify whole-model accuracy.

## Indexer smoke 030153Z: review and next step for the 524K gate (read-only, 2026-09-25)

Receipt receipts/dsa-conformance-smoke-20260926T030153Z (gaussian, blocks
80927, image a3178f67, 33 s): 3 roles x 256 rows exact, 3 identical
executions each, no canary or membership failures, layout equal.

Findings:
- All three roles resolved as production source 'fixed' with no record:
  the MXFP4 indexer space has exactly one legal candidate (native /
  fused_merge auto / score_tensorcore). The "same as production" claim for
  the indexer therefore rests on the single-candidate space, not on a
  selection record; the file's key lookup could not have matched anything
  (the cache loader refuses measured_count <= 1). Correct and fail-closed,
  but the report should say so.
- full and consume: scores 0 ULP against the published formula. produce:
  4 of 256 rows at 1 to 2 ULP (rows 17, 54, 161, 165), at most ~2 of 524K
  positions per row, no selection or candidate difference. Same config as
  full; page 256 plus block-max fusion is the only difference. Non-blocking;
  worth carrying the rows into the full-run summary so a growth on other
  distributions is visible.
- Tie exposure is large even on gaussian: tie_admitted up to 48/42/24 per
  row, opposite-rule changes up to 22/21/13, tie population up to 68.
  BF16 scores over 524K positions put tens of exact ties on the top-512
  threshold. The kernel resolves them lowest-index-first, bitwise
  repeatable, matching the reference. That is deterministic conformance for
  prefill index chunks (256 rows); decode-mode plans (60 rows) are not
  covered by this harness.
- Session lifetime fix (session owns the whole run_role; __exit__ releases
  plan.prepared so plan.selection became None after the with block) is
  correct; the AST regression test asserts the call shape.

Highest-yield next step for the original 524K answer, without touching the
frozen gate: a pinned decode-precision A/B on the same image and the same
80927 regime. Reasoning: the gate prompt's 524K prefill runs the extend
plans, which are BF16 at 2x the rounding floor; the answer tokens are
produced by the ratio1/ratio2 decode plans, which the regime shows as FP8
split (max_chunks 54, split 12), and FP8 decode is the only place the
conformance runs measured a large deviation (outlier and wide-range tails
0.33 to 0.47). The answer is bitwise identical across 3 cold trials, so one
knob change is a clean test. Three arms, 3 cold trials each, gate prompt
unchanged:
  A: production (fp8 decode), already on file;
  B: fp8 decode with a different legal split (perturbation control);
  C: bf16 decode (v41_compute_mode pinned).
Reading: if B alone flips the answer, the wrong answer is a near-tie that
any perturbation moves (model-marginal, not attention precision). If only C
flips it, and flips it to the correct answer, that is evidence for the FP8
decode tail, still not proof (one prompt). If none flips it, attention
numerics are not the lever and the remaining candidates are model behavior
or non-attention paths (MoE bf16 atomics per the R38 blocker). The
override has to be a visible launch contract (seeded selections through
the existing transfer tooling), never a hidden env change. This needs
nodes and a diagnostic boot: user decision. The 80927 decision-row capture
stays the confirmatory step on real inputs, now that the indexer harness
it feeds has passed.

## Correction to the 524K next-step recommendation (user review, 2026-09-25)

Withdrawn: the decode-precision A/B as first priority. The B2 first token is
already wrong (510 vs 739, gap 2) and it is emitted from the target prefill
forward, so decode-only arithmetic cannot repair the forward that produced
it. FP8 decode may matter for later tokens; not first priority for this
failure.

Also corrected: consume score_max_ulps is null, not 0. The consume role
measures selection equality only (no per-position score comparison), so
"full and consume 0 ULP" above is wrong; full is 0 ULP, consume is
unmeasured. And B2 runs deterministic MoE, so bf16 atomic accumulation is
not an active lever on that arm.

Revised view. Everything that decides the first token is inside the final
prefill chunk: the extend attention plans (BF16, conformance at 2x the
rounding floor on synthetic inputs), the indexer selection at the decision
row (exact on synthetic, heavy boundary ties), the layer-20 candidate
blocks, and the deterministic MoE. The synthetic conformance is an
envelope over chosen distributions; what it cannot say is what the real
decision-row inputs look like: the tie population at the real top-512
boundary, whether the real selection equals the deterministic reference,
and the size of the real attention output perturbation on that row.

So the capture of the actual final-prefill indexer and attention inputs at
80927 remains the best next step, and it is now better founded: the
harness it feeds passed on synthetic inputs, so a deviation on real inputs
would be a real-input finding, not a harness question. What it answers,
without an oracle:
- real-data selection equal to the deterministic reference or not, per
  index source, with the tie population at the boundary; many boundary
  ties on real data means the selected set is index-order dependent by
  design, and an equally valid tie order is a perturbation the model
  never distinguishes (model-marginal at the indexer's score resolution);
- real attention output perturbation on the decision row against the BF16
  reference, compared with the synthetic envelope;
- real candidate blocks at layer 20 against the block rule.
It cannot map a perturbation to the logit gap; that stays the oracle
limit, and the finding stays attribution, not a fix.

Secondary, only if the capture is clean: a prefill-side sensitivity arm
(extend plans pinned to fp8 compute, the only other legal direction) on the
frozen gate, 3 cold trials. It tests whether the first token moves under an
attention perturbation at the envelope scale; it does not establish bias
direction. Visible launch contract via the transfer tooling, user decision.

## Indexer full run 030307Z: independent review of results and numerical limits (read-only, 2026-09-25)

Receipt receipts/dsa-conformance-full-20260926T030307Z (4 distributions x 3
roles x 256 rows, blocks 80927, GB10, 134.9 s, failures []). All 3072
selection rows exact against the deterministic reference, all 3 executions
identical per role, all 1024 candidate lists exact, layout equal in every
role, every role resolved as the single legal 'fixed' config
(native/auto/score_tensorcore).

Per-role score conformance (full and produce only):
  gaussian       full 0 ULP; produce max 2 ULP on 4 rows
  codebook       full 0 ULP; produce 0 ULP
  outlier        full max 1 ULP on 2 rows; produce max 1 ULP on 3 rows
  low-magnitude  full max 1 ULP on 1 row; produce max 16 ULP on row 143
                 (other 3 nonzero rows at most 2)
Bitwise fraction never below 0.99999 (at most about 4 positions per 524K).

Tie exposure (reference-side, the tie population at the rank-512 threshold):
  gaussian       pop max 68, admitted max 48, opposite-rule changes max 22
  outlier        pop max 40, admitted max 26
  low-magnitude  pop max 58, admitted max 43
  codebook       pop max 16465 (about 524288/64 as designed), strictly_above
                 0 in every row, admitted 512, opposite changes 512,
                 all 131072 admitted entries in the first position quartile.
Codebook is the extreme: the entire top-512 is decided by the tie rule, and
the kernel picks the 512 lowest positions among the best codeword exactly
as the reference does, for full, produce (block maxima tie too, so the
2048 candidate blocks are the lowest 2048 blocks) and consume. That
settles the implemented tie rule as lowest-logical-index, deterministic,
across three executions and 3072 rows. On the well-conditioned
distributions about 5 to 9 percent of a 512-entry selection is still
tie-decided (median tie population 25 to 28 on gaussian), because BF16
scores over 524K positions collide at the threshold by construction.

Limits of what this run shows (independent list):
1. Consume scores are unmeasured: only selection equality on the pooled
   16384 candidates. No ULP or bitwise fraction exists for the consume
   plan; a consume-only score drift would show up only if it crossed the
   one-ULP band at the threshold.
2. ULP distance is relative to the score's own magnitude, so it inflates
   where the head sum cancels (weights are randn x 0.05 and can be
   negative, so per-row sums pass through zero). The 16 ULP at
   low-magnitude produce row 143 is consistent with a near-zero result
   under accumulation-order noise, but the receipt keeps only the row
   maximum, not the position, its magnitude, or its distance from the
   threshold. The selection band check is the metric that matters for the
   selection; the ULP number is a rounding-stage indicator only. If the
   metric is refined later, absolute error in units of the threshold's
   ULP would be the better report.
3. Produce is not bitwise against the published formula while full is
   (gaussian, outlier, low-magnitude). Same config; the page-256 path with
   block-max fusion accumulates differently somewhere. No selection or
   candidate effect in 1024 rows. Unexplained, non-blocking.
4. Synthetic inputs: keys and queries are randn-derived, weights randn x
   0.05. Production queries, weights and keys (post-projection, RoPE-free
   indexer inputs) have their own magnitude, sign and correlation
   structure that no synthetic distribution guarantees to cover.
   Codebook covers exact duplicate keys; real repeated passages produce
   near-duplicates whose scores may or may not collide in BF16.
5. Coverage is one shape: prefill mode, 256-row index chunk at positions
   524033..524288 of a single 524288-token request, page-aligned counts
   (no partial trailing page), fixed active width, one request per batch.
   Decode-mode plans (60 rows), the draft, other prompt lengths, partial
   pages and multi-request batches are not covered.
6. Single-candidate space: the selection file could not have contributed
   a record for the indexer; the "production config" claim is that the
   space admits one legal config. The file check still guards identity.
7. The reference is the published rounding recipe, not an oracle. This
   run establishes that the kernels implement that recipe with a
   deterministic lowest-index tie rule; it does not establish that the
   recipe or that tie rule is what the model was trained against.
8. Repeat identity is under warm scratch and warm caches; cold-start
   nondeterminism (uninitialized scratch on the first launch of a boot)
   is not exercised by executions 2 and 3.
9. Three executions bound repeatability at three samples; a rare race
   remains unexcluded.

Consequence for the 524K question: the tie population on real data is now
the specific unknown worth measuring. If the real decision row shows
boundary ties comparable to gaussian (tens of positions), the selected
set is index-order dependent at that scale by design, and an equally
valid tie order is a perturbation the first token could be sensitive to.
The 80927 capture measures exactly this on the real inputs, with the
harness's reference already validated against the kernels on synthetic
data.

## Decision-row capture driver (authored, CPU-tested, not run; 2026-09-25)

New files, all mine: claude_run_decision_capture.py (driver),
claude_needle_spans.py (in-image tokenizer spans), and
claude_test_run_decision_capture.py (14 CPU tests, .venv-snapshot-cpu, pass).
No node was contacted; no kit, contract, start driver or manifest file
was touched.

Phases:
  --phase preflight   read-only: per rank, exactly one running container,
      image == decision-row build receipt (a3178f67) with BUILD-OK, image
      labels kind=decision-row-capture and lock sha == claude-decision-row
      .lock.json == build receipt, container env DS41_DECISION_ROW_BLOCKS=80927,
      DS41_NODE == host, kit label == DS41_KIT_SHA256, B12X namespace under
      /cache and equal to the B2 tuning snapshot's cache_path, boot log line
      "b12x autotuning uses 32 temporary KV blocks before allocating 80927
      serving blocks" (kv_cache_config.num_blocks is the overridden count in
      warmup/b12x_prepare.py at 1794dcf1), token not consumed, endpoint idle.
  --phase capture     preflight; arm /home/jugs/.cache/vllm-jj-ds41-tp4/
      claude-decision-row.json on every rank host (atomic write, token
      decision-row-<utc stamp>); one request identical to
      qualify_original_needle.py (frozen input sha 1b89c7f4, prompt sha
      b410c6b1, temperature 0, max_tokens 64, logprobs, top_logprobs 20,
      thinking False) with a fresh uuid4 cache_salt; retained trial.json
      (request, complete response, elapsed) and trial-cache.json; cold
      required (cached tokens 0, else fail closed); response row with
      prepare_decision_reference.signature versus the B2 signature; wait for
      the four <token>-rank<k>.consumed receipts (bounded); helper log lines;
      live selection file per rank from the host cache path (no podman exec),
      exact_regime(80927) per rank, all four must agree; observed.json
      (image, kit, source trees from image labels, regime, signature) and
      expected.json (from receipts/decision-row-b2-reference.json); gather
      ranks 1..3 to dusty by rsync from 10.11.11.{6,5,8} after an ip route
      check (dev enp1s0f0np0, prefsrc 10.11.11.7), sha256 verified against
      each consumed receipt, originals untouched; summary.json.
  --phase audit --receipt <dir>   requires dusty to run no container
      (serving stopped and retained) unless --while-serving; copies the audit
      kit, expected/observed, the MLA envelope
      (mla-conformance-full-20260926T025241Z: bf16 0.00697, fp8 0.539) and
      the frozen input to the dusty receipt dir; runs two CPU-only
      containers of the capture image (no --device, --network=none,
      --memory=24g by default): needle spans (HF cache mounted ro, tokenizer
      of snapshot fb2764a5, token count must be 524288 and the late marker
      must start at the input's recorded 366986 +-1), then
      claude_decision_row_audit.py with --spans; audit.json and spans.json
      copied back; verdict printed.

Safest audit path: a separate CPU-only container of the capture image on
dusty after stop_diagnostic.py, not podman exec into the serving container.
Reasons: exec shares the serving process namespace and memory cgroup, adds
a second torch process beside a 0.85-utilization engine on unified memory
(the NV_ERR_NO_MEMORY history on this fleet), and the capture files are
already on disk before the stop, so nothing is gained by auditing live.
--while-serving exists only for an explicit user decision. --memory on
rootless podman needs cgroup v2 memory delegation; if podman refuses it,
rerun with --audit-memory none.

Launcher change reviewed (launch_contract.py, run_node.py,
start_moe_repaired.py, user-owned): env pin accepted only as exactly 80927,
adds --num-gpu-blocks-override 80927; run_node refuses the env unless the
candidate's diagnostic kind is decision-row-capture; the start driver
restricts --decision-row-blocks to --arm decision-row and unsets an
inherited DS41_DECISION_ROW_BLOCKS before applying the explicit one; an
ordinary launch renders no override (test_launch_contract). vLLM 1794dcf1
kv_cache_utils.may_override_num_blocks replaces num_blocks with the
override and adjusts available_memory to match, so the serving allocation
and the b12x selection key (cache shape) both see 80927. No finding.

Captured fields versus tie attribution: sufficient for the inputs (rank 0
key_pages of owners 2/8/14/20, q_data, q_scales, weights, cache_length,
topk, layer-20 candidates and candidate_len; ranks 1..3 hash the same
inputs). The gap is in reporting: audit_indexer returns exact/band and a
boundary_count only. For attribution it should also report, per index
source: tie population at the rank-512 threshold, tie-admitted count,
strictly-above count, the opposite-rule (highest-index-first) selection
difference, and needle coverage under both tie rules (spans). That is a
small addition to my audit file with CPU tests; recommended before the
audit phase, on request.

Blockers and caveats flagged:
1. Arming allocates about 150 MB pinned per rank on the first eager forward
   after the trigger, which is chunk 1 of the capture request itself; check
   MemAvailable in the telemetry before sending.
2. The trigger is read at most once per second by begin(); the driver arms
   all four hosts before sending, sequentially, so no race with the request.
3. If a rank's helper reports problems in its consumed receipt, the driver
   still gathers; the audit returns invalid-capture. The token is single
   use; a rerun needs a new capture phase (new token), not a restart.
4. Spans need a fast tokenizer with offsets in the image venv; if the
   DeepSeek tokenizer has no fast implementation, rerun with --no-spans.
5. The preflight's boot-log check greps the serving container log server
   side; a rotated or truncated log fails closed.
6. Nothing here validates the KV or index-key bytes of the 63 earlier
   chunks; the audit's stated limit stands.

## Hardened capture driver: read-only review before the 524K request (2026-09-25)

Reviewed claude_run_decision_capture.py after the user's hardening (kit
digest and explicit repaired env checks, pre-request selection match,
failure-finally evidence, audit JSON copied on nonzero exit). 14 driver
tests pass. Generated gather script passes bash -n; the fabric route
assertion runs correctly on a sample route.

Actionable:
1. Failure-evidence gate (fix before launch): the finally block runs
   `podman logs --since "YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM:SS UTC"`. Verified locally with
   the workstation podman: that form fails with "unable to interpret time
   value", so every <node>-final.log would hold only the journal and the
   meminfo, never the container tail. podman accepts RFC3339
   ("2026-09-26T03:15:00Z"); journalctl accepts the space form. Use two
   strings, or `podman logs --tail 500` plus the journal.
2. Audit exit path (minor): on a nonzero audit exit (deviation-found 1,
   invalid/unresolved 3) audit.json is copied but SystemExit fires before
   the verdict is printed; print the verdict from the copied JSON first.
3. Spans container (minor): add -e HF_HUB_OFFLINE=1 -e TRANSFORMERS_OFFLINE=1
   under --network=none so a tokenizer load never waits on a hub probe.
4. Pre-arm host memory (low): arming allocates about 150 MB pinned per rank
   on chunk 1; meminfo is only collected afterwards. A pre-arm MemAvailable
   read per node (fail closed below a few GiB) is cheap.

Verified, no change needed: kit_digest() equals the container kit label
only if the local runtime files are unchanged since launch (intended);
lock trees {b12x, vllm} equal the image label pair; upstream launch
carries B12X_DENSE_SPLITK_TURBO=1 which the decision-row arm overrides to
0 and VLLM_DS41_L2_PREFETCH is absent, so the explicit env checks match a
correct boot; pre-request exact_regime(80927) per rank against the B2
reference blocks arming on any drift; summary.json is written before the
fail-closed raise; selection files are about 570 KB (ssh cat is fine);
host_path rejects paths outside /cache/claude-decision-row.

## Capture abort diagnosed and repaired (2026-09-26, no nodes)

Receipt decision-row-capture-20260926T034234Z: every rank logged "armed",
then "aborted token=... IndexError: index 8191 is out of bounds for
dimension 0 with size 128", then a second abort with token=None. Two
aborts per rank means the first failing layer had two hooks: an index
source with 128 rows, i.e. layer 20. Layers 0..19 had captured normally.

Root cause (source, vLLM 1794dcf1): ced.py ced_decoder_start = 20; layers
20..39 are CED decoders; in prefill the model gathers only the last
CED_WINDOW = 128 rows of the request at the boundary and
attention._query_metadata returns metadata.decoder (128 tokens) for those
layers while the original SWA metadata (which begin() checks) still says
8192. Every hook used the absolute row 8191: q/output/swa_indices/
swa_lengths/top_lengths/page_table/mapped_indices/topk_indices_buffer in
attention(), iq_data/iq_scale/iw/cache_lengths/topk/candidates/
candidate_lens in indexer(), and metadata.positions[8191] in position().
The same assumption sat in the test fixture, which expanded every tensor
to 8192 rows for all 40 layers, so the CPU tests could not see it.

Repair (claude-decision-row-capture.py, schema v3): rows resolved per
layer via layer._query_metadata; expect() requires one prefill request
with exactly 128 rows for is_ced_decoder layers and 8192 otherwise and
stages positions[rows - 1]; every hook indexes rows - 1 and checks the
leading dimension of q, output and the index query; save() records
ced_decoder/query_rows/row/position per layer and reports any position
that is not prompt_tokens - 1; _abort writes <token>-rank<k>.aborted.
Audit: SCHEMA v3, layer_table gains ced_decoder (ced_decoder_start rule),
validate checks the per-layer geometry and position. Tests: fixture now
models the decoder view (metadata.decoder, _query_metadata, 128-row
tensors for layers >= 20) plus three regressions (decoder layers capture
row 127 of distinct-row tensors; wrong geometry aborts without saving and
leaves the .aborted receipt; decoder rows not ending at the decision
position are reported). 22 capture, 20 audit, 14 driver tests pass.
Recipe regenerated: lock 0829cd26 (vLLM tree 1b8b396b, B12X unchanged
03a4e0b3); the image must be rebuilt, the build receipt and BUILD-OK will
then name the new lock, and the driver's preflight will refuse the old
image until then. No model or kernel numerics touched; hooks are copies.

Offline selection comparison, capture boot (80927) vs B2 (81592):
claude_compare_selections on dusty and toby: all 1098 common records
identical (config, assignment, coverage, programs); the candidate has 8
new records = the 80927 sparse-MLA family, whose decoded configs equal
B2's. So the different wrong answer (identity + late code vs B2's identity
+ early code) is not a tuned-selection difference. It is either the KV
geometry itself (page placement, block count) or the hooks (this run had
copies active on layers 0..19 of the decision chunk before the abort), and
those cannot be separated without an unarmed request on the same boot.

Separating fixed-capacity behavior from hooks (recommendation): on the
rebuilt capture boot, before arming, send the frozen request cold twice
(fresh salts): U1 and U2 must be identical (same-boot repeatability, the
analogue of B2's three identical trials; without it an armed difference is
uninterpretable). Then arm and send once: A. Read: A == U means no observed
output perturbation by the hooks; U vs the B2 signature is the capacity/
boot effect with all selections equal. The audit's "same response" limit
statement then refers to U, not to B2; the B2 gate and the accuracy gate
are untouched (both still failed, and U being wrong in a different way is
itself a finding: the answer at this row is boot-geometry sensitive with
identical plans, which is consistent with a near-tie decision). This needs
a control identity in the audit (observed signature vs same-boot unarmed
signature, B2 signature equality reported as information); I can add
that to claude_decision_row_audit.py with tests on request. Driver
changes (two unarmed trials, control.json) are the user's.

claude_decision_row_audit.py: compare_identities(expected, observed,
control) and audit(..., control=None); CLI --control. With a control the
capture must equal it on all five identity keys; B2 regime and B12X tree

## Needle spans: pinned deepseek_v41 tokenizer path (2026-09-26, no nodes)

Capture 041910Z completed on all four ranks with no capture problems; both
unarmed controls and the armed response share signature a1da3081 (identity +
early code, still wrong), B2 diverges after record 13. The offline audit
stopped before arithmetic: claude_needle_spans.py called
AutoTokenizer.apply_chat_template and this checkpoint has no chat_template.

Cause, from the retained launch identity and the pinned sources: the
serve arguments in upstream-launch.json carry --tokenizer-mode deepseek_v41
and --default-chat-template-kwargs thinking=true, reasoning_effort=high.
At vLLM 1794dcf1 that mode maps to vllm/tokenizers/deepseek_v41.py, which
wraps the HF fast tokenizer (TokenizersBackend) and renders the prompt in
Python with vllm/tokenizers/deepseek_v41_encoding.py; apply_chat_template
returns the served ids (tokenize defaults to True, encode with
add_special_tokens=False). The renderer registry maps deepseek_v41 to
DeepseekV4Renderer, which passes the merged chat kwargs straight through;
merge_kwargs lets request keys override defaults, so the frozen request
renders in chat mode (thinking False) and the effort prefix is not emitted.
The historical late position (qualify.py) came from /tokenize on the text up
to the newline before the late marker, so it counts BOS, the user token, the
prefix text and the trailing assistant header tokens.

Helper rewritten (claude_needle_spans.py): imports DeepseekV41Tokenizer from
the image's pinned tree (/opt/jovian-judgement/vllm, the launch contract's
PYTHONPATH entry, asserted via vllm.__file__), renders with the served kwargs
merge, and fails closed unless: served ids equal the offsets pass ids; the
prompt has exactly the historical count; every marker maps to one contiguous
span; rendering the historical before_late message reproduces the recorded
prefix count exactly; and the served ids share that prefix up to the late
marker start, or one token less when the newline is its own token. No
template is invented and no offset is approximated. Identity (encoder file
sha256s, kwargs, counts, prompt sha256, tokenizer class) is printed as
NEEDLE-SPANS-IDENTITY; spans.json keeps the pure {marker: [start, end]} form
the audit expects. Tests in claude_test_run_decision_capture.py model the
wrapper (20 pass): launch defaults pinned to upstream-launch.json, split and
merged newline cases, prefix derivation, historical input assumptions, and a
guard that the helper has no AutoTokenizer or chat_template fallback.

Not verified here: the CPU import of vllm.tokenizers.deepseek_v41 inside the
image (it pulls in vllm.entrypoints.chat_utils and vllm.config); the pinned
platform resolver falls back to UnspecifiedPlatform without a GPU, which is
an import-time debug message, not an error, but no CPU run of that import
exists in the receipts. The core audit does not need spans: needle coverage
is only computed when --spans is given and its summary key is excluded from
the verdict, so --phase audit --no-spans yields the same verdict now and a
later spans run can be added as a further attempt directory.

## Independent review of decision-row-capture-20260926T041910Z and next discriminator (2026-09-26, no nodes)

Receipt review (read-only). audit.json: verdict within-conformance-envelope,
calibrated envelope (bf16 0.00697, fp8 0.539), no problems, cross-rank inputs
and selections equal at all eight sparse layers, all attention rows within
bound, eight exact selections. Needle coverage at the captured decision row:
every sparse layer selects the entries holding the identity, the early code,
the late marker and the late code under both tie rules; the tie-admitted
entries never touch a needle entry. Spans identity: 524288 prompt tokens,
prefix 366986 reproduced exactly, shared prefix 366984 (newline token
separate, header two tokens). Same-boot control identity all equal; B2
response signature differs, regime and B12X tree equal.

Logprob comparison across B2 original-524k/0.json, 034234Z and 041910Z:
the first-token record is identical in all three (510 at -0.127, 739 at
-2.127, same top-20 to the printed precision) across two boots of two
images and two KV capacities (81592 and 80927). Divergence after record 13
lies in far-tail top-20 alternatives at -13 to -25 nats (about 0.1 to 0.5
nat drift) and at record 23, the early-versus-late code choice, which is a
near tie: B2 739 -0.429 / 482 -1.054, 041910Z 739 -0.474 / 482 -0.974,
034234Z an exact 0.5/0.5 tie that fell to 482. That is the whole B2
mismatch. It is not attributed here, but its magnitude is a boot-to-boot
drift of a few tenths of a nat, far below the 2.0-nat identity-first margin.

Existing evidence that already discriminates: (1) the frozen structure with
a fresh identity hex at 524285 tokens on B2 (mixed-long-needle, which ran
alone; overlap was not observed) answered 739184, 482617 correctly;
(2) fresh-hex dual-code prompts at 262000, 500000, 599936 were correct;
(3) the historical R38 boot answered the frozen prompt correctly once
(+1.75) and wrongly twice (-3.75, -6.50), so the pre-determinism stack
moved this first-token margin by about 8 nats between cold trials, while
the deterministic B2/capture stack lands on -2.0 every time. So the
failure is specific to this prompt's content, with all selections and
attention arithmetic conforming at the decision row; the remaining unknown
is whether the -2.0 is the model's own preference for this text or a
systematic local bias. Local nondeterminism cannot explain it (equal across
boots); only a shared systematic effect can, which the reference audit
cannot see by construction (LIMITS item 1). No local bug discriminator is
left for that question without an independent whole-model reference.

Independent reference options checked in the pinned tree: on capability
family 120 vllm/models/deepseek_v41/__init__.py routes unconditionally to
vllm.models.deepseek_v4_1, which requires B12X attention and holds the only
disk Engram (b12x disk_table). The FlashInfer SM120 / FlashMLA / mega-MoE
implementation in deepseek_v41/nvidia is unreachable on GB10 without source
edits and has no disk Engram (475 GiB safetensors, 74.6 GiB weights per
rank resident, tables on disk). A local reference adaptation therefore means
porting disk Engram into that path and qualifying an untested SM120 kernel
stack at 524K: engineering days, not a quick discriminator, and it still
shares tokenizer, model code and checkpoint loader.

Recommendation: run the exact frozen request against an external reference
serving the official DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash weights (thinking off, temperature
0, max_tokens 64, logprobs if offered, three repeats), after verifying the
provider's context limit covers 524288 input tokens. Needs user approval:
external transmission of the synthetic 3.67 MB prompt and cost. Parallel or
fallback local step on a boot of the retained capture image: a hex/length
sensitivity sweep (about eight fresh identity hexes padded to exactly
524288 via /tokenize as qualify.py does, plus the frozen hex at 524285 and
524291), top-20 logprobs, cold salts; needs boot approval and a small probe
script in my files. Not recommended: any stock or FlashInfer launch.

## Sensitivity preflight fail-closed: 81389-block regime differs from B2 (2026-09-26, no nodes)

needle-sensitivity-20260926T045954Z stopped in construct-only preflight:
automatic KV gave 81389 serving blocks (B2 81592, B1 80927) and the decoded
ratio2.extend plan is v41_compute_mode fp8 on all four ranks while the B2
reference has bf16; the other seven plans match. Evidence for the mechanism:
the live selection file holds all 1098 B2 records byte-identical and 16 new
records (8 decode to the 81389 sparse-MLA family; 8 more have the same
config shape at keys my decoder does not label, so their capacity and role
are undetermined until decoded); the 81592 and 80927 families are still
present and unchanged. So the cache was not altered; the selection
is capacity-keyed and each new automatic block count triggers fresh timing
races. compressed_sparse_mla/_tuning.py offers ("fp8", "bf16") for the V4.1
cache format with fp8 as the default at index 0; session.py picks the
minimum median race latency with ties broken by candidate index. Latencies
are not stored in the record (coverage: 2 legal, 2 measured), so how close
the race was is unknown. The block count moved with profiled available KV
memory: 17.3 GiB on this boot against 17.37 GiB on the B2 boot (equivalent
utilization 0.8319 against 0.8327). The mechanism behind that variance is
not measured and not attributed here. Correction: this boot did have
startup NV_ERR warnings (dusty 67, toby 26, rusty 31, kirby 76 per the
user's collection); my earlier statement that it had none was wrong.
A pinned selection exists only as a caller override in
the preparation contract (Plan.override); no environment or file pin, so
restoring bf16 at 81389 would need a source or cache change, and a fixed
block count for the router image needs a launcher change. Both are separate
approvals. The MLA envelope makes the difference material: fp8 plans reach
0.54 relative L2 against 0.007 for bf16 on synthetic inputs, so this boot's
ratio-2 prefill arithmetic is not numerically the B2 regime.
Recommendation recorded in the reply: run the probe as a within-boot
experiment after replacing the B2-regime equality gate in my probe with a
cross-rank equality plus pre/post stability requirement that records and
names the regime difference; no B2 or capture-boot numerical equivalence may
be claimed; the frozen control's first-token record on this regime is a
compute-mode observation confounded with capacity and boot.

## Causal chunking discriminator: source plan (2026-09-26, read-only, no nodes)

Alignment series 053026Z so far (this boot, 81389 blocks, ratio2.extend
fp8): 524287 +7.875, 524289 +3.625, 524032 +4.75 all correct; 516096 wrong
identity+late -1.25; control 524288 +0.75. With 8192-token chunks the last
chunk holds 8191, 1, 7936, 8192 and 8192 rows respectively. Every small or
negative margin observed so far (524288 on four boots, 516096) has a last
chunk of exactly 8192 rows, equal to max_num_batched_tokens and to the
prepared extend plan's query_rows; 524287 differs from 524288 by one filler
token and one row and carries +7.9. Suggestive only: each length changes
content and positions after the marker.

Chunk-size keys, from the pinned sources: every B12X prefill key derives
its capacity from scheduler_config.max_num_batched_tokens (b12x_layers
_capacity, attention.py capacity, compressor capacity, engram max_tokens,
MhcQuery/MlaCompressQuery max_tokens, MoE num_tokens, sparse-MLA
query_rows/max_batch). Changing the budget to 7936 would rekey every
prefill plan and start fresh timing races for all of them, not only the
eight capacity-keyed sparse-MLA plans: an uncontrolled confound. The
scheduler's --long-prefill-token-threshold caps num_new_tokens per step
(scheduler.py 1018 and 1563, chunk ends aligned down to 256-token blocks
at 575-580) and is referenced nowhere in the DS4.1 model or B12X code, so
it changes the chunking with every prepared capacity, key and cached plan
unchanged. 7936 = 31 blocks, block aligned and above the 128-row CED
window; chunk rows 7936 < query_rows 8192 passes the rows guard. CED: every
prefill chunk keeps only its last 128 rows in the decoder layers
(_stage_ced keep_full is false unless prompt logprobs are requested), so
chunking moves the decoder row sets and replay starts; with 7936 the frozen
prompt ends in a 512-row chunk (window 128, decoder row still last), and no
chunk ever equals the plan capacity. A 4096 threshold keeps every chunk,
including the last, full at the threshold while none equals capacity, so
the pair separates "last chunk equals the threshold" from "chunk equals the
prepared capacity". Preserving the eight plans across the boots needs the
KV pin 81389 (this boot's cached family, fp8 ratio2.extend); the launcher
accepts only 80927 and only for the capture image kind, so both the pin
value and the threshold are separately approved launcher changes
(launch_contract.py, runtime.py kind guard, start_moe_repaired.py arm).
A same-boot alternative without launcher changes: shift the chunk grid via
a same-salt prefix-cache hit. Arm A warms the first 8192 tokens (chunk grid
unchanged, last chunk 8192); arm B warms the first 256 tokens (last chunk
7936); both on the current boot with identical token ids and plans, cached
tokens verified exactly. The cache-hit path is shared by A and B and is
compared against the cold control. Suggestive, since the hit path itself
differs from cold. Neither design distinguishes a defect from an expected
floating-point difference by itself; only a direction-consistent, large,
structure-locked change (chunk == capacity) versus unchanged margins under
4096 would point at geometry rather than arithmetic, and precision is not
chosen by accuracy in any arm.

and compares to the baseline for all ranks). The start driver restricts
--prefill-threshold to router-fence without capture or tracing, clears
inherited controls, names receipts chunk<N>-blocks81389, and still runs
the short repeatability corpus after boot. Both flags exist in the pinned
vLLM parser, so check_cli parses the rendered argv. Local kit_digest equals
the stated 67a1de44. No blockers found in the kit.
Probe defects found by the user and confirmed against the retained
router-fence-20260926T045016Z logs: the API server's non-default args line
is a repr with enum and object values (not a literal) and only the head
rank prints it (headless workers start no API server). Repaired: the head
line is read by key pattern only (no eval), required on rank 0; every rank
is verified through the rendered-profile JSON line runtime.py prints before
exec (argv with the two flags, capacity 8192, seqs 4, chunked prefill, env
controls, no capture pin, node identity) plus the 81389 serving-block line.
Tests use the retained dusty-final.log and toby-startup.log as fixtures.
14 chunking tests, 61 total across my three probe suites, plus the user's
4 contract tests, pass.

## Chunking arm 7936 reviewed (2026-09-26, read-only, no nodes)

Boot router-fence-chunk7936-blocks81389-20260926T112822Z: every rank
rendered --long-prefill-token-threshold 7936, --num-gpu-blocks-override
81389, --max-num-batched-tokens 8192; head API line agrees; 81389 serving
blocks allocated on all ranks with 17.37 to 17.44 GiB available KV (the pin
fits). Correction: the boot's *-final.log kernel journals carry startup
NV_ERR_NO_MEMORY counts dusty 66, toby 26, rusty 28, kirby 56 (no Xid); my
earlier "0 NV_ERR" read the *-startup.log, which is container stdout only,
so startup was not a clean kernel pass. Repeatability 256/1024 identical
and correct. Probe receipt chunking-7936-20260926T113308Z: regime
pre and post byte-identical to the pinned 045954Z snapshot, six cold rows
(cached 0), token-id digests recorded, controls share signature 7859300d,
collections exit 0, no NV_ERR/Xid/OOM in the window, MemAvailable 1.51,
1.72, 1.00, 1.61 GiB.
Rows (7936 chunking, last chunk rows in parentheses) against 8192-chunking:
  control 524288 (512)  correct +1.50  (was +0.75)
  516096  (256)         wrong identity+late -2.75 (was -1.25)
  fresh1 524288 (512)   wrong identity+late -2.38 (was -3.50)
  532480  (768)         wrong identity+late -3.00 (was -2.25)
  524287  (511)         correct +4.25 (was +7.88)
Every answer is unchanged with identical token ids; margin deltas are
+0.75, -1.5, +1.1, -0.75, -3.6 with mixed signs. Under 7936 no chunk equals
the prepared capacity and none of these prompts ends in a full chunk, yet
the three that failed with a full 8192 last chunk still fail and 524287
still passes. Bounded conclusion: unchanged answers under a different chunk grid
disfavor chunk fullness (last chunk full, chunk equal to the prepared
capacity) as a sufficient explanation of these outcomes; they do not rule
out every CED or numerical issue, since the grid change moved margins by
up to 3.6 nats and the 4096 arm is still pending. The 053026Z length
correlation is within that movement, so it is not evidence of a boundary
effect on its own. What remains is a length- and content-dependent
sensitivity of the first-token decision (one filler token still moves it
by 2.75 nats under 7936) whose origin, model behavior or a
chunking-independent numerical bias of this stack, this test does not
separate; other local A/Bs may still be designable, none is proposed here,
and no numerical change is proposed from answer accuracy. The cross-boot family shift (B2 bf16 -2.0 versus fp8 family
+0.75/+1.5 on the frozen prompt) stays an observation. The historical
524288 finding stays unresolved.

## Chunking arm 4096 reviewed; both arms against the saved 8192 inputs (2026-09-26, read-only, no nodes)

Boot router-fence-chunk4096-blocks81389-20260926T115917Z: every rank
rendered threshold 4096, override 81389, capacity 8192 (head API line
agrees); 81389 serving blocks on all ranks, 17.27 to 17.41 GiB available;
kernel journals in *-final.log carry startup NV_ERR_NO_MEMORY dusty 75,
toby 37, rusty 35, kirby 65, no Xid (not a clean kernel startup, as with
every boot in this series); repeatability 256/1024 identical and correct.
Probe chunking-4096-20260926T120422Z: regime pre and post byte-identical to
the pinned 045954Z snapshot; identities equal to the 7936 arm apart from
the threshold; token-id digests of all six prompts equal the 7936 arm's;
six cold rows (cached 0); controls share signature 0f65a276; request
window clean on all ranks (collections exit 0, no NV_ERR/Xid/OOM);
MemAvailable at the end 0.56, 1.65, 0.88, 1.69 GiB (dusty low).
First-token margins (nats) by chunk grid, identical token ids and plans:
                      8192     7936     4096
  frozen 524288      +0.75    +1.50    +0.00  (exact bf16 tie, greedy -> identity, wrong)
  frozen 516096      -1.25    -2.75    +2.38  (wrong, wrong, right)
  fresh1 524288      -3.50    -2.38    +1.50  (wrong, wrong, right)
  frozen 532480      -2.25    -3.00    -3.63  (wrong throughout)
  frozen 524287      +7.88    +4.25    +4.50  (right throughout)
All reported margins lie on a 0.125 lattice; that is an observation about
the logprob differences only, and no absolute raw-logit magnitude or ulp
count is inferred from it (a raw-logit receipt would be needed).
Established (bounded): within-boot determinism holds in every arm; on
identical token ids with identical B12X plans, three of five prompts change
their greedy answer with the prefill chunk grid, in both directions: the
frozen control moves by -0.75 from +0.75 to an exact tie that greedy
resolves to the identity, 516096 by +3.6 and fresh1 by +5.0 to the correct
side, while 532480 and 524287 keep their answers with moves of -1.4 and
-3.4. Chunk fullness is therefore not a sufficient explanation, and the
grid is not a semantically neutral parameter of this stack at the level of
these decisions. Not established: whether that sensitivity is
accumulated numerical drift of expected size for 524K tokens through 40
layers or a structural dependence; no accuracy attribution; no model or
kernel fault; precision is not chosen by accuracy.
Source reading (pinned deepseek_v4_1): at the CED boundary the global
compressed KV for decoder layers is built from every encoder row
("Decoder global KV needs every encoder row, but decoder queries, residual
updates and experts only need the selected replay rows"), decoder rows are
the last 128 of each chunk, and decoder SWA specs use a bounded 128-row
replay window with prefix caching disabled. That structure does not by
itself imply a chunk-grid dependence of the final row's decoder path, so
the mechanism is open; the encoder side sees the grid only through
numerics (compressor state across chunk boundaries, attention batch
composition, MoE row batching).
Highest-value next step (local, no numerical change, needs approval): a
per-layer divergence profile at the decision row between two chunk grids
with the existing decision-row capture kit: two boots of the capture image
with the chunking controls and the 81389 pin (launcher currently admits
the controls only for the e06 kind and the 80927 pin only for the capture
kind), 8192 and 4096, the frozen input once each after two unarmed
controls; report across-grid activation differences descriptively (per-layer norms
and relative differences of the captured inputs, the earliest layer where
they diverge, whether index selections differ), never as pass/fail against
the same-input operator-conformance envelope, which applies only to each
captured operator checked separately against its own reference on its own
inputs. Fallback with two confounds (blocks, precision family):
a single 4096 capture against the retained 041910Z capture. Not proposed:
any numeric change fitted to these prompts; a prompt-logprobs (keep_full)
replay was considered and set aside for memory risk at 524K.

## Review: compare_decision_grids.py and the 4096 capture prerequisite (2026-09-26, read-only, no nodes)

compare_decision_grids.py (user): CPU-only, weights-only torch.load,
descriptive per-layer deltas of q/out/attn_sink (L2 norms, changed
elements, max abs, relative L2 to left), indexer query (decoded mxfp4),
weights, top-k and candidate set/order deltas, key-page hash equality,
earliest differing captured layer, explicit scope and limitation lists,
raises on schema/geometry/identity/plan mismatch. Plan records in the hook
are {mode, config} only, so plan equality across two boots holds exactly
when the pinned regime holds. Findings: (1) candidates are compared over
the whole buffer; the audit slices them to candidate_len, so the padding
tail may hold stale entries and selection_delta would raise or report false
differences; slice to candidate_len and report both lengths. (2) Report two
earliest indices, first layer where q differs and first where out differs:
q equal with out different at a layer means the difference entered through
attended context (KV written by other chunks), not the row's own residual;
that signature is the most informative thing this comparison can give.
(3) swa_records and indexed_records are excluded; for CED decoder layers
the SWA window records are exactly the chunk-dependent state, so a
descriptive per-record byte equality count (records ordered by swa
position, not physical slots) would add evidence at no risk. (4) A same-grid
cross-family yardstick (retained 041910Z capture, 8192/80927/bf16 family,
against the new 8192/81389 capture) would show how large boot-and-family
differences are, but the script raises on plan mismatch; a
--allow-plan-difference descriptive mode would be needed for that.
Tests (7) cover self-comparison, earliest-observed layer, refusals,
norms, nonfinite, selection sets, indexer presence; no fixture from a real
capture (the .pt files stay on the nodes), acceptable for a descriptive tool.
Prerequisite: the v3 hook hardcodes CHUNK 8192 in the trigger (prompt_tokens
524288 only), in _begin (num_actual_tokens != CHUNK returns), in
_LayerCapture.rows and in the saved meta (chunk_rows, row_index). Minimal
instrumentation-only change: a chunk_rows field in the trigger JSON limited
to {8192, 4096}, used for the _begin match, the non-decoder row count and
the meta; CED window, positions checks, hooks and copies unchanged; tests
for both geometries. The audit must accept meta chunk_rows in {8192, 4096}
and derive the full-row count from it (CHUNK_ROWS constant today), and take
a pinned regime reference instead of requiring the B2 regime (fp8
ratio2.extend family). The driver (user-owned) needs: blocks 81389 with its
boot-log line, acceptance of DS41_PREFILL_THRESHOLD in the container gate,
chunk_rows in the trigger payload, the pinned 045954Z regime instead of the
B2 reference's proposed_capture_blocks, and an audit/compare phase that
mounts two receipt directories. The launcher (user-owned) currently admits
DS41_DECISION_ROW_BLOCKS only as exactly 80927 and the chunking controls
only for the exact e06 image id, so the capture image kind with the 81389
pin and a threshold is a new combination. The capture image shares the
e06 B12X caches and selection namespace, so the 81389 family will be reused
and the regime should equal the pinned snapshot on both boots.
Scope needing approval: a hook change implies a new lock, a new capture
image build on dusty and distribution over the fabric; the launcher
combination above; two capture boots (8192 and 4096, each two unarmed
controls plus one armed replay) and their audits. Numerical paths stay
unchanged; GLM untouched.
Localization value: the comparison can separate three signatures at the
decision row: differences already at layer 0-19 q (the row's own upstream
path: embeddings, Engram, MoE batching by grid), q equal with out different
(attended context differs), and index-selection differences (a discrete
change in what the row reads). A first difference at layer 20 would point
at the CED decoder window path. It cannot by itself say whether the
magnitudes are expected drift or a defect; per-operator conformance stays
a separate check on each operator's own inputs.

## Matched-grid capture support in driver and audit (2026-09-26, local only, no nodes)

User approved the derivative build, the launcher combination (capture kind,
DS41_DECISION_ROW_BLOCKS 81389, DS41_PREFILL_THRESHOLD 8192 or 4096, lock
3eb16ccc) and the two boots. Driver (claude_run_decision_capture.py):
--mode historical (unchanged: 80927, B2 regime, 8192 grid, threshold
refused) or matched with explicit --chunk-rows 8192/4096; profile_for fixes
blocks, grid, threshold and regime source; pinned_regime verifies the
045954Z snapshot sha, four agreeing ranks and the B2 plan keys, and names
the per-plan differences (ratio2.extend fp8 versus bf16); check_container
gates the pin, the threshold (absent historically, equal to the grid when
matched) and rejects DS41_CHUNKING_BLOCKS; preflight also parses every
rank's rendered-profile JSON line (argv override, capacity 8192, threshold,
env) and the head API line (key pattern, no eval) through the chunking
probe's parsers; trigger carries chunk_rows; tokens are
decision-row-m<grid>-<stamp>; receipts decision-row-matched<grid>-capture-;
observed and control identities carry chunk_rows; summary reports mode,
grid, blocks, regime source, regime_equals_reference (gate) and
regime_equals_b2 (report) with the pinned differences; the audit phase ships
regime-reference.json (mode, source, sha, blocks, grid, regime, differences,
scope) and passes --regime-reference only when that record is the pinned
one. Audit (claude_decision_row_audit.py): compare_identities(matched=)
makes equality with the pinned regime the validity requirement, reports the
B2 regime per plan with an explicit "not required in matched mode, not
claimed" note, requires the record's chunk grid to equal the observed one,
and validate() requires each capture's chunk_rows to equal the observed
identity; without --regime-reference the historical B2 requirement stands
unchanged; report carries mode; LIMITS gained the matched-mode item. Tests:
driver 27 (7 new: profiles, tokens/trigger, container gate per mode, boot
profile and API args per mode, pinned regime and record, identities and
argv, CLI), audit 27 (5 new: matched validity, B2 still required without
the flag, pinned mismatch and lacking record, grid agreement across record,
capture and observed including a full 4096 pass, CLI); capture 22, chunking
probe 14, sensitivity 29, comparator 7, contract 4 all pass. Executable
readiness: local_inputs refuses until the build receipt names lock 3eb16ccc
("Capture lock changed since the build"); the historical mode remains
available on the new image at 80927 if ever wanted.
